## [Unreleased]

- Remove the vulnerable transitive Zod 3 chain by targeting AG-UI 1.0.4, using its validator subpath and requiring patched Zod `^4.6.6` for protocol validation. AG-UI serving needs both peers; the renderer needs only the protocol core. Track the remote host's authored Bun declaration so clean CI typechecking matches local checks (plan 224 Task 10).

- Prove the seven-bundle release from packed consumers, including stable and experimental manifest installs, registry smoke, dependency-ordered npm OIDC publishing, and the six-name bootstrap checklist (plan 224 Task 9).

- Plan 224 Task 8: consolidate experimental memory and knowledge into `@arnilo/prism-memory-system` (root and `/core` libraries) and `@arnilo/prism-knowledge` (`/wiki`, `/rag`), retaining exact experimental version pins.

- Consolidate host libraries and the process driver into `@arnilo/prism-hosts` subpaths, with both host-builder and devtools bins and kernel-lockstep versions.

- Consolidate seven tool packages into `@arnilo/prism-tools`, with explicit capability and work-library subpaths. Office, Ajv and XML dependencies become optional peers; missing peers disable Office tools with a status note. Install the npm bundle once and keep full subpath names in imports and manifests.

- Consolidate eighteen context, credentials, integration, policy and store packages into `@arnilo/prism-extensions` subpaths, with optional native keyring, MCP, Postgres and telemetry peers.

- Bundle all eleven providers in `@arnilo/prism-ai`, with separate provider subpaths, no root export, and bundle-aware release discovery and retirement tracking (plan 224 Task 4).

- Package identities accept exported module subpaths. Manifest installation and removal operate on the npm root while entries, capabilities and configuration stay per specifier. Sibling entries share refreshed integrity and version ranges, roots are hashed once per load, and failed upgrades restore the previous installation. Invalid and unexported subpaths fail closed; bundle errors suggest exported subpaths without loading siblings.

- **Publishing moves to OIDC trusted publishing: no npm token in CI (plan 215 Task 38).** The `publish` job keeps its `npm-publish` environment approval and `id-token: write`, drops the `.npmrc` auth step and every `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN` reference, and installs Node 24.10.0 (bundled npm 11.6.2, above the 11.5.1 floor) so `npm publish <tarball> --provenance` exchanges the workflow's OIDC token for a short-lived credential. `scripts/release.mjs` spawns npm instead of `bun publish`, which has no OIDC support, and asks for npm's sigstore provenance on top of the GitHub build attestation. `.github/workflows/npm-deprecate.yml` is deleted: `npm deprecate` is not OIDC-capable, so `scripts/registry-deprecate.mjs` loses `--apply`, its token read and every write path, and prints `--commands` for the owner to run locally with 2FA. `scripts/registry-cleanup.mjs --trusted-publishers` prints the per-package settings the owner must enter on npmjs.com (owner, repository, `release.yml`, `npm-publish`, `npm publish` plus `npm dist-tag`, tokens disallowed) and marks each name npm has never seen, whose first publish has to be manual because a package's settings page does not exist until a version does. Tests: `workflows_reference_no_npm_token`, `publish_job_requires_environment_and_id_token`, `publish_job_meets_trusted_publishing_minimums`, `registry_deprecate_has_no_apply_path`, `deprecate_commands_are_the_owner_run_lines`, `trusted_publishers_render_lists_names_and_settings`; the Bun-only publish assertions in `docs.test.ts`, `release.test.ts` and `tooling-gate.test.mjs` were inverted to the OIDC path.

- **Registry cleanup run on the 0.13.0 scope: 66 of 68 `@arnilo` names are unpublished (plan 215 Task 37).** `scripts/registry-cleanup.mjs` reads the registry anonymously and prints an ordered, policy-checked `npm unpublish` / `npm deprecate` plan for the owner to run under their own 2FA; it never reads a credential, never spawns npm and changes no registry state. `--inventory` records every name in the scope (`GET /-/org/arnilo/package`, which — unlike registry search, which returns nothing for a fully deprecated scope — still lists them) with versions, deprecation state, dist-tags, maintainers, last-week downloads and in-scope dependents, to `docs/_evidence/registry/arnilo-scope-<date>.json`. The scope reads `@arnilo/prism` (64 versions, `0.12.1` latest) and `@arnilo/liveframe`; the ten names 0.13.0 reuses return at that publish after npm's 24-hour block, and `docs/migrate-to-0.13.md` now states that the 0.12 family is deleted rather than deprecated, with each successor. Three defects the run exposed are fixed with tests: `buildPlan` collapses a name whose every published version is retired into one `delete-name` command instead of one per version (314 approvals became 9), the classifier also reads the repo's `scripts/retired-names.json` so a name retired before 0.13 (`prism-office`, retired in 0.8) is not mistaken for an unlisted keeper, and `registry-deprecate.mjs` treats an absent name as `unpublished` instead of a failure in both `--generate` and `--dry-run`. `scripts/deprecations-0.13.json` is deliberately kept whole: it is the successor mapping behind `scripts/retired-names.json` and the provider retirement tests, and deleting the packages already removed the need to deprecate them. `docs/release.md`'s "Registry hygiene" records the outcome.

- **CI and the release pipeline reviewed and deduplicated (plan 215 Task 36).** No workflow names a version any more: `release.yml` triggers on `v*` and `@arnilo/*@*`, `codeql-release`/`publish` gate on `startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/')`, and the publish step branches on the `v` prefix, so a `v0.13.0` tag publishes — the enumeration that stopped at `v0.12.1` had silently skipped the CodeQL job and `publish` (found by Task 1). `release.yml` no longer copies three gates: it calls `security.yml` (CodeQL SAST, audit, SPDX, licenses, secret scan) and `integration-postgres.yml` (store, channels and conformance legs plus the migration drill, on the pgvector image) through `workflow_call`, so each body exists once; the new `security-release` job replaces `codeql-release` plus `supply-chain` in `needs:`. `canary-providers.yml` defaulted its matrix to `openai`, a provider package plan 182 deleted, so every scheduled run failed on a missing path — the default is now the gating T1 set. `sandbox-browser.yml` pins `actions/checkout` v7.0.1 like every other workflow, and `coding-journey.yml`'s job title no longer says 0.2.6. `scripts/workflow-liveness.test.mjs` gains the CI-integrity checks (`ci_jobs_declare_permissions`, `ci_checkout_does_not_persist_credentials`, `release_publish_path_for_v0_13_0`, `release_gate_runs_plan_gates`, `canary_provider_matrix_names_shipped_providers`, `ci_verify_runs_sdk_ready_steps`) and now requires a version comment beside every action SHA. `src/__tests__/docs.test.ts` gains `docs_release_gate_list_matches_scripts`, which compares the `release:gate` chain with `docs/release.md` step by step. Removed three orphaned test files (`scripts/package-truth.test.mjs`, which already failed on the retired `packages/memory/observational` path; `scripts/phase40-release-dry-run.mjs`, 0.0.1 era and npm-based; `scripts/work-sandbox-image.test.mjs`, which resolved the deleted `packages/prism-work`), and wired three live ones nothing reached: `document-ingestion-packed` (now `bun pm pack`, no npm), `provider-login` and `wire-proxy` (all in `bun run test`). `bun scripts/package-truth.mjs --emit-docs` regenerated the stale generated docs blocks, which is what left `truth-current` red.

- **Docs no longer describe APIs the 0.13.0 train removed (plan 215 Task 35).** A standing sweep, `scripts/retired-names.test.mjs` (in `bun run test`), fails when a retired package, subpath, export, error code, `prism` flag, or tool name is used in the live tree. Its list, `scripts/retired-names.json`, is generated from the v0.12.1 → 0.13.0 compat-baseline diff by `bun scripts/retired-names.mjs --generate`. The first run fixed the pages it caught. `docs/node-filesystem-config.md` (`@arnilo/prism/node/config`) and `docs/run-bundle.md` (`snapshotRunBundle()`) move to `docs/history/`. `docs/settings-auth-trust-security.md`, `docs/host-security.md` and `docs/resource-loading.md` drop `@arnilo/prism/node/{settings,trust}`, the settings-provider factories, `loadManifestResource()`, `createSecureAgent()` and `createAgentRunLifecycle()`: `createPathTrustPolicy` is a root export, store conformance is `storeConformance()` from `@arnilo/prism/testing`, and durable resume is `session.resume(decisions)`. `docs/coding-security.md` replaces `createCodingApprovalPolicy` with a host `ExecutionPolicy` imported from `@arnilo/prism-tools/coding`. `docs/operations.md` drops the workflow coordinator, operator handler and saga APIs, and `docs/disaster-recovery.md` drops the 0.2-era enterprise-state stores. Also fixed: the `docs/credentials-and-redaction.md` OAuth providers, the `docs/performance.md` example (0.13 `createAgent` and `mockProvider`), and stale imports in the `@arnilo/prism-extensions/store-sqlite`, `@arnilo/prism-memory-system/core`, `@arnilo/prism-extensions/mcp` and `@arnilo/prism-extensions/approval` READMEs.

- **The provider-agnostic SDK journey (plan 215 Task 32, ex-plan 186 Task 3).** `apps/prism-agent/src/__tests__/live-provider-journey.test.ts` is the tier-2 release check. It runs ten scenarios on a real provider, selected by `PRISM_LIVE_JOURNEY_PROVIDER` (`hyper`, `opencode-go`, `commandcode`, `ollama-cloud`): the stream contract, the tool loop with thinking replay, approval suspend and resume across two processes, gate integrity on a modified resume, rewrite re-gating, ownership isolation, limits, LLM compaction, a remote host with two principals, and abort. Each provider's full run is capped at 36 requests and 80k input tokens. Every event, `inspect()` snapshot, SQLite row, remote frame, child-process output, and error is leak-scanned. Without credentials the suite skips with a reason; its three hermetic tests run in `bun run test`. It passed live on Command Code (`Qwen/Qwen3.7-Flash`) and Ollama Cloud (`gemma4:31b`).
- **Fix: a gate-denied tool call now emits `tool.completed { status: "blocked" }`.** A `tool.call` deny, or an `ask` the host refused, wrote the `hook_denied` result to the transcript but emitted no event, on fresh and resumed runs alike. `docs/events.md` promises one `tool.completed` per settled call, and `RunController.executeTools` now emits it, with `reason` and `error`. Found by the live journey.
- **Fix (`@arnilo/prism-tools/coding`): writes into new subdirectories are no longer hard-denied.** The containment check resolved only the immediate parent of a missing path, so `write notes/x.txt` with no `notes/` was refused as "outside coding roots". It now resolves the nearest existing ancestor, so a symlinked directory above new ones still escapes and any other error still fails closed. Found by the live journey.

- **Long-term working memory, the episode view and episode merge removed (plan 216 Task 7).** `@arnilo/prism-memory-system`'s long-term memory loses `kind: "working"`, labeled blocks and the `block` target of `memory.insert`/`memory.replace`/`memory.forget`, config `ltm.workingMemoryTemplate`/`ltm.limits.maxWorkingMemoryBytes`, the episode view over observational-memory ids, and episode merging (`ltm.lifecycle.merge.episodes`); `@arnilo/prism-memory-system/core` loses the `WorkingMemoryStore` contract and its SQLite, Postgres and markdown drivers. Named migration errors: `working_memory_retired` (a `working` write, or a markdown store that still holds a `working/` note) and `episode_kind_not_available` (the reserved `episode` kind). `docs/migrate-to-0.13.md` lists every removed name. The presets' context blocks are unchanged; their tool schemas changed.

- **Memory-system components and `capabilitiesFor` (plan 216 Task 6).** `@arnilo/prism-memory-system`'s components `stm`, `wm`, `rehearsal`, `ltm` and `sleep` each take an object or `false`; a configured component whose dependency is off fails `createAgent` with a `memory_system_component_missing` message, a component that is off loads and registers nothing, and the new `memory.system` service reports which components run (`setScope`, `endEpisode`, `rehearse`, `sleep` answer `not_available_yet` until their plans). An omitted `rehearsal` is on only when `wm` and `ltm` both are, so a session-tiers-only host no longer runs the dropper. Profiles `ltm`, `session`, `stm` and `full` run in `examples/memory-system.ts`. Kernel: a package definition may add `capabilitiesFor(config)`, which narrows its declared `capabilities` per instance from the validated config; the result must be a subset of `capabilities` (the set a manifest records), and a capability outside it, a non-array or a throw fails the load with `definition_invalid` (`docs/packages.md` → Capabilities). The reference host lists the memory opt-in as `memoryProfileFor` (`ltm` plus the `session` tiers, unchanged behaviour).

- **The fabric becomes long-term memory in `@arnilo/prism-memory-system` (plan 216 Task 5, owner decision 2026-11-08).** `@arnilo/prism-memory-fabric` moves into `packages/memory/system/src/ltm` as the memory system's `ltm` component, with no alias: a manifest or host config naming the old package fails closed with the loader's own `import_failed`/`package_missing`, which names it (`retired-fabric-names.test.ts`). `memoryFabric(config)` is `memorySystem({ ltm: config })` with the same fields, and a host that listed both packages lists one `memorySystem({ ...sessionConfig, ltm })` (listing it twice fails with `duplicate_package`); without `ltm` no long-term memory is set up. `createMemoryFabric` is `createLongTermMemory`. Removed from the root with no alias: `memoryFabric`, `packageName`, and the session helpers the separate fabric imported (type guards, token estimates, recall paging, `recordData`, `serializeSessionEntry`), now imported in-package. The `memory.*` tools, `memory` commands, presets, stores, delivery tiers, lifecycle, scopes, team store, exchange archive, lessons, standing rules and the read-only `memory.ltm` service keep their names and behaviour; the presets' provider requests are byte-identical to plan 210's slimmed product as re-pinned by Task 3. A retired fabric field fails with the same named error at `/ltm/<field>`. Session state the old package wrote is not read by the new one. The memory bench accepts `session`, `session+ltm` and `ltm…` arm names as aliases of the historical ids, which reports keep.

- **Observational memory becomes `@arnilo/prism-memory-system`, the memory system's session tiers (plan 216 Task 4, owner decision 2026-11-08).** `@arnilo/prism-memory-observational` moves to `packages/memory/system` (sources under `src/session`) as `@arnilo/prism-memory-system`, factory `memorySystem()`, with no alias: a manifest or host config naming the old package fails closed with the loader's own `import_failed`/`package_missing`, which names it (`retired-observational-names.test.ts`). The old name was never published. Names move to the memory model's components: config `observer` → `stm` (`messageTokens` → `triggerTokens`), `reflector` → `wm`, `dropper` → `rehearsal` (old keys fail closed naming the successor); commands `om`, `om:model`, `om:status`, `om:view` → `memory:session`, `memory:session:model { stm?, wm? }`, `memory:session:status`, `memory:session:view`; event `om.status` → `memory.session.status`; background task, state, log and ledger record types `om.*` → `memory.session.*`; the rendered heading `# Observational Memory` → `# Session Memory`, the context block title and compaction strategy `observational-memory` → `session-memory`, block metadata `source: "om"` → `"memory.session"`, and the workers' provider session id `om:<id>` → `memory.session:<id>`. A ledger record under an old type fails closed with `MemorySessionLedgerError` (`retired_ledger_type`) and an export hint; no reader is kept. Worker behaviour is unchanged (plans 217–219 redesign it) except plan 210 Task 7.2's three fixes, carried here: each worker section takes `instruction` and `thinkingLevel`, and every worker call carries them plus the session's secret set (the set the kernel hands the compaction slot, kept per session); an unread tail larger than half of `worker.maxMessageBytes` is observed in ordered batches with coverage advanced per recorded batch (a failed batch leaves coverage at the last one), and a single oversized entry is redacted, cut and marked with its id and size; the worker tools answer `{ ok: false, reason }` when nothing was stored. `examples/observational-memory.ts` becomes `examples/session-memory.ts`. Pinned before the move: `OM-before` = revision `37ee1a24` (`docs/_evidence/memory-bench/memory-model/baselines.json`).

- **Live pre-flight check (plan 215 Task 31, ex-plan 186 Task 2).** New `scripts/live-preflight.mjs` (`bun run test:live:preflight`) checks the release acceptance credential rules before a live run spends money. `--suite <id-or-prefix>` (repeatable) selects active rows of `scripts/live-matrix.json`. It prints one row per check: the env file is a regular file with mode `0600` or stricter, outside the repository or ignored by git; every `requires`/`requiresAny` variable is present, reported by name only; no credential value occurs in a tracked file (`git grep` reads the value on stdin, never argv); `pi` and the cache-parity row's pi extensions are installed; the LiteLLM pool has been started. With `--probe` it sends one request per provider through that provider package's own function (`list<Provider>Models`, one single-question `decide` for Jev and hosted Liquid, reachability for Laya and `llama-server`). It exits 0 only when every check passes and 2 otherwise, and scrubs every known credential value from its output. `scripts/live-preflight.test.mjs` (in `bun run test`) covers it offline with injected fs, exec and fetch. `docs/live-testing.md` documents the checks, and the acceptance procedure now preflights `release/journey-` and `release/cache-parity` instead of `release/`, which also selects the manual-only rows. Operator tooling only: nothing in the kernel or packages changed.

- **Release acceptance methodology (plan 215 Task 30, ex-plan 186 Task 1).** `docs/live-testing.md` gains "Release acceptance on live providers". It covers the tiers (T0 hermetic, T1 for the ten gating provider rows, T2 for the SDK journey on Hyper and OpenCode Go, T2c for cache parity against pi, and T3 for the packed post-publish smoke), the entry criteria, the credential rules, model selection, the budgets, the provider-agnostic rule, the exact strict-mode procedure, triage and flake classes (`transport`, `provider-behavior`, `prism`), and what the evidence file must contain. `docs/release.md` links it. `scripts/live.env.example` is rewritten for Bun. The `node --env-file`/`dist/` commands are gone, and so are the variables of the deleted provider packages. Every manifest variable is documented, including the release journey and Laya variables. Every assignment is commented out, so a verbatim copy enables nothing, and comments sit on their own lines because the loader keeps an inline `# …` as part of the value. The new `live_env_example_has_no_values` test in `scripts/live-matrix.test.mjs` checks the template: it must have no active assignment, an empty value for every secret-named variable, no secret-shaped value, `<placeholders>` for any URL credentials, and every env name the manifest reads. `SECRET_SHAPED` is exported from `scripts/live-matrix.mjs` for that test. Operator tooling and docs only: nothing in the kernel or packages changed.

- **The ontology leaves memory for the wiki; the wiki and RAG are knowledge packages (plan 216 Task 3, owner decision 2026-11-08).** `@arnilo/prism-memory-wiki` is renamed `@arnilo/prism-knowledge/wiki` (`packages/knowledge/wiki`, factory `wiki()`) and `@arnilo/prism-memory-rag` is renamed `@arnilo/prism-knowledge/rag` (`packages/knowledge/rag`), with no alias: a manifest or host config naming an old package fails closed with the loader's own `import_failed`/`package_missing`, which names it (`retired-knowledge-names.test.ts`). The wiki now keeps concepts, claims, relations, subject bindings and ingested sources in its own store (markdown under `wiki.root`, default `<workspace>/.prism/wiki`, files 0600 and directories 0700), reads long-term memory only through the new read-only `memory.ltm` service (`createMemoryLtmPort`: `scopes`, `scopeId`, `epoch`, `read`, `scanMetadata`, `activity`, `deletions`; no write member), and runs without memory on its own sources. New wiki tools `wiki.search` (concepts first, claims with their cited ids, a drill-down line) and `wiki.read`, a passive `context.build` pointer (one concept per turn, in play by name or touched path, byte-stable per turn), and config `root`, `store`, `search.maxClaimsRendered`, `bindThreshold`, `pointer`; `requires.services` is gone. Deletion still cascades with no model call: the wiki reads memory's deletion feed after every retraction epoch and reads every cited id back on open (covering retention, eviction and import-replace hard deletes); a claim whose citation is unreadable is never rendered. One wiki root serves one project's memory (`MemoryScopeError` otherwise). The fabric loses the `concept` kind (a stored concept reads as unreadable, reason `retired-concept`), the `memory.fabric` and `memory.fabric.concept-deletion` services, concept-first recall and the concept prior, the directory's concepts line, the concept pointers and cues, concept sharing, write-time binding and the extractor's subject list, and the `ontology.*` config. `memory.recall`'s kinds enum and description no longer name `concept`, so both presets' tool-schema bytes change (the plan 210 golden is re-pinned with the reason; context blocks are unchanged). The memory worker loop, its limits and the trust scan move to `@arnilo/prism-memory-system/core` (`MemoryWorkerError`/`MemoryWorkerLimitError`). `examples/three-tier-memory.ts` becomes `examples/memory-and-wiki.ts`; the reference host's `ontology` option no longer needs `memory`. `memory.recall` p95 on 1,000 notes: 5.1–5.9 ms before, 5.0–5.7 ms after.

- **Compatibility promise and forward-compatibility rules (plan 215 Task 29, ex-plan 185 Task 5).** New `docs/compatibility.md` states what is stable (root and subpath exports, the hook catalog, slot contracts, `PackageAPI`, `HostPort`, event payloads, the manifest format, the session entry schema), what a minor release may change versus what needs a new API level, and the host, package and store rules for staying compatible with later minors. The kernel follows them: every `HostUi` answer is validated before a package or the tool-approval path sees it, so an unknown `status` or a wrongly shaped `answered` value (a truthy string for a `confirm`, for example) becomes `unavailable` instead of an approval. New root export `isKnownAgentEventType(type)`. `SessionEntry.kind` widens to `SessionEntryKind | (string & {})` and `SessionEntry.schemaVersion` to `number`, and `SessionEntryKind` joins `PRODUCER_ONLY_TYPES`. `@arnilo/prism-extensions/store-jsonl` keeps a line of an unknown kind with a valid envelope and a line from a newer `schemaVersion` as opaque entries instead of reporting them as errors, and warns once through the new `logger` option (the package passes its own); a structurally invalid line is still an error. The SQL row codec keeps any numeric `schema_version`. `storeConformance` gains the `unknown-entry-kind` leg (`unknown_entry_kind_round_trips`), which the memory, JSONL, SQLite and Postgres stores pass. The experimental notice and the memory READMEs' graduation sections link the page.
- **Migration-era shapes retired (plan 215 Task 27, ex-plan 185 Task 3).** `resolveCredentialValue` and `CredentialValueSource` are deleted from the kernel; `CredentialRecord` moves to `src/credentials/record.ts` and stays on the root. `@arnilo/prism/provider-kit` exports `SecretSource` (`() => Promise<string | undefined>`), and every low-level provider factory, discovery and quota helper (all eleven provider packages, `createRoutedProvider`, `createOpenAICompatibleProvider`, the SystemOne client and decision provider) takes `apiKey?: SecretSource` — a literal string no longer type-checks; `refSource()` bridges a ref. `resolveProviderApiKey` drops its unused `providerId` parameter. Kilo's `KiloProviderOptions.apiKey` was a literal `string`; it is a `SecretSource` now. `@arnilo/prism-tools/web`'s `WebCredentialSource` is the same lazy shape. Channels: `connectTelegram({ webhookSecret })` takes an `env:`/`keyring:` ref (a literal throws `ChannelTokenLiteralError`), the low-level Telegram adapter and webhook handler take `ChannelSecretSource` for `botToken` and `webhookSecret`, and the channels-local `CredentialValueSource` is deleted. `resolveChannelSecretRef` now resolves through the agent's assigned credentials slot or the kernel `env:` resolver — the old keyring branch read `.secret` instead of `Secret.value`, so `keyring:` channel refs never resolved. `@arnilo/prism-tools/coding`'s `forge.token` is an `x-prism-secret` field (it was pattern-validated only). `InputAssemblyLayout` is `"cache_aware"` only, `toolCallStrictness` drops `"legacy"`, `AgentDefinition.create` and `AgentDefinitionResolutionContext.activateAllCapabilities` are removed, and the 0.1.5 `RunOptions.maxToolRounds` `TypeError` guard is gone (the `limits.maxToolRounds` axis stays). `ProductionPersistenceStore` stays and names its implementers. Docs pages that described pre-0.13 credential helpers (`docs/credentials-and-redaction.md`, `docs/host-security.md`, `docs/settings-auth-trust-security.md`, `docs/provider-kit.md`) now describe the credentials slot and `SecretSource`; the replacement table is in `docs/migrate-to-0.13.md`.
- **Smaller root surface: `@arnilo/prism/net` and `@arnilo/prism/artifacts` (plan 215 Task 26, ex-plan 185 Task 2).** The SSRF-safe network plumbing (`pinnedFetch`, `requestPinned`, `resolvePinnedAddress`, `defaultResolver`, `raceAbort`, `boundResponse`, `isLoopbackAddress`, `isLoopbackHostname`, `normalizeHostname`, `PinnedFetchOptions`) moves from the root to the new `@arnilo/prism/net` subpath; `assertSsrfAllowedUrl` stays on the root. The artifact review records and citation binding (`ArtifactRecord`, `ArtifactBodyStore`, `ArtifactCitation`, `citationBindingDigest`, `HARD_CITATION_EXCERPT_BYTES` and the rest of `artifacts.ts`) move to `@arnilo/prism/artifacts`. `MODEL_FAMILY_TOKENS`, `ModelFamilyTokens`, `resolveModelFamily` and the `EventSubscriber` class leave the public surface; `RunLimitTracker` is exported as a type only (`createRunLimitTracker` builds one). The root entry no longer loads `node:http`/`node:https`: the kernel's default media fetch imports the pinned fetch lazily. Every first-party caller (tools-web, tools-work, tools-openapi, mcp, rag, governance, evals, remote, devtools) imports from the subpaths; `@arnilo/prism-extensions/mcp`'s unreachable `transport.ts` re-exports of the pinned-fetch helpers are deleted. The host-surface gate allows the two subpaths. Migration rows are in `docs/migrate-to-0.13.md`; the subpath is documented in `docs/host-security.md`.

- **Fabric feature removals from the plan 210 audit (Task 7).** `@arnilo/prism-memory-fabric` loses 14 features: `retrieval: "hybrid"` and the `embeddings` service use, the `linker` and `evolution` workers and `passive`, `consolidate.decider`/`consolidate.model` (the `decide_memory` model call), decision points P1, P2 and P6, the `state` wrapper, the `scoped` policy layer (`createScopedMemoryPolicy`, `runScopedMemoryEval`), scope widening and narrowing (`lifecycle.widen`, `widenedTo`, the `widened` and `superseded` report fields, `openScope`), `similar` links, `recall.supersessionHint`, exchange keyword enrichment, and the timeline (resolved `dates`, and `from`/`to`/`order` on `memory.recall`). Eleven were inert and are verified byte-identical on the `conversational` and `coding` presets by `removal-byte-identical.test.ts`; three were measured negative in wave 1 and change recall results (keyword enrichment, the timeline, the `keyed-only` marker). A host config that still sets a removed field fails to load with a named error; `docs/migrate-to-0.13.md` lists each field and its migration. Five owner-held features move to the experimental tier (multi-query recall, `unmatchedTerms`, read-time state grouping, the memory directory, agentic lexical navigation), and `stability.test.ts` gains an `experimental` tier. `scripts/decision-bench.mjs` and its fixture drop the P1, P2 and P6 items. Evidence: `docs/_evidence/memory-bench/210/decisions.md`.

- **Cache-parity gate and first run (plan 215 Task 24, ex-plan 184 Task 4).** `scripts/live-matrix.json` gains the manual `release/cache-parity` row (`bun scripts/cache-parity/live.mjs`; Command Code chat, Responses and Messages plus Ollama Cloud, 120 requests per cell). `parityGate` and `shouldRerun` in `scripts/cache-parity/metrics.mjs` pass a cell only when Prism's median `hitRate` is at least pi's (no tolerance) and no Prism run reports `usage_mismatch`; an `inconclusive` cell is rerun once and fails strict mode; Hyper, OpenCode Go and the paid OpenRouter cell are `deferred`. `scripts/cache-parity/kilo-cli-reference.mjs` runs the Kilo CLI headless on plan 203's fixture. The first live run found three defects, now fixed: the Prism Agent host's config dropped a model object's `compat` and `cache` (Command Code ran `gpt-5.6-luna` on Chat and sent no `cache_control` marker on Messages), `scripts/wire-proxy.mjs` read Command Code Chat usage and OpenAI Responses usage as Anthropic's exclusive shape, and `mapOpenAIChatUsage` / `mapOpenAIResponsesUsage` ignored the vendor cache-write counts (`cacheWriteTokens` is now reported; `inputTokens` was already right). Runner fixes found by the first live run: the pi-extension copy filter skipped every file because the extension lives under a `node_modules` path, a second report on the same day no longer overwrites the first, and a `harness_failed` run records a secret-scanned stderr tail. The first run and its verdict are in `docs/_evidence/cache-parity-0.13.0.md`. Operator tooling: nothing in the kernel or packages changed.
- **Cache-parity runner (plan 215 Task 23, ex-plan 184 Task 3).** `scripts/cache-parity/run.mjs --provider <commandcode|ollama-cloud> --model <id>` runs one fixed coding task (`fixture/`, `task.md`) three times per harness through the wire proxy, once with the reference host's one-shot coding worker and once with pi headless, and reports each harness's median `hitRate` and range, the first-turn-excluded rate, and a per-cell verdict (`pass`, `behind`, `inconclusive`, `usage_mismatch`, `budget_exceeded`) to `docs/_evidence/cache-parity/`. Prism's `turn.completed` usage is cross-checked against the proxy per request; the request budget is 120 per cell; the system prompt is salted per run; `~/.pi/agent` is signature-checked around each run. `scripts/wire-proxy.mjs` gains `--max-requests` (HTTP 429 without contacting the upstream past the ceiling). The reference host's `runCommand` and `createCodingWorker` gain a `limits` dep (agent-wide `RunLimits`, because a run-scoped limit can only narrow the kernel defaults of 16 turns / 40k input tokens / 120 s) so the runner can give the coding worker a 20-turn loop. Operator tooling: nothing in the kernel or packages changed.

- **Wire-level prefix stability (plan 215 Task 22, ex-plan 184 Task 2).** `runPrefixStabilityConformance` gains `encode`, `segments` and `turns`: with an encoder it serializes every captured request and asserts that consecutive bodies keep `tools` and `system` equal and every message below the earlier request's last index byte-equal, with only `cache_control` markers allowed to move, naming the segment, the index and the first differing byte. New exports from `@arnilo/prism/testing/prefix-stability-conformance`: `anthropicSegments`, `openAIChatSegments`, `defaultWireSegments`, `assertWireSegmentsStable`, `WireSegments`. `createRoutedProvider` and `createOpenAICompatibleProvider` now return `RoutedProvider`, a `ModelProvider` with `encode(request)` that returns the body `generate` would send (same route selection and codec, no key, no network); `createHyperProvider` and `createOpenCodeGoProvider` return it too. `hyper`, `opencode-go` and the built-in `openai-compatible` provider run the check offline for each route.

- **`@arnilo/prism-extensions/context-files`: `grep`/`glob` on a single file no longer fails the run (plan 215 Task 20).** The nested `AGENTS.md` hook treated every `grep`/`glob` path as a directory and threw `ENOTDIR` on `<file>/AGENTS.md`, which failed the run with a `context.build` hook error. It now uses the file's directory and treats `ENOTDIR` as absent. Regression test `nested_grep_or_glob_on_a_file_uses_its_directory_and_never_fails_the_run`.

- **Host-builder evaluation (plan 215 Task 20, ex-plan 212 Task 8; closed with no measured claim).** `scripts/host-builder-evals/` holds the pre-registered design (`criteria.json`: seven fixture tasks, three arms `none`, `skill` and `skill+mcp`, four claim scorers, trials 3, claim rules at alpha .05), the fixture workspaces and hidden acceptance tests (the builder never sees them), oracle solutions, and `run.mjs`, which runs offline on a scripted oracle (about 15 s, no key) and live on paid Ollama Cloud models (`gpt-oss:120b`, `beam`, `gemma4:31b`) after the owner approves a dry-run cost estimate. Each trial gives the builder a staged install with real copies of the published packages, so its read tools can reach the docs. `run.test.mjs` checks that the criteria are pre-registered, that the seed fails and the oracle passes every task, that the hidden tests never reach the builder's copy, that the arms differ only by the kit, that the builder's shell sees no live key, and the claim rules on synthetic reports. **Outcome: no claim.** On `gemma4:31b` the `skill` arm beat `none` in every complete run (kit-v2, natural loading: acceptance 0.67 against 0.24), but the difference did not pass the pre-registered bar (q 0.125 against alpha .05 at 7 tasks x 3 trials), and the MCP server added nothing over the skill. The first run's transcripts led to kit fixes, published as kit-v2 (see the `@arnilo/prism-host-builder` changelog): member lists in the API index, a principal warning in the skill, and a corrected upgrade map. The package README states no performance claim and links the evidence in `docs/_evidence/host-builder/212/`. `scripts/skill-evals/run.mjs` now exports `liveProvider`.

- **A Prism agent as the host builder: `examples/host-builder-agent.ts` (plan 215 Task 19, ex-plan 212 Task 7).** The example installs the `prism-host-builder` skill into a temp project with `prism-host-builder install --target prism`, then runs an agent with `tools-coding`, `contextFiles` and `@arnilo/prism-extensions/mcp` connected to `prism-host-builder mcp --root <project> --entry src/agent.ts#createInspectionAgent`. A scripted turn loads the skill, calls `prism_api({ symbol: "createAgent" })` and `prism_inspect_host`. It runs on the mock provider with no credential or network and joins the example execution gate. `apps/prism-agent/README.md` and the root `README.md` link the package and the example; the reference host's code is unchanged.

- **Opt-in recall-quality variants and ceiling instruments (plan 210 Task 3.1).** Five tuning fields in `@arnilo/prism-memory-fabric`, all default off, with results, injected text and tool schemas byte-identical when unset. Plan 210 Task 5 measures them once before any default changes:
  - `recall.expansion`: a fixed informal/formal query table (`gf` ↔ `girlfriend`), with no embeddings and no model call;
  - `recall.gather`: a `gather` argument on `memory.recall` that returns the whole matching history oldest first, up to the 32-hit window, at 3,000 snippet tokens instead of 1,200. A gather leaves out other-scope, forgotten and quarantined (flagged) notes;
  - `inject.cardExcerpt: "query"`: pointer cards show the query-centred excerpt over both halves of an exchange instead of the first 120 characters of the user half;
  - `directory.stopWords`: the memory directory drops topics made only of stop words;
  - `recall.supersessionHint: "keyed-only"`: the "superseded by" label is shown only for keyed or `supersedes` groups.

  New harness instruments in `scripts/memory-bench/`: `ceiling.mjs` (recall vs oracle, wrong answers by stage, counts only) and `power.mjs` (minimum detectable paired difference). Each variant has an offline mechanism probe (`mechanisms.json`, `<row>#<variant>`).
- **Memory mechanism fixes from the offline probes (plan 210 Task 2).** `scripts/memory-bench/mechanism-probes/` probes every audit candidate (fires, reaches what the agent receives, correct) and writes `docs/_evidence/memory-bench/210/mechanisms.json`. Fixes it found in `@arnilo/prism-memory-fabric`:
  - The package's `memory.read { around | session }` always answered `not_found`: the package built its own tool set without the session navigator `createMemoryFabric().tools()` has. Both now share one navigator (`navigation.ts`), and the package's `memory.replace { id, pin }` now pins (it ignored `pin`) and finds a note in any active scope.
  - `recall.expansion` (opt-in) reported a hit's coverage over the expanded terms, so a match found only through an alternate fell under `memory.recall`'s no-match floor and the tool abstained. Coverage is now counted over the caller's own terms, the scale the floor already used.
  - `rules.capture: "confirm"` without the exchange archive captured nothing, silently. Setup now leaves a warning status note, and the README states the dependency (both presets turn the archive on).
  - Named inspectability tests for the `om` commands and the `memory` toggle / `memory:status`.
- **The SQLite store reads a session branch in time linear in its depth (plan 210 Task 0.4).** `readBranchPath` and `queryEntries({ leafId })` in `@arnilo/prism-extensions/store-sqlite` walked ancestors through the `(session_id, id)` index, so every step scanned the whole session and a cold branch read was O(depth × session size): ~1.3 s for a 2,000-entry branch. The recursive query now looks each ancestor up by primary key (~6 ms at 2,000), which every resume, checkout and new-process rebuild pays. Entries, order, paging and ownership scoping are unchanged, with no schema change. The Postgres store's plan was checked and already used key lookups, so it is unchanged. `session-turn-overhead` drops its SQLite run caps and runs both stores at 1,000 runs (~12 s each).
- **A session no longer re-reads its branch on every provider turn (plan 210 Task 0.3).** The kernel's session snapshot cache is now extended in place by the session's own appends that the store accepted at the cached tip, with one detached copy of the new entry, instead of being dropped and rebuilt from `readBranchPath`/`list` (which cloned the whole branch). When the cache's `snapshotCacheTtlMs` expires, one `store.get` of the tip revalidates it: the tip must still be stored in this session with the same parent and ownership stamps, or the branch is re-read as before. A compaction entry, an append conflict, checkout, fork and resume still drop the cache, a store without `get` still rebuilds, and `snapshotCacheTtlMs: 0` still reads the store on every snapshot. The missing-usage fallback estimate memoizes each message's share, with the same result. Provider requests are byte-identical (plan 202's cache-stability pin is re-attested). On `session-turn-overhead` (1,000 runs, 2 KB payloads, memory store) store entries read per run fall from ~6,100 to 0, and the run takes ~13 s instead of 87 s. New ceilings in `scripts/budgets.json#sessionTurnOverhead.ceilings`, enforced by `session_turn_overhead_within_frozen_ceilings`. No API or config change.
- **The markdown memory store stops rewriting its whole file map on every write (plan 210 Task 0.2).** `@arnilo/prism-memory-system/core/fabric`'s derived path → entry map is now a snapshot (`.index-files.json`) plus an append-only journal (`.index-files.journal.jsonl`): a single-note `upsert`, `delete`, `invalidate` or `clearInvalidation` appends only the entries it changed, open replays the journal, and reconcile, rebuild, or a journal longer than `max(1024, map size)` ops fold it back into the snapshot. A torn, unparsable or newer-version journal is never trusted; the map is dropped and the open rebuilds from files. Lookups by id use an index kept in step with the map, `log.md` is re-parsed only when it changed since the store's own write, and each `index.md` line is rendered once per entry. `index.md` and `log.md` bytes are unchanged, and OKF export never ships the journal. On the scale bench (paired medians, same session), file-map bytes per single-note upsert fall from ~1.14 KB per stored note to a constant ~729 B, and write p95 falls to 0.46× at 10k notes and 0.39× at 50k. The whole `index.md` rewrite remains the O(n) residual. New gate `markdown_store_write_amplification_within_budget` (`scripts/budgets.json` `markdownStore`). Fixed on the way: `invalidate` and `clearInvalidation` moved note files without persisting the file map, so closing and reopening the store after an archive move logged `removed-externally` and deleted the moved note's index row until the next `rebuild()`. They now journal the move.

- **`prism-host-builder install`: the installer (plan 215 Task 18, ex-plan 212 Task 6).** `prism-host-builder install --target <claude|codex|cursor|agents|prism> [--mcp] [--root <dir>] [--force] [--dry-run]` copies the skill into the project's skill directory with a version-and-hash stamp, merges the MCP server into `.mcp.json` or `.cursor/mcp.json`, and prints the snippet for Codex's TOML config and for a Prism builder agent. Same-version re-installs are no-ops; a locally edited copy or a different `prism` server entry is `ERR_PRISM_HOST_BUILDER_INSTALL_CONFLICT` unless `--force`; paths are realpath-checked inside `--root` (`ERR_PRISM_HOST_BUILDER_INSTALL_ROOT`); a malformed config is refused (`ERR_PRISM_HOST_BUILDER_INSTALL_CONFIG`). No export added, no user-global write, no package manager run.
- **`prism-host-builder mcp`: checking the host's own code (plan 215 Task 17, ex-plan 212 Task 5).** `prism-host-builder mcp` takes `--entry <file>#<export>`, `--package <file>#<export>` (both repeatable), `--trust-project`, `--check-timeout <seconds>` (default 30, at most 120) and `--pass-env <NAME>`, and serves `prism_inspect_host`, `prism_check_manifest` and `prism_check_package`. Each runs the host's code in one bounded `bun` child process (own process group, killed on timeout and after exit, environment limited to `PATH`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `PRISM_HOME`, the `--pass-env` names and `PRISM_HOST_BUILDER_CHECK=1`, 1 MiB output cap, `--pass-env` values masked). Targets come only from the start flags and must resolve inside `--root` (`ERR_PRISM_HOST_BUILDER_ENTRY`); a factory that runs a session or makes an outbound request fails with `ERR_PRISM_HOST_BUILDER_CHECK_MODEL_CALL`. No kernel change and no baseline diff.
- **`prism-host-builder mcp`: lookup tools, docs resources and prompts over stdio (plan 215 Task 16, ex-plan 212 Task 4).** `@arnilo/prism-host-builder` gains an MCP server (`prism-host-builder mcp [--root <dir>] [--allow-version-mismatch]`, served through `servePrismMcpStdio`) with four read-only tools: `prism_api` (exact symbol or lexical query: signature, package, specifier, docs, examples), `prism_errors` (meaning, owner, declaration, docs and the skill's fix), `prism_examples` (ranked, or one indexed example's source) and `prism_docs` (heading-anchored excerpts from the docs installed under `--root`). Installed docs and READMEs are `prism-docs://<package>/<path>` resources, refused when a symlink leaves `node_modules`; `build-host`, `add-capability` and `upgrade-host` are prompts. A tool call against an `@arnilo/prism` that is not the indexed version (or is not installed) returns `ERR_PRISM_HOST_BUILDER_VERSION` naming both versions and the fix. Results are capped at 64 KiB and marked when cut. No network, no model, no embeddings, no write.
- **`prism-host-builder` skill and its drift gate (plan 215 Task 15, ex-plan 212 Task 3).** `@arnilo/prism-host-builder` ships `skills/prism-host-builder/SKILL.md` and seven references (`choosing`, `layout`, `recipes`, `pitfalls`, `security`, `verification`, `upgrading`) that point a coding agent at the installed docs, indexes and CI-tested examples instead of its memory of 0.12. `src/__tests__/host-builder-kit.test.ts` (`host_builder_kit_matches_surface`, wired into `release:gate`) typechecks every fenced `ts` block, resolves every `node_modules/@arnilo/*` path against the packed file list, every example against `data/examples.json` and every error code against `data/errors.json`, and fails on a retired name or a secret-shaped literal; `scripts/package-skills-conformance.mjs` gains a `packages/hosts/host-builder` fixture (the package registers nothing; the gate registers the skill under its name). `ModelErrorCode` now documents `model_required`, so `errors.json` carries its meaning.
- **New host library `@arnilo/prism-host-builder`: version-exact reference indexes for coding agents that build hosts (plan 215 Task 14, ex-plan 212 Task 2).** `packages/hosts/host-builder` ships `data/api.json` (every public name per package and `exports` subpath: kind, signature, one-line summary, the docs that mention it, the examples that import it), `data/errors.json` (the `ERR_PRISM_*` codes and the short codes of the `*ErrorCode` unions: owner, declaration, class, meaning, sample message), `data/examples.json` and the example sources, with a `prism-host-builder` bin (`info`, `--help`; an unknown command exits 2) and `loadHostBuilderIndex` / `readExampleSource`. `bun scripts/host-builder-index.mjs` generates them from the release gate's declaration model (new exports on `scripts/release-gates.mjs`: `declarationDir`, `extractEntrySurface`; `parseDeclarationFile` gains additive fields, so the compat baselines do not change) and `--check` fails on stale data; the freshness test, a secret-shape scan of the copied examples and a tarball size budget (`budgets.json` `hostBuilder`) gate it. `docs/history/**` and the `migrate-to-0.5` … `0.11` pages are left out of the data. The package is lockstep with the kernel; the skill (Task 15), MCP server (Task 17) and installer (Task 18) follow. New baseline `scripts/compat-baseline/arnilo__prism-host-builder.txt`; `version-literal-gate.test.mjs` now expects 52 manifests.

- **Package skills example and the reference host's parity agent (plan 215 Task 12).** `examples/package-skills.ts` runs on the mock provider: an agent with `tools-web` and `tools-coding` prints `inspect().skills` (`package`, `requiresTools`), then a run with `tools: { only: ["read"] }` drops `web-research` from the catalog and `load_skill("web-research")` returns `skill_unavailable`. It joins the example execution gate. `templates/README.md` gains a "Host skills" note. The reference host's catalogs are coding 0 entries, research 6 entries (873 B), orchestrator 1 entry (204 B); the cache-parity fixtures of Tasks 22–23 use the orchestrator.

- **Skills are listed only when their tools are available (plan 215 Task 6, ex-plan 211 Task 2).** `Skill` gains `requiresTools`, read from frontmatter `metadata.prism-requires-tools` (space-separated; a malformed value fails the parse, which fails a package's setup and makes `context-files` skip a discovered skill with a note). On each request the kernel lists, renders, re-attaches and loads a skill only when every required name is in the tools that request advertises, after `disable.tools`, `tools.only`, per-turn clamps and package-tool `when` predicates. `load_skill` on an unavailable skill returns a `skill_unavailable` tool error naming the missing tools and the run continues; an unavailable loaded skill is named in the "Previously loaded skills" line, not re-attached, and stays loaded. A hidden skill takes no catalog slot, and `agent.skills`, `inspect().skills` and `PackageAPI.skills.list()` still list it with `requiresTools`. Unlike `toolNames`, a missing tool never fails the run. New root exports: `SkillUnavailableError`, `SKILL_UNAVAILABLE_ERROR_CODE`, `missingSkillTools`. The computer-use skill moves from `toolNames` to `requiresTools`, so a host that exposes only some desktop tools no longer fails the run, it just does not get the skill. A skill with no requirement renders the same bytes as before.

- **Experimental status in the docs, the claims and the roadmap (plan 215 Task 4).** The five memory READMEs open with one notice (template in `docs/release.md`, "Experimental packages"): install with `@experimental`, outside the stability promise, may change or be removed until graduation. `docs/index.md` marks the five entries "(experimental)", `README.md` and `PRODUCT.md` say memory is experimental and opt-in, `docs/migrate-to-0.13.md` gains a "Memory packages" note, `templates/` marks `memory-rag` experimental, and `roadmap.md` records the experimental line in its header, the version targets and Phase 11. The fabric, observational and core `## Compatibility` sections are retitled as the contract each package keeps from graduation. New docs tests `experimental_packages_carry_notice` and `stable_packages_carry_no_experimental_notice`. Memory claims are unchanged.

- **Stable packages and the reference host's defaults run without the experimental memory line (plan 215 Task 3).** New gate `src/__tests__/experimental-boundary.test.ts` (`stable_packages_never_depend_on_experimental`) fails when a publishable non-experimental package names an experimental package in `dependencies`, `optionalDependencies` or `peerDependencies`, or ships `src/` that imports one (static, type-only, re-export, bare or literal dynamic); it reads the set from `scripts/independent-packages.json`, and `devDependencies` used by `__tests__` stay allowed. `@arnilo/prism-extensions/observability` declares the RAG telemetry types it consumes locally (`src/rag-telemetry.ts`), so `createRagTelemetry` no longer imports `@arnilo/prism-memory-rag`; `rag_telemetry_types_stay_compatible` fails typecheck if memory-rag's seam drifts. The compat baseline for observability is unchanged. `@arnilo/prism-extensions/context-engineering` gains the `file:../../memory/core` devDependency its test already used. In the private Prism Agent host the coding and research workers load `memory-observational` only with `memory` set (beside the fabric), and the research worker loads `memory-rag` only with `embeddings` set: with both unset, no default agent loads an experimental package, and `memory-compaction` alone owns compaction. This changes the default prefix, which the cache-parity tasks measure.

- **The experimental release line (plan 215 Task 2).** `scripts/independent-packages.json` gains an `experimental` list that names the five memory packages, `@arnilo/prism-memory-{core,observational,fabric,wiki,rag}`, now at `0.13.0-experimental.0`. They pin each other exactly, in `dependencies` and `peerDependencies`. `bun scripts/release.mjs` publishes them with `--tag experimental` and never `latest`, a lockstep `v*` cut neither validates nor publishes them, and a listed package without a prerelease version (`experimental_version_mismatch`) or an unlisted one with it (`stable_version_prerelease`) fails before any registry request. `release.mjs` orders versions by semver §11, lets a prerelease target satisfy only an exact range, and `bump --package <name> --type prerelease` moves `0.13.0-experimental.0` to `.1` and rewrites the exact pins that name it. The compat gate keeps their baselines but reports a removal or change in an `experimental` section instead of failing. The package-truth, version-literal and packaging gates accept the prerelease for exactly that set. See `docs/release.md` "Experimental packages".

- **Recall no longer hides its best match behind a conversation-wide group (plan 208 Task 5).** `memory.recall` grouped exchanges whose keywords overlapped and showed each group newest first. Every turn of a two-person chat carries both speakers' names, so a whole conversation could collapse into one group and push the one note matching every query term past the page the model sees; on the LoCoMo development screen that was the cause of 20 of the 34 wrong answers. Grouping is now anchored on each group's best match and ignores terms nearly every hit shares. The benchmark's BEAM suites also answer under BEAM's own published answer prompt.

- **Notes written without keywords now reach the wiki (plan 208 Task 5).** The fabric's `memory.fabric` port gives the wiki the archive path's deterministic keywords (`exchangeKeywords`) for any evidence note stored without keywords, so a plain `remember` call or an import that passes none can join an unbound keyword cluster and reach the accumulation trigger. Before, such notes were invisible to it: on the MemoryAgentBench-CR development screen the ontology tier made zero passes. View only; stored notes, recall weighting and the directory are unchanged. The benchmark also gives MemoryAgentBench-CR its own answer rule (newest serial wins, answer from the pool), which the generic instruction left out.

- **The three-tier stack end to end: example, reference-host option and positioning docs (plan 207 Task 9).** `examples/three-tier-memory.ts` runs observational memory (T0), the fabric (T1) and the wiki (T2) deterministically on scripted `mock/main`, `mock/observer` and `mock/curator` models: three sessions write fact notes, `wiki:consolidate` creates a `payments` concept whose claims each cite a note, a later note binds to the subject at write time with no model call, `memory.recall` returns the concept first and `memory.read` drills down to a cited note and a verbatim exchange, and `memory.forget` on a cited note removes its claim. It finishes in under a second and is part of the example execution gate. The reference host's research and coding workers gain an opt-in `ontology: true` (and `ontologyModel`) that adds `@arnilo/prism-memory-wiki` beside the fabric; it is off by default and is an error without `memory`. `PRODUCT.md` gains a "Memory" subsection (standing rules, the record, the ontology), and the README, docs index and the three memory packages' READMEs name their tier. No quality claim is made: the held-out verdict belongs to plan 210.

- **Human curation, claim lint and the team ontology (plan 207 Task 7).** A concept is a markdown file a person can edit, and the fabric now reviews that edit before it indexes it, with no model call: a body that breaks the item grammar is rejected (the last valid version stays in use, the file is left as written, and `log.md` gets an `invalid-concept` line); a claim the person changed or added is marked `[c1 human]` and is read-only to the curator and to every non-human writer (`claim_human`); `sources`, `title` and the stale marks follow the body; and the trust scan still runs, so an edit that trips it quarantines the concept as a candidate until a clean edit lifts it. `@arnilo/prism-memory-system/core`'s markdown store gains the `reviewEdit` hook that makes this possible, and the same review applies to the checked-in team store. `@arnilo/prism-memory-wiki` gains `wiki:lint [scope]`, a read-only, model-free report over unresolved or stale citations, alias collisions (these two make it `ok: false`), uncited human claims, candidate-only claims, missing relation targets, over-cap concepts, team concepts whose citations are provenance only, human-edited concepts and ontology gaps. The wiki pass now protects a person's claims one by one instead of a whole concept, and never plans or writes the `team` scope, which the fabric port exposes read-only.

- **The concept pass: background consolidation from cited evidence into the ontology (plan 207 Task 6).** `@arnilo/prism-memory-wiki` now maintains the concept notes. A background pass, checked when a session opens and every `consolidate.minIntervalTurns` (default 5) turns inside `session.background("wiki.consolidate")` under one lease per scope, makes one model call per due subject or cluster and applies closed, cited, incremental edits through the fabric's `memory.fabric` port: `create_subject`, `bind`, `add_claim`, `update_claim`, `retire_claim`, `merge_claims`, `add_relation`, `add_open_question`, `note_contradiction`, and no operation that rewrites a concept. Work becomes due by read-time consolidation (notes read and used together in `consolidate.minCoReads` turns, the primary trigger), accumulation (`consolidate.minNotes` uncited notes) and repair (a cascade-marked concept, which `consolidate.triggers` cannot remove). The answer is validated whole (every id shown, every claim cited, ≤ 280 characters, a claim cap with grow-and-refine, merges citing the union) and fails closed; one shape or truncation failure gets one repair call; a trust hit drops that edit and counts it; a claim a person wrote is read-only. `wiki:consolidate` runs a pass now, `wiki:status` reports the last pass with its calls and tokens, and each pass that did work emits one `wiki.consolidated` event. The system prompt and the tool schema are constants, so the cached request segments are byte-identical across calls. The fabric port gains the read side this needs (`pending` filters `subject`/`unbound`/`coRead`; note views carry `keywords`, `links`, `coRead`, `generatedBy`, `stale`) and two methods, `settle` and `screen`. Opt-in and unmeasured: the default is not to install the package.

- **`@arnilo/prism-memory-wiki` becomes the ontology tier; the 0.12 compiler is deleted (plan 207 Task 5).** The package now requires the fabric's `memory.fabric` service and fails closed with `missing_service` without it (the fabric provides it only when `semantic` memory is on). It registers `wiki_ingest`, `wiki:ingest` and the new `wiki:status` (concept and dirty-subject counts per scope, no model call), takes `consolidate` and `ingest` config, and rejects unknown fields. Deleted with no alias: the compiler, profiles, manifest, scaffolder, contradiction engine, linter and re-point/retire handlers, the `.wiki/` page store, the `qmd` client, the Context7 hydrator, the tools `wiki_search`/`wiki_read_page`/`wiki_record_insight`, the commands `wiki-init`/`wiki-refresh`/`wiki-lint`/`wiki-ingest`, the two bundled skills, the `wiki-guidance` hook, the `prism-wiki` bin, `createWikiDeletionHandler`, and the config fields `wikiRoot`, `rawRoots`, `profile`, `qmdPath`, `autoDeploySkills`. The fabric's dev dependency on the wiki is gone and the wiki peers on the fabric. The tier stays opt-in and unmeasured; the concept pass, claim lint and source ingest into the fabric follow in plan 207 Tasks 6–8. Migration: `docs/migrate-to-0.13.md` ("Wiki → ontology tier").

- **Observational-memory role measured and benchmark worker failures corrected (plan 202 Task 16).** OM remains the T0 tier: its ledger feeds the ordinary fabric `promote` pass, and its in-session compression is unchanged. Seven offline lifecycle seeds measured 0.375 → 0.393 task success for fabric alone with promotion and 0.732 → 0.732 for OM+fabric; promotion wrote 0.125/0.25 notes per session and none of those candidates was later recalled and cited, so the reference host keeps promotion off. A six-run one-question live smoke on two free models completed `om`, `om+fabric` and `fabric-x-tools` with zero worker errors. Live worker cost on one LongMemEval-S question was 233,494 observer input and 1,702 reflector input tokens per 10k source-message tokens. The benchmark now preserves a worker failure's cause (`MemoryBenchWorkerError` retained only a generic message), treats token-ceiling as a budget stop, and records other background worker failures instead of aborting the arm. The `memory/observational-live` matrix row is active with an opt-in OpenCode Go observer/reflector smoke. Evidence: `docs/_evidence/memory-bench/202/observational/`.

- **Lifetime archive growth tier (plan 202 Task 15).** Exchange notes now have a separate `lifecycle.exchangeCapacity.hotTokens` tier (default 200,000) instead of spending the fact/preference/procedure budget. Over the tier, maintenance demotes the lowest-value unpinned exchanges with the audit verb `demoted`; text stays in the searchable archive, so no exchange is deleted. A 12-month LongMemEval-M stream (1,427 exchanges) ran `fabric.maintain()` each month and passed the pre-registered month-12/month-1 criterion both on the shipped archive path and a 20k-token stress arm. The 100k-exchange store envelope measured **1.06 ms p95 recall on SQLite** and **4.15 ms on Postgres**; refreshed markdown evidence keeps the mixed-workload envelope at 10k notes and records 50k as a read-heavy stress point (`docs/_evidence/memory-bench/202/growth.md`, `exchange-scale.md`, `docs/_evidence/memory-markdown-scale-0.13.0.md`). SQLite now caches a scope's fallback generation and reads indexed terms for coverage, removing a full JSON scan from every lexical query.

- **Memory safety suite and default gate (plan 202 Task 14).** New `scripts/memory-bench/safety/run.mjs` runs the offline safety gate: poisoning through the real exchange archive with and without legitimate memories, two-user/two-project leakage with the team store on and off, deletion through markdown files/index/derived facts/links/core/OKF export, the full LongMemEval-S/LoCoMo false-refusal audit, PII retention, and a host-redactor path. The gate pre-registers its thresholds in `safety/criteria.json` and writes `docs/_evidence/memory-bench/202/safety.{json,md}` with counts only, zero model calls. The exchange archive now normalizes benign invisible code points (emoji selectors/ZWJ, formatting zero-width and bidi marks, mojibake soft hyphens) without weakening the adversarial shapes, and detects `curl -s https://`-style flag forms; LongMemEval-S false refusals fall from 2.271 to **0.575 per 1,000**, needed-session loss from 3 to **0**, and adversarial recall rises to **34/34**. `memory.forget` also strips dangling links from surviving notes. The conversational preset remains opt-in.

- **Decision-model gates for memory operations (plan 202 Task 12).** `memoryFabric({ decisions: { model: "liquid-local/d1-3B", points?: [...] } })` opts into per-point decision calls through `prism.models.decisions()`: P1 durable capture, P2 supersession classification, P3 passive relevance, P4 memory-needed, P5 sufficiency, P6 rule applicability, and P7 instruction-like text. Each point has a calibrated threshold and abstain band; inside the band, or on `DecisionsError`/timeout, the incumbent path decides. P3/P4 share one batched request; only already-scoped text is sent; P7 can only add refusals; no gate deletes or archives. Absent, behavior is unchanged. Adds a 700-item memory-gate fixture and `decision-bench.mjs --memory`; the live per-point qualification screen remains with Plan 210 Task 9.

- **Coding memory: standing decisions and the `coding` preset (plan 202 Task 11).** A new additive `decision` note kind carries `rationale` and `decisionStatus` (`proposed`/`active`/`superseded`) with the existing scope, path-glob, supersession and source-entry fields. In `memoryFabric({ preset: "coding" })`, a user turn with a lasting-rule cue is captured lexically as **proposed** (no model call; tool output is never an input); `memory.insert { kind: "decision" }` is also proposed. One batched `host.ui` confirmation or two sessions of use activate it; an unavailable UI leaves it proposed. Active path decisions use the existing path cue (≤ 3/turn, 300 tokens, with `why: memory.read <id>`), active project decisions take the core slice (`core.kinds`), and a superseded decision stays readable but is never delivered. The preset makes no write-path generative call and needs no embeddings service. `prism-agent.json` accepts `memory: "coding"`. `CODING_PRESET_REVISION` and `MemoryDecisionStatus` are exported. Measured lifecycle-suite target remains with Plan 210 Task 9.

- **Memory timeline: resolved dates and time-scoped recall (plan 202 Task 6).** Archived exchanges now resolve relative date expressions ("yesterday", "last Tuesday", "two weeks ago", "in March") against the turn's `tRef` with a deterministic English resolver, storing an additive `dates: [{ text, resolved, precision }]` note field with no model call. `memory.recall` accepts optional `from` and `to` (inclusive ISO dates; a date covers that whole UTC day) and `order: "relevance" | "time"`; time order renders `tRef` and resolved dates, and passive delivery shows the resolved dates inline. A note matches a window by any resolved event date, else its `tRef` or write time. A time window scans up to 5,000 archived metadata rows instead of stopping at the relevance top-K. Unknown expressions stay unresolved; month and weekday names are English. Older readers preserve the field, and existing archives are not rewritten.

- **Ollama Cloud provider (plan 206 Task 2).** New `@arnilo/prism-ai/ollama-cloud` package ships native `/api/chat` NDJSON by default and OpenAI-compatible chat as an opt-in route. Both report cached-token usage; native additionally streams thinking and preserves measured tool-call ids. Includes bounded parsing, ref-only credentials, capability/context-window discovery, and an explicit `ollamaCloudBalance` helper. No local Ollama or Anthropic Messages route. Offline conformance uses synthetic recordings based on Task 1 wire measurements; live generation verification follows in Task 5.

- **Live credentials are read only from outside the repository.** `scripts/live-matrix.mjs`,
  `scripts/cache-parity/provider-probe.mjs` and `scripts/decision-bench.mjs` now default to
  `~/.config/prism/live.env` (or `PRISM_LIVE_ENV_FILE`) and no longer fall back to `scripts/live.env`, so a
  credential file in the checkout is never read; the matrix report records the path used, and the template
  installs it with `install -m 600 … ~/.config/prism/live.env`. `.gitignore` and the secret-scan carve-out are
  unchanged as defence in depth.

- **Shared live harness per-route hooks (plan 206 Task 5 follow-up).** `LiveRouteConfig` in `@arnilo/prism/testing`
  gains two optional fields, threaded through `runProviderLiveScenarios` and `executeProviderLiveScenarios`:
  `structuredPrompt` replaces the shared schema-less prompt for the structured-output scenario, and `errorModel`
  names an unknown model so the error scenario provokes a real 404 when the service accepts an empty `messages`
  array. `liveScenarioStructuredOutput` takes `prompt` and `liveScenarioErrorResponse` takes `errorModel` directly.
  This removes the last per-provider scenario skip: Ollama Cloud now runs all six shared scenarios on both routes
  (13 passed, 0 skipped). Pinned by `live_scenarios_use_per_route_structured_prompt_and_error_model`; documented in
  `docs/testing.md` and amended into plan 183. Additive fields, no exported name or signature removed.

- **Memory bench on the LiteLLM pool (plan 214 Task 5b).** `scripts/memory-bench/run.mjs` gains a `litellm` provider entry (`PRISM_LIVE_LITELLM_BASE_URL`, default `http://127.0.0.1:4000`, key `LITELLM_API_KEY`), so `--provider litellm --model prism-free/<group>` runs an arm through the local free pool; `--free-only` authorizes such a model only when its group starts with `prism-free/` and the catalog prices input and output at exactly `0`, so group names are never authorization and an unknown price is still refused. The wire proxy's usage line now carries four allow-listed gateway fields — `callId`, `modelId`, `modelGroup`, `responseCost` (`x-litellm-model-api-base` and key labels are never copied) — and reports gain `servedDeployments: { [modelId]: count }` plus `servedCallIds`, which becomes `{ count, path }` pointing at a sidecar JSONL past 1,000 ids. Four fail-closed guards, all in the new pure `scripts/memory-bench/served-model.mjs` and all reporting `MemoryBenchServedModel`: a `--free-only` arm that was charged anything aborts where it stands and keeps its checkpoint, reading the stream usage chunk's `cost` because a stream has no total cost header; a paired comparison is refused when the two arms' served-deployment sets differ (Stage A's `x`/`x-tools` pair and each Task 5 control/candidate cell); a scored arm on `prism-free/smoke`, or on any group whose catalog reports more than one deployment, is refused; and held-out/confirmatory subsets refuse a group that served more than one deployment. The free-route pacer keeps its 20/minute default, with the pool's per-group ceiling passed explicitly and never raised automatically. Verified live against the pinned pool: a pool-key call through the bench's own wire proxy recorded `callId`, `modelId` and `modelGroup` for the deployment that answered.

- **LiteLLM free test pool tooling (plan 214 Task 5).** New `scripts/litellm/` operator tooling — never published, never part of a run — that starts a loopback-only LiteLLM proxy over free tiers from a committed descriptor (`free-pool.mjs`): `prism-free/smoke` for wire and integration tests, a same-model `prism-free/gpt-oss-120b` group across Groq, NVIDIA NIM and Cloudflare Workers AI, single-host pinned groups for scored arms, and a `prism-free/priced` deployment with a fabricated price so a paid call accrues spend. `render.mjs` emits the YAML (structured emitter, every string quoted) into a `0600` scratch dir outside the repo, keeps only deployments whose env keys exist, drops emptied groups and their fallbacks, and pins both images by digest; credentials travel as `os.environ/NAME` and compose `${NAME}`, never as values. `up.mjs` waits for liveness and mints (or rotates) one key limited to the rendered groups with `max_budget` `$0.01`/30d under a `user_id` with role `internal_user`, writing it only with `--write-key` after asserting the live env file is `0600`; `down.mjs --purge` discards the volume, keys and spend logs. Verified live on the pinned image: zero-cost chat, a priced call accruing spend, 401/403 refusals, and `/spend/logs/v2` scoped to the key's own user with foreign call ids returning nothing. The pool's hosts are Groq, OpenRouter (free variants), Cloudflare Workers AI and Google AI Studio, each key-gated (Mistral was evaluated and dropped: its Free mode no longer issues API keys without a paid plan); deployments whose quota is per-day (OpenRouter, Gemini) or whose price is fabricated (the `prism-free/priced` tripwire) opt out of background health checks, which otherwise spend 288 calls a day on them. `docs/free-models.md` is a generated, dated list of the free models each configured provider's own catalogue offers (with the criterion used and the pool group that reaches it), refreshed by `scripts/litellm/free-models.mjs` without calling a model; a provider with no key is shown as skipped rather than omitted, and a test fails if the page drops a pool deployment. Nine new tests (renderer + list) plus the existing reconcile suite; `docs/live-testing.md` gains a pool section.

- **Observability trace-context propagation and usage allowlist (plan 214 Task 4).** `@arnilo/prism-extensions/observability` gains two opt-in, provider-neutral config fields. `propagateTraceContext` (default `false`) registers one `model.request` transform that injects the current span — the latest chat attempt for the run, else the run span — as W3C `traceparent`/`tracestate` through the API's `propagation.inject`, writing only those two headers, never overwriting a caller's `traceparent`, never touching `authorization`, and falling back to the span's own ids when no propagator is registered or it throws. `usageMetadata` (default `[]`, ≤32 paths of ≤128 chars) names dotted paths into `Usage.metadata` that are exported as `prism.usage.<path>` scalars on the chat span under `content: "metadata"`, subject to `fieldPolicy` (`drop`/`hash`); non-scalar and absent paths add nothing, `content: "none"` still adds nothing, and the schema refuses an overlong path outright. Neither option adds a request or a model call. `rawOpenTelemetrySpan`, `injectTraceContext`, `activeTraceSpan` and `formatTraceparent` are exported as the seam for hosts that build their own propagation.

- **LiteLLM spend-log reconciliation (plan 214 Task 3).** `@arnilo/prism-ai/litellm` exports `litellmSpendLogs({ baseUrl, apiKey, callIds, since, until, waitMs, fetch, signal })`, which reads the proxy's own spend log for calls Prism already made — up to 20 ids as indexed `GET /spend/logs?request_id=…` reads, otherwise up to 50 pages of `GET /spend/logs/v2` over a date range filtered locally — polls until the batch writer has flushed (measured first visibility 11.8 s, so the default wait is 24 s capped at 60 s) and then reports `{ rows, missing }`. Rows are rebuilt field by field (`callId`, `model`, `modelGroup`, `modelId?`, `spend`, `promptTokens`, `completionTokens`, `cacheReadTokens?`, `startTime`, `endTime`, `status?`), so prompts, completions, key hashes and `api_base` never reach evidence, and every row is filtered to a requested id even when the key can read other tenants. No admin key is needed or accepted: a virtual key reads its own rows on the pinned proxy. A cache hit is stored under `"{request_id}_cache_hit{epoch}"`, so `callId` prefers `litellm_call_id` and otherwise strips that suffix. `scripts/litellm/reconcile.mjs` compares a bench report or usage log against this and exits non-zero on a delta over `--tolerance` (default `1e-6` USD) or a missing row, taking the key from `LITELLM_API_KEY` only so it never reaches argv.

- **LiteLLM Proxy provider (plan 214 Task 2).** `@arnilo/prism-ai/litellm` registers `litellm` with one Chat Completions route to a host-run proxy (default `http://127.0.0.1:4000`, `http:` accepted only on a loopback host, otherwise `ERR_LITELLM_INSECURE_BASE_URL`). Every call mints its own `x-litellm-call-id` — a caller cannot set or override it — and the package reads the proxy's `x-litellm-*` response headers through a package-local `fetch` wrapper, merging scalars into `usage.metadata.litellm` (`callId`, `modelId`, `modelGroup`, `version`, origin-only `apiBase`, `responseCost`) on the `usage` and `done` events; error messages name the call id and the proxy's key fingerprints are redacted. `Usage.cost` comes from `x-litellm-response-cost` when present (including `0`), otherwise the catalog prices the turn — streams carry no total cost header, measured. Discovery groups `/model/info` by `model_name` with conservative claims (smallest window, capability only when every deployment supports it, largest per-token cost, deployment count and provider list) and falls back to `/v1/models` ids when the key may not read details. Prompt caching is marker-free by default with a per-model `anthropic-blocks` opt-in and `prompt_cache_key`/`prompt_cache_retention` stripped; cache fields map from both OpenAI and Anthropic shapes. Errors map the measured surface: 401 `auth`, unknown group `permanent`, 422 `budget_exceeded` not retried, 429 `rate_limited` with `Retry-After`, 5xx `transient`, and the 413 `context window limit` body normalized so plan 194 classifies `context_overflow` with no kernel change. Offline conformance replays 12 recordings (stream text, tool loop, reasoning, vision, three-turn cache, six refusals); the live suite passes 4/4 against the pinned proxy with two by-design skips, and it skips with a reason when no proxy answers. Root counts move to 50 publishable / 10 provider packages.

- **Kilo AI Gateway provider (plan 213 Task 2).** `@arnilo/prism-ai/kilo` registers `kilo` with three wires selected by the catalog's `opencode.ai_sdk_provider` (`anthropic` → `/messages`, `openai` → `/responses`, absent/`openrouter` → `/chat/completions`), an explicit `compat.route` winning. The key resolves from `env:KILO_API_KEY` per request with a `keyring:kilo` device-login fallback; with neither, the package still registers and notes that only `:free` models are reachable (both anonymous shapes answered 200). Every wire carries the provider-owned `X-KILOCODE-TASKID` affinity; `organizationId` adds `X-KILOCODE-ORGANIZATIONID` and switches discovery to `/organizations/{id}/models`, `mode` adds `x-kilocode-mode` only on `kilo-auto/*`, and `dataCollection` adds `provider.data_collection`. Usage maps USD `cost`, cache reads/writes per wire, `is_byok` and the served model/provider into `usage.metadata.kilo`. The error table maps 401/402/403/413/429/502/503 and the recorded overflow bodies (`context_overflow` on Chat and Messages). Catalog discovery maps windows, modalities, tools/reasoning/structured output, per-million costs, `isFree`, `mayTrainOnYourPrompts` and alias targets. The adaptive Messages thinking hook moved to `@arnilo/prism/provider-kit` as `anthropicAdaptiveThinking` with Command Code's public name kept. Wire fixtures replay three routes offline; the live suite defaults to the three paid exemplars and accepts free ids. The Responses route sends no `prompt_cache_key`, matching the CLI; the offline cache-levers test compares each wire against recorded CLI 7.8.18 requests and pins a byte-stable prefix across turns. Root counts move to 49 publishable / 9 provider packages.

- **Cross-process retraction revalidation (plan 202 Task 4.14).** A pinned fabric prefix now revalidates the core notes it shows with one bounded `memory.get` per scope on every build. A note forgotten, revoked or retired by another process ends the epoch on the next request, while other processes' additive writes keep the prefix byte-stable. A failed revalidation drops the core block for that request instead of resending it; an abort still propagates. Measured p95 0.31 ms per pinned build on a 10k-note SQLite store.
- **Extractor prefix caching (plan 202 Task 4.13).** Opt-in fabric promotion shares a constant cache key per system-prompt variant across sessions, preserving `sessionId` attribution. The tool schema no longer embeds per-pass ids or varies with existing notes and segments; the parser still rejects foreign ids and episodes without shown segments. Savings cover only the stable system-plus-schema prefix, with no extra model call.

- **Memory prefix epochs (plan 202 Task 4.12).** Directory and core blocks stay byte-identical across additive writes until session start/restore, compaction, retraction or explicit refresh. Forget, revoke, correction, retention, imported tombstones and retirement metadata invalidate the next request. Working memory is re-read into the tail after the transcript, ahead of semantic memory. Closed sessions release their snapshots.

- Fix Command Code Beam tool follow-ups by preserving plain reasoning on Chat; fix memory-benchmark free-route accounting and readiness enforcement. Remote agents explicitly reject unsupported reload requests.

- **Hot reload for a live agent (backlog: hot-reload / dynamic update).** `agent.reload()` re-reads `AgentConfig.skills.dirs`, re-runs every loaded package's `setup` on a fresh hook bus and slot registry, and re-compiles `AgentConfig.hooks` in place. Open sessions keep their conversation and see the new skills and hooks on their next run; `agent.tools`/`skills`/`commands`/`inspect()` reflect the swap. Assembly is atomic: a failing package setup or slot/model conflict rejects the reload and leaves the committed runtime untouched.
