This makes editing `.agents/skills` or a host-compiled `hooks.json` during a session a normal operation instead of a rebuild.

- **Command Code returns as a first-party provider on the Provider API (plan 205 Task 2).** `@arnilo/prism-ai/commandcode` registers `commandcode` and picks one of three wires per model: `claude-*` → `/messages` (`x-api-key` + bearer, adaptive thinking mapped to `output_config.effort`, `cache_control` at `system_prompt`/`last_message`), a catalog entry advertising `/responses` → `/responses` (`store: false`, 64-char `prompt_cache_key`, `x-client-request-id` affinity, local `max_output_tokens` floor of 16), everything else → `/chat/completions` (`max_tokens`, `reasoning_details` replay, no cache fields because the wire has no lever — measured `cached_tokens` 0 → 448 → 0 across identical turns). `zdr: true` adds the provider-owned `x-cmd-zdr: 1` header; a model without a ZDR upstream answers `422 cmd_zdr_no_providers` and the classifier calls it permanent. Discovery maps `supported_endpoints` and `context_length`, capability claims come only from the families verified live (a model published later stays text-only rather than guessed), and the over-context body classifies `context_overflow` on all wires that return it. Wire fixtures replay all three routes offline (22 package tests, 99.4% line coverage); the live suite passes 15/15 on the three exemplars with the warm-prefix probe deliberately omitted until plan 184 measures the levers. Root counts move to 48 publishable / 8 provider packages. The Messages route also sends the reference's adaptive shape (`thinking: {type: "adaptive", display: "summarized"}`, verified live) and `cache_control` markers on the system prompt and last message; `cache-levers.test.ts` proves the cache levers and the frozen prefix against pi's own recorded requests for all three wires (plan 205 Task 3).

- **Tool names are encoded on the wire (provider compatibility fix).** OpenAI and Anthropic accept only `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,64}` as a function name, while the memory fabric registers dotted names (`memory.recall`, `memory.read`). Every Chat Completions, Responses and Messages request through `createRoutedProvider` now builds one `ToolNameMap` (`createToolNameMap`) and uses it twice: the tool list and replayed `tool_call` blocks are serialized through `wireToolName` (`memory.recall` → `memory_recall`), including a call replayed for a tool this request no longer offers (narrowed tools), and the stream is decoded back through `productToolName`, so the registry still sees the product name. The map is a bijection within a request — a tool already named `memory_recall` keeps it and the dotted one takes `memory_recall_2` — so two tools can never collapse onto one wire name. Routes that call a codec directly must pass the same `toolNames` to the body and the stream; the routed provider does it for them. Found by the plan 202 baseline: OpenCode Go and OpenRouter free endpoints rejected the fabric arm with `400 invalid_value tools0.function.name`.

- **Memory claims carry their limits, and the register is machine-readable (plan 188.11 Task 9).** The fabric README's Conversational preset section now states only what the held-out v3 evidence supports: 76/100 against 69/100 whole history at 26% of the input tokens (`mimo-v2.5`, temperature 0, the same model judged), paired +0.07 (95% CI -0.03..0.17) inside the 3-point non-inferiority margin; passive injection alone scores below whole history, so the read tools are required; 1.5 tool calls per question and 2% run-limit. The harness's substitution label is not used in preset prose, including the 188.10 Changelog entry and `docs/_evidence/memory-bench-0.13.0.md`. A new `### Known limitations` subsection lists twelve limits with evidence paths (one benchmark family; no held-out preference or abstention; judge equals answerer; lexical top-32 ceiling at 11/30; temporal reasoning 12/20; 78% irrelevant injection; unmeasured capacity growth; F3 foreign-context exposure 13/24; F4 extraction reliability; F7 lifecycle safety power; F9 `om+fabric`; the Task 8 trust-scan rates), mirrored by `docs/_evidence/memory-bench/188.11/limitations.json` with the owning plan 202 task per entry. The observational README states that OM has no completed benchmark arm and no live quality evidence in 0.13.0. Docs and evidence only; no product change.

- **The reference host adopts the conversational preset behind an option (plan 188.11 Task 7).** `prism-agent.json` gains `memory: "conversational"` (off by default), validated with a closed value set and applied to the research and coding workers; the markdown store lives under `<dirname(storePath)>/memory` and the fabric jails it to that `tools.root`. `memory.recall`/`memory.read` join the research worker's prompt-free allow-list; writes still ask. The new offline scenario `apps/prism-agent/src/scenarios/conversational-memory.ts` states a fact in one session, recalls the archived exchange in the next, forgets it, and recalls nothing in a third, on the mock provider. The research worker now carries the host's `ownership` scope (`{ tenantId: "prism-agent", userId: "prism-agent" }` unless a host passes one), matching the coding worker. `scripts/live-matrix.json` adds the planned `memory/fabric-conversational-live` row (≤ 6 requests / ≤ 20k input tokens) and re-points `memory/observational-live` from the deleted OpenAI provider to OpenCode Go. Plan 186 Task 3 gains scenario 11, memory across sessions, with its budget raised to 36 requests / 80k input tokens.

- **The memory compatibility promise is written and enforced (plan 188.11 Task 6).** Each memory package README's `## Compatibility` section now states, at API level 1, what is stable (export names and signatures; the fabric tool names and effects; the fabric and observational command names; the `contract` config fields; the note-file rule; the preset names and contracts; the OM ledger record names), what a later minor version may change (the `tuning` config values, ranking and scoring, injection budgets, delivery format, prompt and tool-description text, and a preset's contents inside its contract), and what needs a new name or an explicit opt-in (a new stored kind, a new write-path model call, a new required service, a change to what a default stores). Every fabric and observational config field carries an `x-prism-stability` annotation (`contract` or `tuning`), and a new test (`packages/memory/fabric/src/__tests__/stability.test.ts`) compares every field against the README table, so a field without a tier or a table that drifts fails. The kernel accepts the annotation as documentation only and validation is unchanged. The stale "provisional until plan 188.6" markers are gone: those values are documented as tuning defaults.

- **The memory export surface is reduced and frozen (plan 188.11 Task 5).** The fabric, core and observational root barrels now export what a host, a store author or an embedder author needs, plus a documented *shared* tier that only the sibling memory packages use. Removed from the barrels: prompt constants (`EPISODE_MERGE_SYSTEM`, `EPISODE_MERGE_TOOL`), tuning constants (`DEFAULT_*`, `MAX_*`, `MIN_*`, `HARD_*` that only mirror config defaults), scoring and eviction helpers (`evictionOrder`, `shouldArchive`, `isActiveNote`, `capacityUsage`, `duplicateGroups`), the lifecycle, directory and core-block builders, the observational workers, settings, flush, commands and compactor, and core's cursor codecs, lineage parsing, directory scan and `validateIdentifier`. 80 fabric, 69 core and 81 observational value names left their barrels, with the 28 + 2 + 37 types that only they named. Nothing was documented as public; the names were reachable only because the barrels re-exported everything. `src/__tests__/root-exports.test.ts` in each package freezes the remaining set, so adding a name is a deliberate edit. The three compat baselines were regenerated in the same change.

- **Forward-compatible memory notes (plan 188.11 Task 4).** A fabric note whose `fabric.v` is above 1 (or whose fabric object this version refuses) is *unreadable*: indexed, counted, excluded from recall, injection, directory, core and `memory.read`, and never rewritten, renamed, moved, archived or evicted. `memory:export`/`memory:import` carry its file byte for byte, `import --mode replace` does not delete it, `memory.replace` refuses it with reason `unreadable`, and `memory.forget` by id still deletes it. `memory:reindex` and `memory:status` report `unreadable` and `unreadableReasons`. A `v: 1` note keeps unknown `fabric.*` keys and unknown top-level frontmatter keys through a recall touch, an edit, maintenance and import (before, the first touch stripped them). The `VectorStore` and `Embedder` contracts are documented as frozen at API level 1 and pinned by a type-level test.

- **Conversational memory preset (plan 188.11 Task 3).** `memoryFabric({ preset: "conversational", tools: { root } })` expands, before any other resolution, to the configuration that passed the 188.10 held-out acceptance: `exchanges: {}`, `inject: { query: "task", kinds: ["exchange"] }`, `semantic: true`, `promote: false`. An explicit top-level field replaces the preset's value whole (no deep merge) and emits one status note naming the override; an unknown preset name fails with `MemoryValidationError`. `memory:status` reports `preset` and `presetRevision` (`CONVERSATIONAL_PRESET_REVISION`, exported), and setup writes the same pair to a status note. The README's "Recommended conversational profile" is now "Conversational preset" and states the contract: verbatim exchanges, no write-path generative model call, no embeddings service, and a new preset name or opt-in field for any later write-path model call or stored kind. The memory-bench `fabric-x` and `fabric-x-tools` arms are expressed as the preset, with a test that they resolve to the 188.10 arm. The preset is opt-in; defaults are unchanged.

- **Reference-host evaluation suite and release gate (plan 201 Task 6).** `apps/prism-agent` gains `@arnilo/prism-evals`, a 12-item dataset (`apps/prism-agent/evals/dataset.ts`, `dev` and `heldout` splits, tags `coding`/`research`/`approval`) covering approved coding edits, denied writes, cited research answers, and `web_fetch` prompt-injection items built from `createInjectionDataset`, plus `apps/prism-agent/src/evals/suite.ts` and `apps/prism-agent/evals/baseline.json` (`eval:baseline` script). `apps/prism-agent/src/__tests__/evals.test.ts` gates the reference-host suite on `requireInvariants`, `minimumPassHatK` (`k: 4`), dataset content-hash parity, and `maximumRegression` against the stored baseline record, while `scripts/live-matrix.json` adds the manual `release/eval-gate` row (`apps/prism-agent/evals/live.test.ts`) for `heldout` evaluation on Hyper under a 2M input-token ceiling.

- **Stratified, weighted online sampling (plan 201 Task 5).** `scoreRunLive`, `scoreRun`, and `runExperiment` replace `sampleRate` with `sampling: { rate?, strata?, rates?, always? }` (plus per-run `seed` on `ScoreRunOptions`). `strata(result)` runs once per evaluation with no extra model calls; `always(result)` forces inclusion probability `1`; probability `0` records nothing; and sampled runs stamp `metadata.sampling = { stratum, probability }`. `@arnilo/prism-evals` exports `estimateOnline(records, { scorerId, seed?, resamples?, confidence? })` for Horvitz–Thompson (Hájek ratio) weighted means, Kish effective sample size, per-stratum breakdowns, and `bootstrapInterval` confidence intervals, failing closed with `ERR_PRISM_EVAL_STATS` on unweighted records.

- Evaluation scoring, experiments, and scenarios accept a type-aligned telemetry sink; each redacted record emits content-free metadata, and sink failures preserve records (live scoring reports them through `onError`).

- SQLite and Postgres now persist bounded, ownership-scoped evaluations and experiment summaries via migration `011_evaluations`, with shared conformance and indexed pagination.

- Plan 201 Task 2: move evaluation persistence types to the kernel and memory evaluation storage to
  `@arnilo/prism-extensions/store-codecs`; add optional evaluation/experiment persistence capabilities, bounded
  content-free experiment summaries, exact ownership filtering, and one awaited summary write per experiment.


- **Prompt-injection evaluation (plan 200 Task 6).** Cross tasks with four original attacks and sandboxed tool-result carriers; report benign utility, attacked utility and deterministic attack success with Wilson intervals and per-attack/carrier breakdowns. Experiment factories now receive the current item for isolated per-trial hooks.

- **State grading (plan 200 Task 5).** `createStateMatchScorer` compares redacted final environment snapshots against exact or recursive subset goals, with JSON-pointer ignores, optional invariants, a 1 MiB state cap, and bounded redacted mismatch diffs. Arrays compare by index.

- **Simulated users (plan 200 Task 4).** `createUserSimulator` drives standalone and dataset scenarios through fresh tool-less sessions, with bounded turns/time/output, redacted text-only conversations, stop reasons and model provenance. Simulator failures skip agent scorers; serialized specs require rebinding their host-owned agent before execution.

- **Scenario dataset items (plan 200 Task 3).** Experiments execute scripted multi-turn inputs through the shared scenario loop, with fresh sessions per trial, per-turn or final scoring, item/trial/turn record identity, environment snapshots, and summed usage. Standalone scenario signatures remain available; simulated-user execution follows in Task 4.

- **Dataset hygiene (plan 200 Task 2).** Evals datasets are immutable JSON snapshots with canonical SHA-256 content hashes, validated splits/tags/label provenance, and optional searchable canaries. Experiments filter splits, require explicit heldout access, record hashes, and aggregate statistics by tag; paired comparisons reject differing hashes and support BH-adjusted tag comparisons. Release manifests require a dataset hash and can verify it against a report. Curation stamps human/curated provenance and preserves dataset metadata; rubric hashes now use the shared sorted-key canonical JSON format and must be regenerated.

- **Ranked comparisons (plan 199 Task 6).** `runComparison` supports up to 16 trials, counts candidate errors as forfeit losses, and reports per-scorer Bradley–Terry strengths with seeded item-bootstrap intervals and shared ranks for overlapping intervals. The pure `bradleyTerry` helper is exported. Unidentifiable or unconverged fits and degenerate bootstrap evidence are reported as unavailable rather than confident ranks.

- **Judge calibration (plan 199 Task 5).** `calibrateJudge` measures agreement with human scores using Cohen’s κ, optional quadratic κ, nominal Krippendorff’s α, Spearman ρ, a confusion matrix and a seeded item-bootstrap interval. `labeledItemsFromFeedback` loads owner-scoped, bounded traces without passing human feedback to judges. Evaluation thresholds can require calibration agreement and sample size.

- **Judge panels (plan 199 Task 4).** `createJudgePanel` combines every member’s verdict by majority, mean or median, preserves per-judge results, and fails when fewer than half succeed. Known duplicate models are rejected by default through scorer `judgeModel` declarations. Experiment scorer statistics report Fleiss’ κ from complete panel label rows; missing or undefined agreement remains absent. Panels retain member input requirements and redaction, and add no model calls.

- **Position-swapped pairwise judges (plan 199 Task 3).** `createPairwiseJudge` judges both answer orders through the shared bounded, tool-less rubric runner. Inconsistent verdicts become ties; records preserve both redacted verdicts and the answer length ratio. `runComparison` forwards prompt redaction, retains scorer metadata, and reports successful position-consistency rates per scorer. Hosts supply both candidate model identities or explicitly allow self-judging. Two calls per pair without retries, up to four with the default attempts.

- **Rubric judges (plan 199 Task 2).** `@arnilo/prism-evals` exports `createRubricJudge`: versioned labeled rubrics, optional references, bounded tool-less agent sessions, strict JSON validation and retries, model identity checks, and redacted prompt/reasoning metadata. Hosts supply the candidate model identity (or explicitly allow self-judging). Judge error codes survive failed scoring records.

- **Confidence-aware evaluation gates (plan 198 Task 7).** `minimumLowerBound` requires scorer confidence bounds; `maximumRegression` rejects significant paired drops using BH-adjusted q-values. `powerAnalysis` sizes paired binary datasets with explicit discordance assumptions, alongside the existing inverse `minimumDetectableEffect`.

- **Paired experiment comparisons (plan 198 Task 6).** `compareExperiments` pairs shared item ids, averages repeated scores per item, and reports candidate-minus-baseline differences, paired bootstrap intervals, McNemar/sign-flip p-values, and BH-adjusted significance. Invalid pairings fail closed. The memory benchmark now shares the package McNemar implementation.

- **Evaluation reports include usage, complete billing, and run latency (plan 198 Task 5).** Each item retains every trial's usage, status, run duration, and cost-completeness evidence. `aggregate.cost` includes observed token totals, per-item means, and trial p50/p95 latency; billing is omitted when any trial or provider attempt is unpriced, estimated, missing, or in a different currency. The new `paretoFrontier` selects quality/cost tradeoffs without extra provider calls.

- **Experiment reliability (plan 198 Task 4).** Scorers accept `passThreshold` (default 1). Reports add item-weighted `passRate` and, for repeated trials, `passAtK`, `passHatK`, and per-k contributing/excluded item counts in `passKCounts`. Missing scored trials are excluded from each k; missing estimates fail the new `minimumPassHatK` gate. Reliability aggregation computes all k in linear time without new dependencies.

- **Per-scorer experiment statistics (plan 198 Task 3).** Reports now carry item-weighted `scorers[id]` means, clustered SEs, seeded bootstrap/Wilson intervals, and an unweighted `macroMean`. Replaced the pooled report fields without aliases; thresholds use `minimumMacroMean` and per-scorer means. Devtools treats overlapping scorer intervals as a quality tie; fixtures and examples use the new shape.

- **Evaluation statistics utilities (plan 198 Task 2).** `@arnilo/prism-evals` exports Wilson intervals, CR1 clustered standard errors, seeded percentile bootstrap, exact McNemar, paired sign-flip tests, Benjamini–Hochberg q-values, pass@k and pass^k, and paired-proportion power/MDE. Inputs fail with `ERR_PRISM_EVAL_STATS`; resampling is capped at `HARD_STATS_RESAMPLES = 100_000`. Known-answer tests and a bootstrap runtime check cover the new API; experiment aggregation and gates follow in later tasks.

- **Scorers and judges get a redaction seam (plan 197 follow-up).** A scorer's input is raw — `result`, `item`, and an untimelined run carry the text exactly as recorded — while `resolveRedactor` only ever applied to stored records. `scoreRun` now resolves `redactor`/`secrets` once per run and passes the result to every scorer as `ScorerInput.redactor`, so a scorer that sends text out of the process (a judge prompt, a simulator turn, a state diff in metadata) has one place to redact rather than each one inventing its own. `createModelJudge` uses it: the target and the dataset item are redacted before the byte-limit check and before `options.judge` is called, so a secret in the run text or in `item.input` can no longer reach a judge prompt. `RunScenarioOptions` gains `secrets` (it had `redactor` only) and its per-turn scoring passes both to the seam, so scenario scorers see the same redactor as `runExperiment`. With neither option configured the seam is `undefined` — the host declared no secrets, so there is nothing to redact. Plans 199/200 consume the seam for the rubric/pairwise judges, the judge panel, the user simulator, and the state-match diff.

- **`@arnilo/prism-extensions/observability` is now an optional peer, loaded on demand (plan 197 Task 9).** Timeline projection is the only reason evals needs the observability package, so it moved from an undeclared dev-only reach to `peerDependencies` + `peerDependenciesMeta.optional` and is loaded through one helper (`src/timeline.ts` `loadTimeline()`) with a literal dynamic import behind a type-only `typeof import(...)`. A text-only host installs neither the peer nor its OpenTelemetry glue, and nothing loads it unless a timeline is requested (`timeline: "metadata" | "redacted_io"`); asking for one without it installed throws `EvalError` `ERR_PRISM_EVAL_TIMELINE_UNAVAILABLE` naming the package and keeping the import failure as `cause`. The three computed specifiers (`const pkg = "@arnilo/prism-extensions/observability"`) in `score.ts`, `experiment.ts`, and `scenarios.ts` are gone, so bundlers and the host-surface gate can see the edge. Also: the package README was rewritten to the host-library page structure (install, scorer table with `requires`/`invariant`, experiments, scenarios, failure injection, curation, thresholds, errors, cost) and no longer documents the non-existent `createComparisonJudge`; the empty plan-096 section heading in `trajectory.ts` is removed.

- **Curation is idempotent, and non-numeric dataset versions increment cleanly (plan 197 Task 8).** `datasetFromRuns` now skips a run whose item id is already in `input.dataset` with reason `already in dataset` instead of appending a second item with the same id; the rest of the batch is still curated, and when every candidate skips the input dataset object and `version` come back unchanged. The check is one `Set` of existing ids, consulted before any trace read/redaction/mapping; a custom `toItem` id that collides is caught after mapping and skipped the same way, so a single pass cannot add a duplicate either. Datasets stay immutable versions: an existing item is never replaced — a changed label is a new item id or a new version, the host's choice. `nextVersion` no longer produces `v1-2-2`: numeric versions increment as before (`1` → `2`), and a non-numeric label keeps its prefix and counts in a trailing `.N` (`v1` → `v1.1` → `v1.2`, `v1.9` → `v1.10`, `2026-10-26` → `2026-10-26.1`).

- **Scenarios: bounded turns fail closed, `aborted` status, assertion codes, and per-turn scoring (plan 197 Task 7).** `runScenario` no longer slices `turns` to `maxTurns`: more turns than `maxTurns` (default 8) or a `maxTurns` outside `[1, 32]` throws `EvalError` `ERR_PRISM_EVAL_SCENARIO_BOUNDS` before the agent is built, so a scenario that silently dropped half its turns can no longer pass. `ScenarioResult.status` gains `"aborted"` (host signal, or a final run the agent reported as aborted); previously both reported `"succeeded"`. A throwing `assertReply` now stops the scenario with `status: "failed"` and `error.code` `ERR_PRISM_EVAL_SCENARIO_ASSERTION` naming the turn, instead of surfacing the bare assertion error with no code. New `scoreTurns: "last" | "each"` (default `"last"`, unchanged behavior): `"each"` scores after every turn on that turn's result and timeline slice — the folder's cumulative snapshot minus the steps already scored, no second subscription — and stamps `metadata.turnIndex` on every record, so a refusal at turn 2 that recovers at turn 4 stays visible. `"each"` multiplies scorer calls (and judge cost) by the turn count, as the README states. Hosts that relied on truncation must either extend `maxTurns` or split the scenario.

- **Experiments: per-trial seeded streams, abort keeps finished work, and `hooks`/`slots` reach the factory (plan 197 Task 6).** With `seed`, each `(itemId, trialIndex)` now samples from `mulberry32(hash32(seed, itemId, trialIndex))` instead of one shared stream, so scoring decisions are identical at concurrency 1 and 8 — previously the sample pattern depended on the order concurrent workers drew from the single generator. An explicit `random` still shares one stream and is documented as order-dependent. Aborting (or a fatal worker error) no longer discards the report: `mapPool` gained an optional settle-all mode (one helper, no second pool) that fills the slots which never ran instead of rejecting, so finished items keep their `result` and evaluations, unstarted items carry an `error` naming the abort, `status` is `aborted` (signal) or `failed` (fatal), and every aggregate metric covers only the finished items (`aggregate.itemCount` still reports the pinned dataset size). `RunExperimentOptions` gains `hooks` and `slots`, passed to the agent factory as `runScenario` does; combining them with an `Agent` instance throws `EvalError` `ERR_PRISM_EVAL_EXPERIMENT` instead of silently ignoring them. Note: a `tool.call` hook deny stops the call before the tool pipeline, so no timeline step is emitted for it — the denial is visible as a `hook_denied` tool_result in the transcript.

- **Invariants are a static scorer declaration, and `requireInvariants` is enforced (plan 197 Task 5).** `Scorer.invariant?: boolean` (also on `defineScorer`) marks a hard gate; `scoreRun` stamps `metadata.invariant: true` on every record of such a scorer, **including a `failed` record from a scorer that threw**, and `runExperiment` reports `invariantsPassed: false` unless every invariant record scored `1`. Previously invariance came from metadata a scorer returned, so a crashing invariant (a timeout, a bug) produced a record with no flag and `invariantsPassed` stayed `true`/`undefined` — the gate opened exactly when the check broke. Built-in hard checks now declare it: `createApprovalBeforeEffectScorer`, `createGuardrailPackScorer`, `createNoLoopScorer`, `createCitationIntegrityScorer`, the eight ERP invariant scorers, `createErrorClassScorer({ deny })` (general any-failure mode stays a quality signal), and `createToolCallMatchScorer({ deny })` / `{ invariant: true }`. A `metadata.invariant` returned from `score` is now stripped, so invariance cannot be decided at runtime (hosts that stamped it should pass `invariant: true` instead — the memory `revokedIdsAbsent`/`caseConclusionsNotProcedures` bodies no longer return it). `assertEvaluationThreshold` honors `requireInvariants: true`: it fails when the report carries no invariant records or when `invariantsPassed !== true`, so a threshold that expects a safety gate cannot pass because the gate was never configured; omitting the option still fails a present-but-failing invariant. `examples/coding-browser-evaluation.ts` now asserts that a red oracle blocks the threshold. No recorded declaration text changes, so the compat baseline is unchanged.

- **Scorers declare the input they need, and every entry point fails closed without it (plan 197 Task 4).** `Scorer.requires?: readonly ("timeline" | "trace" | "environment")[]` (also on `defineScorer`). `scoreRun`, `runExperiment`, and `runScenario` run one `checkRequirements` before any agent run and throw `EvalError` — `ERR_PRISM_EVAL_TIMELINE_REQUIRED`, `_TRACE_REQUIRED`, `_ENVIRONMENT_REQUIRED` — naming the scorer, instead of scoring a missing timeline as an agent failure or, worse, a pass. All built-in trajectory scorers (`createToolCallMatchScorer`, `createStepBudgetScorer`, `createNoLoopScorer`, `createSchemaScorer`, `createErrorClassScorer`, `createApprovalBeforeEffectScorer`, `createGuardrailPackScorer`) declare `["timeline"]`; `createCitationIntegrityScorer` declares `["environment"]`. A scorer called directly without its declared input still returns `0` with `metadata.invariant`. `createErrorClassScorer()` now returns `0` with `reason: "no timeline available for error class verification"` when it has no timeline (it used to return `1`, so an unscored run passed a deny-list invariant). `forEach` now passes the step it is scoring as `ScorerInput.step` — previously the step only produced a `stepId`, so a per-step scorer scored the same run repeatedly (D3) — and a `forEach` run with no matching step records one `skipped` record per scorer with `reason: "no matching steps"` instead of nothing. `examples/` and `README.md` updated; the trajectory scorer options and entry-point signatures are unchanged, so no compat baseline row moves.

- **`approval_before_effect` correlates an approval to the tool call it allowed (plan 197 Task 3).** The scorer used to accept any `hitl` step that was not `denied`/`failed` — including one still `suspended` — and any `succeeded` `guardrail` step as approval for every later tool, so a denied approval passed and an inline approval (which produced no `hitl` step at all) failed. It now requires an `approved` `hitl` step for the same tool call, matched on `metadata.toolCallId` (a tool step's `tool:<callId>` id also counts), with a lower `order`; each approval covers exactly one call. A denied decision reports the denial, a still-suspended one reports that, and an approval that lands after the effect fails. Guardrail steps no longer count. New option `tools: readonly ToolInfo[]` (normally `agent.tools`) narrows the check to calls whose `effects` include `write`, `execute`, or `external`; a tool step the agent does not declare is treated as effectful, `toolName` still narrows to one tool, and with neither option every tool step is checked as before. `matchApproval` keeps working and now receives `hitl` steps only. The default path builds one id map instead of scanning every step per effect. `examples/behavior-evaluation.ts` now demonstrates the real thing: a `tool.call` hook asks before `write_record`, a `HostUi` answers inline, and the scorer reads the resulting `approved` step from `tools: agent.tools`; the devtools comparison fixtures correlate on call ids. New `ToolInfo` entry on the host-surface allow-list.

- **The timeline records approval decisions per tool call (plan 197 Task 2).** `run.suspended` now opens one `hitl` step per pending decision instead of one per suspension: each carries `status: "suspended"`, `metadata.toolCallId`, `approvalId`, and `toolName`/`guardrail` when the kernel reports them, so a batch of gated calls is distinguishable. `decision.resolved` settles each step to `approved` or `denied` with `metadata.via` (`inline` or `resume`), and a decision with no matching step — the inline path, which resolves before the timeline sees a suspension, and a durable resume, which projects into a fresh timeline — adds an already-settled step at the point the decision settles, ahead of the tool's own step. `ExecutionStepStatus` gains `approved` (`denied` already existed), so a scorer can read an approval from the evidence rather than inferring it from whether the tool later ran. A suspension with no pending decisions keeps the previous single step. Decision steps carry ids, outcome, and `via` only, never tool arguments. Four new timeline tests cover resume-deny, inline-allow ordering, a two-call batch, and a `full_io` timeline that still leaks no argument.
- **Scope change: Command Code and Ollama Cloud drop out of 0.13.0 (owner decision 2026-10-25).** Both providers move to the release after 0.13.0, so the 0.13.0 provider set stays the seven packages already in the tree (`hyper`, `laya`, `liquid`, `openai-codex`, `opencode-go`, `openrouter`, `typesafe`) — no package, code, contract, or docs claim changes. `roadmap.md` records the deferral in its header, the Phase 5 provider-packages bullet, and Out of scope; `plans/README.md` pulls plans 205 and 206 off the N3/N4 release-path rows and marks them `planned (post-0.13.0)`; plans 203 and 204 lose their "four provider plans" framing and 204 no longer ties Command Code to the release; plans 205 and 206 keep their research and task breakdown and now state that they run after 0.13.0, do not gate the freeze (185), the strict run (186), or publish (187), and amend the standing parity suite rather than the 0.13.0 gate. `plans/backlog.md` gains one P2 entry per deferred provider. Existing docs were already accurate (they list the seven shipped providers), so nothing else moved.

- **Live acceptance for the Codex provider, and the three defects it found (plan 204 Task 9).** The owner signed in on the real account and `bun scripts/provider-login.mjs` (new, with an offline test) drove every lifecycle step: registration writes the record, the strict live suite passes 6/6, `--expire` proves a refresh rotates and persists across processes, a second keyring namespace imports a grant with its own host ID and one request, `provider-probe` measures **reuse 0.982** against the 0.8 threshold, and a request that bypasses `omitFields` returns `400` with `subscription_sharing_unsupported_capability`, mapped to `permanent` with no token in the message. 45 inference requests against the 40 the task allowed (the account's plan window expired mid-run, so the final confirmation cost 13 extra); `docs/_evidence/openai-codex-live-2026-10-25.md` records each step and `docs/live-testing.md` gains an "OAuth providers" section. Three defects, each with a test: a sign-out deleted the whole record, so the next sign-in registered a second client (logout now keeps the issued client and host ID as a `custom` marker secret with no token); a re-sign-in's callback carries no `client_id`, which the previous rule rejected (the presented client stands in, an echoed one must match, and a registration's callback still must carry one); and a usage limit arrives as a **200** response whose stream nests the failure under `error`, which the Responses codec mis-read as a flattened frame — it now classifies that frame, and `classifyProviderFailure` treats a quota body without a numeric status as `quota`, which is never retried.

- **The Codex provider reaches the release path (plan 204 Task 8).** `roadmap.md` lists `openai-codex` in the Phase 5 provider set and the §2.9 catalog, its credentials bullet documents `credentials:write` and the rotating keyring grant behind `keyring:openai-codex`, and the provider-packages bullet names the Sign in with ChatGPT flow and the public `/v1/responses` route. Plans 185, 186 and 187 carry "Amended 2026-10-25 by plan 204" notes: the shape freeze covers the package and its baseline (it resolves and writes through `PackageAPI.credentials` rather than `SecretSource`, so the `resolveCredentialValue` retirement does not touch it); release acceptance gains a strict `providers/openai-codex` row whose preflight checks the keyring record instead of an environment variable and warns rather than fails when the access token is past its one-hour window, because the record refreshes on use; and the retired-name sweep gains nothing, since the package is a new name — 33 deprecation entries stay. The `@arnilo/prism-provider-openai` deprecation message now points ChatGPT-plan users at `@arnilo/prism-ai/openai-codex` while keeping `@arnilo/prism/provider-kit` as its successor, which `retired-providers.test.mjs` asserts, and `docs/migrate-to-0.13.md`'s family map lists it. Also corrected here: `package-api-freeze.test.ts` was missing `credentials:write` from its frozen capability tuple (plan 204 Task 2 added the capability and documented it but not the tuple), and `release.test.ts` expected 43 packages against the 47 the tree loads — three of those are `liquid`, `openrouter` and `openai-codex`, and one had gone uncounted before plan 204 started, so the count is re-anchored with the reasoning recorded.

- **The OpenAI Codex route caches exactly like pi's Sign in with ChatGPT request, and the probe can now measure it (plan 204 Task 6).** `cache_levers_match_pi_openai_chatgpt` builds installed pi-ai 1.4.0's own request for plan 203's three-turn transcript through a fake `fetch` and compares it to this provider's wire request: `prompt_cache_key` and its 64-character clamp, `session_id` + `x-client-request-id`, `store: false`, `include: ["reasoning.encrypted_content"]`, `reasoning` effort and summary, the tool array, and every `input` item are byte-equal across all three turns, and the instructions, tools, and earlier items never move between turns. The one difference is deliberate and asserted on both sides: pi carries the prompt in a leading `developer` item, this package uses the Responses `instructions` field, with identical text. `cache_key_clamp_matches_pi` proves a 104-character session id clamps to 64 on the body while both affinity headers keep the caller's value. `provider-probe.mjs` gains `--credential keyring:<name>`: the credential slot comes from `@arnilo/prism-extensions/keyring` (never an environment variable, because the record rotates), an empty record fails closed with a named reason, and the provider receives the ref plus the slot so it can persist a rotation — `createOpenAICodexProvider` and `listOpenAICodexModels` accept that pair directly. Measured on the sanctioned route: four turns reused 0.983 of the 8 334-token prefix (`docs/_evidence/cache-probe/openai-codex-gpt-6.1-sol-2026-10-25.json`), against a like-for-like two-turn Codex CLI 0.164.0 run on the same model and account: it reuses 0.842 when the first prompt is bare and only 0.481–0.638 once that prompt carries user content, because it re-reads its own fixed harness block and none of the conversation (`docs/_evidence/cache-probe/codex-cli-turn2-2026-10-25.json`), while Prism reuses 0.981; pi's private route measured 0.969–0.982, so plan 204 Task 7 (WebSocket continuation) does not run. Live re-measurement also fixed a discovery regression: the catalog now carries per-model instruction text and reached 567 675 bytes, over the transport's 64 KiB default, so discovery declares its own bounded 2 MB cap and a runaway body still fails. Plan 184 gains the two `openai-codex` cells.

- **OpenAI models on a ChatGPT plan, through Sign in with ChatGPT (plan 204 Task 5).** `@arnilo/prism-ai/openai-codex` registers the `openai-codex` provider and keeps its grant in a writable `keyring:` ref (default `keyring:openai-codex`): the `oauth` `AuthMethod` runs the fallback-free SIWC flow — `dynamic_agent_client` registration on first sign-in with `agent_name_hint` and `ext:agent_host_id`, PKCE S256 with `state`/`nonce`, a loopback listener raced against a pasted callback URL, ID-token verification against the discovery JWKS, and a scope check for `chatgpt.tokens.use.direct` — and `openAICodexApiKey` refreshes inside a five-minute margin, single-flight per ref, persisting the rotated record before it is used (a terminal refresh code asks for a new sign-in). The package declares `credentials:write`, refuses an `env:` credential ref at load, and a missing record is a `warn` note (`openai-codex: not signed in`) that still registers the provider. One stateless route: `POST /responses` with `store: false`, `include: ["reasoning.encrypted_content"]`, `prompt_cache_key` clamped to 64 characters and mirrored as `session_id`/`x-client-request-id`, the fourteen measured route-refused fields deleted after the body is built, `max_output_tokens` passed through with a local minimum of 16, and no `previous_response_id`. Errors go through `providerHttpError`, so a plan usage limit is `quota` with a message naming ChatGPT Settings → Usage and `subscription_sharing_invalid_user` asks for a new sign-in. Kernel: `isTransientErrorInfo` now declines any `failureClass: "quota"` failure, so a `429` plan limit is never retried while an ordinary `429` rate limit still backs off, and `runProviderLiveScenarios` gained the `responses` route. Catalog: `gpt-6.1-sol` (500 000 context) and `gpt-6-luna` (272 000) static, plus `GET /models` discovery that keeps `visibility: "list"` and maps windows, modalities, and reasoning levels. Wire recordings cover text, tool loop, structured output, image, and a three-turn cache probe against real captures (error bodies marked as hand-built), and the README carries the owner-approved Sign in with ChatGPT scope wording.

- **The Responses wire is a route format, not a provider (plan 204 Task 3).** `createRoutedProvider` accepts `format: "openai-responses"`, backed by the new `src/provider-kit/codecs/openai-responses.ts`. `openAIResponsesBody` puts the first system message on `instructions` and later system content on `developer` items (never a `system` role), maps the rest to `input_text`/`output_text`/`input_image` items plus `function_call` and `function_call_output`, serializes flat `function` tools, lands structured output on `text.format`, and snaps `reasoning.effort` to the model's declared levels. `store: false` forces `include: ["reasoning.encrypted_content"]`, `prompt_cache_key` is `cacheKey ?? sessionId` truncated to 64 characters, `omitFields` deletes keys after construction so a model's `parameters` cannot re-add a refused field, and `previous_response_id` is never sent — the HTTP route is stateless. `max_output_tokens` passes through (the measured refusal list of 14 is exported as `OPENAI_RESPONSES_UNSUPPORTED_FIELDS`; the field is not in it) and a value under 16 throws `ResponsesMaxOutputTokensError` locally. `openAIResponsesEvents` streams text and reasoning-summary deltas, reconstructs one tool call from a `function_call` item and its argument deltas, maps `response.completed` usage (`input_tokens` inclusive, cached tokens to `cacheReadTokens`, reasoning tokens to metadata) and `response.incomplete` reasons to the closed stop taxonomy, and treats `response.failed`/`error` as terminal: the wire `code` and `param` stay in the message and an overflow payload is reported as a 400 so the shared classifier sees `context_overflow` (plan 194's one compact-and-retry, then `ContextOverflowError`). A stream without a terminal event is an error. Encrypted reasoning round-trips through `ThinkingContent.data` as `{ provider, item }` and is replayed only to the provider that produced it. `providerConformance({ recordings })` now reads Responses recordings — the request is rebuilt from `instructions`/`input`, an error frame is detected by its `type`, and a replayed reasoning item without `encrypted_content` fails the run. `docs/provider-kit.md` documents the format; the old `createOpenAIResponsesProvider` stays removed.

- **Packages can persist a rotated credential through the slot (plan 204 Task 2).** `PackageAPI.credentials` gains `store(ref, secret)` and `delete(ref)`, present only for a package that declares the new `credentials:write` capability alongside `credentials`; without it both members are `undefined`, so existing packages are untouched. Writes pass through to the `credentials` slot's own `store`/`delete` (the keyring resolver already implemented them), and the written value joins the agent's secret set immediately, so events, notes, logs, hook errors, and `inspect()` redact it exactly as a resolved value. `env:` refs are never writable: the kernel throws the exported `CredentialRefNotWritableError` with the code the keyring already uses (`credential_ref_unsupported`) rather than adding a second spelling for one refusal, and a slot that declares no `store` fails closed with the exported `CredentialStoreUnavailableError` (`credential_store_unavailable`) before anything is asked of it. Three declared-but-unimplemented credential contracts are retired in the same pass: `OAuthProvider` and `OAuthLoginCallbacks` (no implementer or consumer since the 0.12 provider factories were removed) are deleted from the public contracts, while `OAuthCredentials` stays because `@arnilo/prism-extensions/keyring` converts `Secret` metadata to it; the keyring's OAuth store adapters keep using it. Docs: `docs/packages.md`, `docs/models.md`, `docs/credentials-and-redaction.md`.

- **Provider cache diagnostics and pi lever proof (plan 203 Task 5).** A manual-only `scripts/cache-parity/provider-probe.mjs` replays a stable ≥8K-token fixture through one dynamically imported provider, caps requests/output/time, records reported per-turn cache usage and latency, and keeps absent counts `unmeasurable`. Optional no-session runs write separate secret-scanned evidence. OpenRouter's offline tests compare three turns against pi-ai 1.4.0 source-derived requests (affinity, Anthropic marker placement, reasoning replay, unchanged prefix); the future plan 184 parity row gains a separately paid-gated pinned Anthropic cell. No paid probes were run and no harness parity is claimed.

- **OpenRouter provider restored (plan 203 Task 4).** `@arnilo/prism-ai/openrouter` adds ref-only credentials, model discovery with context limits and cache pricing, reasoning-details replay, session affinity, Anthropic block caching, routing preferences, attribution, served-model/cost usage, and actionable classified errors. Offline fixtures are explicitly synthetic; separately gated paid cache tests default off. Shared live suites can select scenarios, and recorded provider conformance recognizes HTTP-200 terminal error frames.

- **The shared OpenAI Chat codec learns the gateway wire (plan 203 Task 3).** Terminal error frames are now errors: a top-level `error` object or `finish_reason: "error"` yields one `error` with the status `code`, the redacted `metadata.error_type` and message, and the `classifyProviderFailure` class — so a 429 mid-stream is `rate_limited` (`quota` on daily-limit text), 502/503 are transient, 400/402 permanent, and a 400/413/422 overflow frame is `context_overflow`, which drives plan 194's one compact-and-retry before output and `ContextOverflowError` after it. No `done` ever follows an error frame. `reasoning_details` streams merge into logical entries — consecutive `reasoning.text`/`reasoning.summary` deltas join, `reasoning.encrypted` entries stay discrete — and each becomes a `ThinkingContent` whose `data` is the entry JSON, so `reasoningReplay: "reasoning_details"` replays them unchanged and in order; `delta.reasoning` is not repeated when details carry the text. `cacheControl: "anthropic-blocks"` (plus `cacheControlOnTools`) stamps `cache_control` blocks at the same `resolvePromptCachePlan` breakpoints the Anthropic codec uses — pi's `system_prompt`, `tools`, `last_message` defaults, non-empty text parts only, a string `content` becoming a one-part array, `ttl: "1h"` only when the model declares long retention — and emits nothing when the option is absent. `mapUsage(usage, chunk)` on events and routes now receives the decoded SSE chunk, and `assertRecordedStreamConforms` accepts a 200 recording that carries an error frame. Kernel surface is unchanged; the size budget is rebaselined for the added codec.

- **`openrouter` is a live provider name again, and `liquid` reaches the umbrella message (plan 203 Task 2).** The 0.13.0 retirement is reversed: `@arnilo/prism-ai/openrouter` leaves `RETIRED_PROVIDERS`, and its deprecation is scoped to `<0.13.0` as a reused name whose successor is `@arnilo/prism-ai/openrouter@0.13.0` (the `@arnilo/prism-extensions/mcp` message shape), so the 0.13.0 line can never be deprecated. `@arnilo/prism-providers`' successor now names `{hyper,liquid,opencode-go,typesafe,laya}` — `liquid` had been missing since plan 196 — and `deprecation_messages_name_supported_providers` derives that set from `packages/providers/*`, so a new adapter cannot drift out of the message again. `host-completeness-evidence` stops treating `openrouter` as a retired suite name, `docs/migrate-to-0.13.md` lists it among the shipped packages and the 16 that stay removed, roadmap Phase 5 and §2.9 record the 2026-10-24 owner decision, and plans 187 and `plans/README.md` carry the 16/15 split. Docs and gates only: no package, no code, no kernel contract.

- **The reference host compacts from measured budgets and clears tool results in batches (plan 194 Task 7).** Observational memory's absolute `context.compactAfterTokens` gate is replaced by `context.compactAt: { ratio?, maxTokens? }`: the threshold is `min(ratio × the model's input cap, maxTokens)`, default ratio `0.6`; a model that declares no window uses `maxTokens` and fails config validation without it. `ResolveShouldCompactOptions.compactAfterTokens` (the legacy attach-loop gate) is removed. The coding worker now uses `memory-compaction` with `profile: "coding"` and a trigger from [`src/host/context-budgets.ts`](apps/prism-agent/src/host/context-budgets.ts), plus `context-engineering` batch folding and `context.accounted`; the research worker gets the same fold plus `blocks.maxShare: 0.2`. The one measured budget is `opencode-go/mimo-v2.5` at 96,000 tokens (fold batch 9,600); unlisted models use `input_ratio` 0.6, a 4,096-token batch, and one `info` status note. The new `long-session` scenario runs 60 mock turns with a 6 KB tool result each: it stays under the trigger plus one turn, folds in batches, compacts at least once, and recovers from a scripted overflow at turn 40 in under a second. Host config model objects now keep a pinned `limits` block (real refs still resolve from the provider catalog), and the one-shot golden fixture is rebaselined for the extra accounting tokens.

- **A measured context-rot curve puts one number on each release model's effective input budget (plan 194 Task 6).** New harness `scripts/context-rot/`: 30 deterministic self-graded tasks (10 coding edits against a tiny fixture repo, 10 non-lexical needle lookups whose fact is stated at session start, 10 multi-step tool tasks with exact final-state checks) run after 0/16k/32k/64k/96k tokens of unrelated completed tool turns built from a checksum-pinned `expressjs/express` snapshot, alternating file reads and grep output. Every length runs twice — plain and with `context-engineering` fold batch mode (`clearAtLeastTokens: 4096`, `keepLast: 3`, deterministic truncate summaries) — and reports accuracy with Wilson 95% intervals, wire-proxy input tokens, cache-read ratio, and the effective input budget (largest length within 5 accuracy points of the 0-length baseline with overlapping intervals). The shared history prefix is pinned by an explicit cache breakpoint, `--max-input-tokens` (default 50M/model) leaves a partial report, and `--offline` is a sub-30-second mock smoke at lengths 0/4000. Evidence lands under `docs/_evidence/context-rot/` with a row in `docs/_evidence/context-rot-0.13.0.md`; the `release/context-rot` live row is manual-dispatch only. Measured pilot on OpenCode Go `mimo-v2.5` (one task per kind, five lengths, one repeat): 4,110,549 input tokens of which 3,887,872 were cache reads (94.6%), 99 calls, $0.0467 at list price, and an effective input budget of 96,000 tokens with and without fold; 16k without fold was the only miss (2/3). The 30-task × 3-repeat matrix and the Hyper row remain operator runs. Harness, docs and evidence only: no package, protocol, product default or export changed.

- **A request rejected for context size recovers once instead of failing a long run (plan 194 Task 3).** `ProviderFailureClass` gains `"context_overflow"`: `classifyProviderFailure` returns it for a 400/413/422 whose bounded body matches a supported route's overflow string (`context_length_exceeded`, `maximum context length`, `context window exceeds limit`, `prompt is too long` — the first two were recorded live from OpenCode Go's two routes). When a call fails that way before any output started, the session compacts the branch through the `compaction` slot with `trigger: "overflow"` (ignoring `compaction.when` — the provider already decided), re-runs `context.build`/`model.request` on the shrunken branch, and retries the call once inside the same turn; `maxTurns` is not charged twice and the rejected attempt records no projected usage, only the call that ran. A route with no distinguishable body (Hyper and SystemOne routes were unmeasured) still recovers on a 400/413/422 while our estimate exceeds the model's declared window. A second overflow, an overflow after output started, or a failing compaction fails the run with the new exported `ContextOverflowError` (`code: "context_overflow"`), whose message carries the estimated input and the declared window (never prompt text). `compaction.completed` and the stored compaction entry now carry `trigger` (`"manual"`, `"auto"`, or `"overflow"`), and `memory-compaction`'s LLM compactor stamps it too. Docs: `docs/loop.md`, `docs/events.md`, `docs/provider-kit.md`.

- **Skill directories are readable, never writable (plan 190 Task 6).** `coding.targets` now adds `dirname(skill.path)` for every effective skill with an absolute path, but only for `read`, `glob`, `grep`, and `diagnostics`. A skill's `references/` file outside the workspace is readable, approval sees the same roots, and `write`/`edit`/`delete`/`move` keep the coding roots, so a write under a skill directory is still hard-denied; `shell` is unchanged. The list is read lazily after setup and cached until its paths change. `createCodingTargets` gains an optional fourth argument for the skill source; no kernel change.

- **`context-files` discovers coding-agent skills and falls back to `CLAUDE.md`** (plan 190 Task 5). `contextFiles({ root })` now registers `<name>/SKILL.md` from `<root>/.agents/skills` and `<root>/.claude/skills`, and from `<globalRoot>/.agents/skills`, `.claude/skills` and `.codex/skills` when `globalRoot` is set. Roots are de-duplicated by realpath (a `.claude/skills` symlink to `.agents/skills` is scanned once), project roots precede global roots, and the first skill keeps a duplicate name. An explicit `skills: { roots }` replaces the defaults; `skills: { enabled: false }` turns discovery off. Each file is parsed by `parseSkillFile` and registered through `prism.skills.register`; a malformed, oversized, untrusted or conflicting skill produces one `status.note` naming the path and is skipped, so skill discovery never fails package setup. `AGENTS.md` now falls back to the same directory's `CLAUDE.md` when absent, never loading both, and the mtime cache tracks both paths. Discovery costs one `readdir` per root and one read per `SKILL.md` at setup only; `allowed-tools` stays inert metadata. Docs and the `discover-skills` example updated.

- **The skill catalog is one block, and a bad skill cannot fail a run** (plan 190 Task 4). `skillMessages` now renders one system message: `Skills are task-specific instructions. When a task matches a skill below, call load_skill with its name before starting.` followed by one `- <name>: <description>` line per skill; loaded and eager bodies stay separate `Skill <name>:` messages. Descriptions over 512 bytes truncate instead of throwing, only the first 64 skills are listed (ending `(N more skills are installed but not listed.)`), and a catalog over 256 entries no longer throws. A body over the 256 KiB hard cap is rejected at registration with the new `skill_too_large` package error (the message names the skill and cap, never the body), and `createSkillRegistry` rejects it too; contribution discovery skips an oversized `SKILL.md`. `SkillDisclosureError`, `isSkillDisclosureError`, `SKILL_DISCLOSURE_ERROR_CODE`, `HARD_MAX_SKILL_CATALOG_ENTRIES`, and `HARD_MAX_SKILL_DESCRIPTION_BYTES` are removed.
- **`compaction.when` handlers can abstain instead of silencing the host's gate (plan 194 Task 2).** The decision is now a tri-state vote: any `{ compact: true }` compacts, else any `{ compact: false }` vetoes, and only when every registered handler returns `undefined` does `CompactionOptions.thresholdEntries` (the kernel fallback) decide. Installing a package that had nothing to say — observational memory with its per-session toggle off — no longer disabled auto-compaction for the whole agent; the 2026-10-17 repro (0 compactions with OM installed and off, 6 without it) is now the test `om_off_does_not_suppress_host_compaction`. `CompactionWhenDecision.compact` is `boolean`; a `CompactionWhenDecision` of `{ compact: false }` is new public behavior, and a throwing handler still fails closed. OM votes `false` while its toggle is on but below its gate, `memory-compaction` votes `false` below its configured `trigger`, and context-engineering attention abstains below `compactRatio` (an extra reason to compact, never a veto). `docs/hooks.md`, `docs/loop.md`, the three package READMEs and the `thresholdEntries` doc comment state the new rule.

- **The reference host can configure every web knob the package now has (plan 193 Task 9).** `apps/prism-agent`'s `web` block accepts the full backend set (`brave`, `exa`, `firecrawl`, `obscura`, `native`, `searxng`, `tavily`, `kagi`) plus `searxngOrigin`, and three optional blocks passed straight to `@arnilo/prism-tools/web`: `web.fetch` (`backend`/`apiKey`, so the research worker reads pages with the keyless native backend by default), `web.domains` (the host's own allow/block policy for search rows and every fetch path), and `web.cache` (opt-in per-session page cache). The loader validates types and unknown keys and leaves range checks to the package, and the host's approval allow-list and `obscura.nativeTools: false` are unchanged. `examples/web-no-key.ts` now also shows `toolsWeb({ backend: "native" })` registering `web_fetch` with no key and no status note, and the roadmap, `docs/index.md`, package description and package-truth tables name the shipped backend set.

- **Every web backend is reachable from package config (plan 192 Task 8).** `toolsWeb` used to drop `ObscuraWebLimits` on the floor; `obscura.limits` is now declared in the config schema and passed to `createObscuraWebTools`, with out-of-range values failing setup as `invalid limits config` naming the key. The Obscura backend also provides the `web.fetch` service through a new exported `createObscuraPageFetcher` — the same URL-validated, byte-bounded `markdown` dump its `web_fetch` tool uses — so wiki ingest and RAG URL loaders work without a Firecrawl key. And a new `extract.schema` config field registers `web_extract` over that schema (main key for `backend: "firecrawl"`, `fetch.apiKey` otherwise), compiled at setup by the kernel's `validateJsonSchema` so an unsupported subset or remote `$ref` fails setup naming `extract.schema`, while a missing Firecrawl key writes a `warn` note and skips only `web_extract`. `ToolsWebObscuraConfig.limits` and `ToolsWebExtractConfig` are new exported config types.
- **Web transport queue and retry fixes (plan 192 Task 7).** The transport no longer refuses a call as soon as `maxConcurrency` requests are in flight: calls queue up to a new `maxQueued` limit (default 64, hard 256, added to `WebLimits`, `DEFAULT_WEB_LIMITS`, `HARD_WEB_LIMITS` and the package config schema) and only beyond it fail with `ERR_PRISM_WEB_LIMIT` `Web operation queue is full`, while a waiter stays bounded by `timeoutMs` and the caller's signal (an aborted waiter leaves the queue, takes no slot, and issues no request). `transport.json` also takes a per-request retry mode by billing semantics: `idempotent` for GETs (connection failures and 429/5xx), `safe-post` for Exa `/search` and Firecrawl `/v2/scrape` (connection failures and 429 only — a 5xx may mean the work was already done), and `never` for Firecrawl `/v2/extract` job creation so a paid job is never submitted twice; extract polling GETs stay `idempotent`. Finally, a `Retry-After` given as an HTTP date is converted to a delay from now (a past date means 0) instead of being ignored, still capped by `maxRateLimitDelayMs`.
- **`web_search` takes structured search controls that each backend maps (plan 192 Task 6).** New tool arguments `freshness` (`day`/`week`/`month`/`year`), `include_domains` and `exclude_domains` (up to 10 bare hostnames each), `country`, `language` and `offset` replace hand-written query operators: Brave maps them to `freshness`/`country`/`search_lang`/`offset` (0-based, ≤ 9) and `site:`/`-site:` operators, Exa to `startPublishedDate`/`includeDomains`/`excludeDomains`/`userLocation`, and Obscura's DuckDuckGo profile to `df` and a `kl` region-language pair plus `site:` operators. Each adapter declares a `supports` set — one table the tool builder and the adapter both read — so the schema and description expose only what the configured backend honors; an unsupported control (`offset` or `language` on Exa, `offset` on Obscura, which would need a `vqd` token from a second request) or a malformed one fails with `ERR_PRISM_WEB_INPUT` naming the argument instead of being silently ignored. Domain values must be bare hostnames — a scheme, path, wildcard, or `site:` syntax is rejected — and the operators they add count toward the provider's query byte, character, and word limits. One search is still one provider request. `WebSearchAdapter.search` now takes `WebSearchCall`, and `ObscuraSearchProfile.searchUrl` accepts an optional controls argument.
- **Web search results are a compact numbered list, and tool descriptions say what to use when (plan 192 Task 5).** `web_search` no longer hands the model raw JSON: its text content is `1. <title>` / the URL / `<published> — <snippet>` (≤300 characters) plus at most two 200-character provider highlights, rendered by a shared `packages/tools/web/src/render.ts` used by Brave, Exa, and Obscura. `value` keeps its citation structure, and per-result `metadata` is gone — provider and rate facts appear once on the response (`normalize.ts`). Titles, snippets, and highlights are collapsed to one line, so untrusted text cannot fake a numbered entry, and no Markdown link is synthesized from snippet text. `obscura_scrape` renders one numbered entry per URL with its row as JSON text, and `obscura_fetch` returns a 32 000-character slice of its dump as text while `value` keeps the whole output. `web_search` and `web_fetch` descriptions now state the tool's purpose, when to prefer one over the other, the `count` default and maximum (the schema's `count.maximum` comes from the resolved `maxResults`), the fetch slice defaults, and that content is untrusted — and name no backend vendor. A recorded ten-result search renders 3.1 kB of text against 6.3 kB of the previous payload.
- **`web_fetch` returns a context-sized slice of a page and pages on demand (plan 192 Task 4).** A fetched page can be a megabyte, which exceeds most context windows before compaction can run, so `web_fetch` now takes `max_chars` (default 32 000, maximum 100 000) and `offset`, and returns one slice with a `nextOffset` to continue from: text content is a `Showing characters <offset>–<end> of <total>.` header, a continuation line, then the slice, and `value` carries `{ markdown, offset, totalChars, truncated, nextOffset?, sourceTruncated }` beside the citation. Slices end on a paragraph or line break in the last tenth of the window and never split a surrogate pair. A page is memoized per tool instance by `(sessionId, url)` (8 pages, 10 minutes), so paging makes no new request and never crosses sessions. A document over the fetch limit is kept up to it with `sourceTruncated: true` instead of failing — only a response over `maxResponseBytes` still fails — and the `web.fetch` service honors `maxBytes`, which the RAG loader now passes. Firecrawl's Markdown rejection is gone.
- **Served MCP tools return structured content as well as text (plan 192 Task 3).** `serveTools`'s `contentResult` used to drop a result's `value` whenever the tool authored text, so an MCP client that reads `structuredContent` never saw it. A `tools/call` answer now carries the text blocks plus `structuredContent` when `value` is a JSON object within `limits.resultBytes`, falls back to a clipped-JSON text block when the content has no text, answers `isError: true` with `<code>: <message>` for an error, and answers an untrusted result with the kernel envelope from the new `formatUntrustedToolText` export — the same bytes the internal loop sees — followed by any non-text blocks. An over-cap value is served as clipped text only. `packages/hosts/mcp-server/README.md` documents the mapping.
- **Untrusted tool results reach the model in one kernel envelope, and the tools stop shouting their own banner (plan 192 Task 2).** A `ToolResult` whose `metadata.trust` is the new `UNTRUSTED_EXTERNAL_TRUST` (`"untrusted_external"`) is wrapped once, in `toToolResultMessage`, as a fixed preamble plus `<untrusted_external_content tool="…" source="…">` around the body, so the stored transcript, folded/compacted text, and every codec carry identical bytes instead of each package inventing a warning the transcript silently dropped whenever `value` was set. The body is the joined text content when present, else `JSON.stringify(value)`; `source` comes from `metadata.source` when it is an http(s) URL; a closing tag inside the body (any case, optional whitespace) is escaped so page text cannot end the envelope, and a forged opener is inert. An error result is not wrapped, and results without the marker keep plan 189's rule (`value` wins over text). `formatUntrustedToolText(result)` is exported for hosts that serve results over another protocol, and the kernel never names a package. Security: this is the one place that decides what untrusted text can look like to the model; `packages/tools/{web,obscura,browser,work}` no longer emit banner text, openapi and work lose theirs too, `web`/`obscura`/`openapi`/`wiki_ingest` now set `metadata.source` where the URL is known, the wiki ingest brief drops its second banner, and `wiki-init`/`wiki-lint`/`wiki-refresh` no longer mark their own status output untrusted. `tools-research`'s `withLedgerLine` stops copying the ledger line into `value` for untrusted results, because the text now reaches the model; trusted results keep the copy. Docs: `docs/tools.md` ("Untrusted external results"), `docs/provider-kit.md`, the web/browser/computer-use/wiki/work READMEs. Additive kernel exports only.

- **Transform hooks now carry the run's identity, so a package can key per-run state from `run.input` (plan 191 follow-up).** `HookBus.transform(name, value, identity?)` takes an optional third argument that supplies `context.sessionId`/`context.runId` when the payload is a bare value (a message, a message list, a `ProviderRequest`); a payload that names them still wins. The kernel passes the run's identity for `run.input` (both start input and mid-run steers), `message.append`, `context.build`, `model.request`, and `model.response`, so `context.runId` is no longer `""` on those hooks. `run.started` stays the observe hook for resetting a package's per-run state. `@arnilo/prism-extensions/advisor` now captures a run's task from `run.input` instead of re-reading the session branch at `turn.before`: the capture sees the exact input the run was given, a steer cannot replace it, and it costs no branch read.

- **A second model can now review work, and completion can be checked instead of claimed (plan 191 Task 8).** New package `@arnilo/prism-extensions/advisor`: a `model` is required and an unresolvable one is a `warn` note that registers nothing. It adds one `advise` tool (one `models.client` call with no tools over `selectTranscript(<branch>, context)` plus the question, capped by `adviceMaxChars`), opt-in `triggers.beforeTools` (a `tool.call` decide hook: `revise` denies the call with `Advisor: <advice>` as the result, and a retry with identical arguments is allowed without a second consult) and `triggers.beforeFinish` (a `run.end` decide hook that continues with the advice as a steer), and an opt-in `validate` stage. `validate` pins the run's task (captured at its first turn, inferred from the first user message when a run was resumed empty), asks a model or a hidden `delegate` child through the `delegate` service for a `complete`/`incomplete` verdict, continues with the `missing` list while rounds remain, and emits `unverified` after `maxRounds`; it fails closed, so an error, timeout, unparseable verdict, or missing engine never yields `verified`. Hosts read `advisor.consult` and `validator.verdict` `package.event`s (never the transcript, the advice, or the final answer); a run reporting `completed` alone does not mean verified. Advisor consults never block: every failure allows the tool call or ends the run normally.

- **A branch can be handed to another agent as a bounded, portable transcript (plan 191 Task 7).** `selectTranscript(entries, { lastTurns?, maxChars, maxToolChars?, pinned? })` is the one place that decides what a second consumer may see: it accepts branch entries or live `Message[]` and returns `{ summaries, pinned, messages, turns, omittedTurns, truncated }`. It walks the branch like `rebuildSessionContext` (a compaction summary replaces what it covers) and keeps only messages and summaries, so `package_state`, `custom`, `event`, `suspended_run`, `metadata`, and `label` entries never travel — a copy cannot hydrate a package's state or resume someone else's run. Rendering is provider- and tool-set-neutral: text only, a tool call as `[tool_call <name> <args, first 200 chars>]`, a tool result as `[tool_result <name>: <text, first maxToolChars>]` in a user-role message, images as `[image omitted]`, thinking and provider metadata dropped, consecutive same-role messages merged, and an assistant tool call without its result dropped so an in-flight call never becomes an unmatched `tool_use`. Turns are taken newest first up to `lastTurns` and `maxChars`; a newest turn too big alone is cut from its oldest end (`truncated`), summaries are cut last, and `pinned` messages are charged first and never repeated. Additive root export only; the first consumer is `@arnilo/prism-extensions/delegate`'s `context` config.

- **Nested `AGENTS.md` files arrive just in time, frozen at the first touch** (plan 190 Task 7). `@arnilo/prism-extensions/context-files` appends a deeper directory's `AGENTS.md` (or `CLAUDE.md` when absent) as one extra text part on the first coding tool result that touches its subtree, with `metadata.contextFiles` and `source: "context-files-nested"` on that message. `read`/`edit`/`write`/`diagnostics` use `path`, `grep`/`glob` their directory `path`, `move` `from`/`to`; paths outside `root` are ignored. The first insertion's exact bytes live in package session state, so a file edit cannot rewrite an earlier request; compaction drops the anchor and the next touch reinserts the then-current file. Each directory appears once per provider view. `nested` defaults to `true`; `false` restores root-only behavior, and `{ maxFileBytes, maxFiles }` lowers the 16 KiB / 16-file defaults (hard caps 64 KiB / 16); over a limit is one `status.note("info", …)` naming the path, and a realpath escaping the trusted roots is skipped with a warning. The shared tool-result serializer now appends trailing text parts, so both the OpenAI Chat and Anthropic Messages codecs carry the text. The package gains the `session-state` capability; no kernel contract changed.

- **`load_skill` returns the body in its result, and compaction re-attaches only the current run's skills** (plan 190 Task 3). The tool result is now the header plus the full instructions, with `metadata.loadedSkill` and `metadata.loadedSkillRunId`; a second load answers `Skill <name> is already loaded; its instructions are earlier in this conversation.` instead of failing. Progressive disclosure no longer appends every loaded body to every request: a body is re-attached only when no non-folded provider-visible tool result carries it, and after compaction only for skills loaded in the current run. Earlier-run skills leave the loaded set and are named once in a sorted, capped `Previously loaded skills (load again if needed): …` tail line, so a later `load_skill` returns the full body again. Restored checkpoint bodies belong to the resumed run. `MAX_LOAD_SKILL_RESULT_BYTES` grows to the body hard cap plus a header allowance; failure texts keep their 512-byte bound. The prefix-stability conformance fixture now expects the body in the tool result and a stable prefix.

- **Frontmatter from Claude Code, Codex, and the skills CLI now parses without guessing** (plan 190 Task 2). `splitFrontmatter` reads the subset those agents write: folded/literal block scalars with chomping, multi-line plain scalars, single- and double-quoted scalars, nested maps, flow lists, and block lists of maps (the subagent `hooks:` shape). Values stay strings, so `true` and `1.0.0` are never coerced; anchors, aliases, tags, flow maps, tab indentation, and nesting beyond 8 levels fail with `Malformed frontmatter in <path>: <reason> at line N` instead of silently turning a folded line into a metadata key. `parseAgentFile` now also returns the body as `instructions` and a top-level `description`, for plan 191's subagent loader. No YAML dependency and no `node:*` import; fixtures cover the six repo skills plus Claude Code and skills-CLI samples.

- **Multi-session recall passes its live held-out acceptance: passive injection plus the read tools is non-inferior to whole-history context at about a quarter of the tokens** (plan 188.10 Task 9). Stage A selected `fabric-x-tools` on development data, so the one fresh draw ran once: held-out v3, 100 ids read for the first time, labels on, protocol v2. The manifest comes from `screen-a/verdict.json` and refuses a product hash other than Stage A's, and the criteria are data in it (multi-session ≥ 24/40, a paired improvement with a 95% CI lower bound above 0, per-ability non-regression) with `acceptance-report.mjs --plan 188.10` computing the verdict. Measured: 76/100 and multi-session 28/40 for `fabric-x-tools`, against `fabric-x` 49/100 and 10/40 — a paired +0.45 (95% CI 0.275–0.625) and overall +0.27 — and 69/100 for whole history at 112,955 tokens per question, so the selected arm is +0.07 at 29,669, inside the 3-point non-inferiority margin: non-inferior, not an accuracy win. The lever is read access to the exchange store (152 tool calls, evidence coverage 0.63 → 0.83), not a wider window. Gate 1 used 23,216,343 of 34,000,000 tokens, including an aborted attempt whose caps ignored the kernel's in-flight reservation; it is kept as reliability evidence and the caps are now 3M/9M/14M. `packages/memory/fabric/README.md` documents this as the recommended conversational profile; package defaults stay off, because the safety side of storing conversation content by default is unmeasured. F13 moves to `fixed`. Harness, evidence and docs only: no product, protocol or default changed.

- **Liquid d1 ships as the fifth provider package, hosted and local** (plan 196). `@arnilo/prism-ai/liquid` registers two System One decision providers from one package: hosted `liquid` — `d1.1` and `d1-ultra` at `https://api.liquid.ai/decisions/v1/systemone`, key from `env:LIQUID_API_KEY`, $0.02 and $0.042 per MTok input with free output and text/image input — and `liquid-local` — `d1-3B` served by llama.cpp `llama-server` at `http://127.0.0.1:8080` with a 32,768-token context, `input: ["text", "image"]`, an optional `localApiKey` ref for `--api-key`, and no key by default. `hosted: false` gives a local-only setup that records no missing-key note; a missing hosted key notes a `warn` and still registers the catalog, a literal key is `secret_literal`, and the key joins the redaction set. The hosted path is pinned to the documented `/decisions/v1/systemone` (the root `/v1/systemone` alias is undocumented), and the README covers Liquid's 4.5 MB / 8-image request caps, the `b11480` minimum `llama-server` build, `--alias` versus router mode, the LFM Open License 1.0 terms for the `d1-3B` weights, and the unconfirmed hosted-vs-local weight identity (the agreement rate lands with the plan 196 bench). It reuses the kit's `createSystemOneDecisionProvider` rather than `systemOneProviderPackage`, because that helper registers exactly one provider. Offline wire recordings are reconstructed from Liquid's docs (no hosted key was available), gated live rows `providers/liquid` and `providers/liquid-local` are registered, and the compat baseline, package-truth tables, packaging gate, coverage row and docs are updated for the fifth adapter. Additive package only; no kernel contract changed.

- **Endpoint recipes and one offline decision example** (plan 195 Task 5). `docs/decisions.md` now shows how to reach OpenRouter, Fastino GLiDE, Celeris-1 Decision, Microsoft Foundry, TrueFoundry, `llama-server`, and `laya-serve` by changing only the base URL, path, key ref, and model id of `createSystemOneDecisionProvider`, with one caveat and one price source each (System One replies have no output tokens), plus operating rules for pinned ids, per-model threshold calibration, keeping the question out of the state, never relying on a decision model alone against prompt injection, and Q8_0-or-higher local heads. `examples/decision-models.ts` runs offline on the mock provider's `decisions` adapter: a `models`-capability package calls `prism.models.decisions()` from a `run.input` hook, routes a ticket with `noul`/`choice`/`score` questions, prints the probabilities, and asserts a model without the capability throws `DecisionsError("unsupported_model")`. Docs and example only: no new exports or packages.
- **System One providers now expose raw `decisions()`, images, and a configurable endpoint path** (plan 195 Task 4). `createSystemOneDecisionProvider` returns a `decisions()` adapter over the same client as `generate()` (key resolution, retry, abort, redaction), so `typesafe` and `laya` callers get probabilities, confidence, legend, usage and timing instead of rendered text; 401/422 map to `DecisionsError("invalid_request")` with the credential redacted. `SystemOneBody.images` carries inline `data:image/*` URLs; `compileSystemOneState` now returns `{ state, images }`, moves user image parts out of `state`, forwards images on both the decide and `structuredOutput` paths, and omits the field when empty so text-only bodies stay byte-identical. `path` (default `/v1/systemone`, must start with `/`) is a client option and package config, so Foundry-style deployments need no fork. Both catalogs declare `decisions: true` and stay `input: ["text"]` (an image to either is rejected before fetch); `systemOneConformance` gains decide cases and the live suites gain one raw decide call with all three question types. Additive client/package options and a kit return-shape change (`compileSystemOneState`); compat baselines regenerated.
- **Typed decision calls are a kernel capability** (plan 195). `ModelProvider.decisions?()` and `ModelCapabilities.decisions` sit beside embeddings, and `prism.models.decisions(ref?)` returns an adapter with the resolved model id bound, so a package calls `decide({ state, questions })` and gets raw probabilities, confidence, legend, usage and timing back instead of rendered text. `noul`/`choice`/`score` questions are validated once in the kernel before any adapter runs: 1-64 questions, 2-255 choice options, 2-10 score levels, a 256 KiB state ceiling, and inline `data:image/*` images capped at 8 MiB decoded, with `unsupported_model` / `invalid_request` / `state_too_large` typed errors. Images require a declared `image` input. The `/testing` mock provider gains a `decisions` adapter for offline tests, and `assertDecisionRequest` is public so provider adapters enforce the same bounds on the raw `provider.decisions()` path. Additive exports only; compat baseline regenerated. The System One codec, provider adapters and endpoint recipes land separately.
- **Passive injection plus the read tools clears the development target on the exchange store, so Stage B is not needed** (plan 188.10 Task 7). Gate 1 was approved at 34,000,000 tokens, recorded verbatim with its date in `GATE_1_APPROVAL` and in the manifest, and an unapproved cap is refused by `assertScreenAManifest`, so the campaign cannot spend before the owner records it.
