New selector `--acceptance-188.10-screen-a` runs the frozen 60-id `screen-ms-v1` development draw with labels off and concurrency 4: a local LLM-free `fabric-x` writer, the paid passive `fabric-x` reference over its snapshot, and `fabric-x-tools` (passive injection plus `memory.*`) over the same snapshot, so the only difference between the two paid arms is tool visibility. `T_dev` lives in the manifest (multi-session ≥ 18/30, every other ability loses at most 1 of 5, run-limit rate ≤ 5%) and the campaign writes `verdict.json` from it. Measured: 1,639,027 input tokens (writer 0, `fabric-x` 239,050, `fabric-x-tools` 1,399,977) in about 19 minutes wall against the 60-minute limit — multi-session 8/30 → 22/30, total 28/60 → 50/60, with knowledge-update and single-session-user unchanged and abstention +3, single-session-preference +3, single-session-assistant +1, temporal-reasoning +1, and a 0 run-limit rate. Both channels were visible in all 30 multi-session questions, with 89 tool calls and 447 notes read by id; the cost is the extra steps (533k injected tokens) and not a larger window. `verdict.json` records `selected: "x-tools"` and `next: { skipStageB: true, heldoutArm: "x-tools" }`, so Task 8 is recorded as not needed. The re-run text-free diagnostic over the same snapshot (post-Task-4 cue) reports 17 `partial_evidence`, 7 `answer_gap`, 5 `selection_gap`, 1 `candidate_gap`, 74/77 lexically reachable rounds and 7/30 passive coverage: the residual failures are evidence the agent does not chase, not a missing store. The first Stage A journal is archived as `spend-journal-pre-split.json` because the reference arm was split into a free writer and a paid reader after the initial two-arm manifest had already spent 1,137,259 on one arm. Harness, evidence and docs only: no product, protocol or default changed.

- **Conversational facts are instance-safe, category-keyed and dated** (plan 188.10 Task 6). The extractor's shared prompt rules now say what aggregation needs: **one fact per instance** (each event, purchase, visit, trip, item or person is its own note, even when several share a category), **keys only for changeable attributes** (never two instances on one key — an instance takes no key or one unique to it, and a stated count is an attribute while the items are instances), **2–4 generic category keywords** ("doctor", "health", "appointment" for a dermatologist visit) with specific names and synonyms in `aliases`, and **dated events** (an event with no time of its own takes the date of the candidate it came from). Prompt text only: no new note kind, field or write-path code. Because the keywords and aliases are merged into the note's indexed keywords at 3× weight, "which doctors did I see" now reaches a fact that only says "dermatologist", and because the supersession rule still needs kind, scope, key *and* a strictly later `tRef`, two un-keyed visits stay active instead of one deleting the other. The system prompt grows 619 B (≈155 tokens), inside the 250-token budget, and the pinned prompt-breakdown test is updated. Tests prove the rules are stated, two instances both stay active, a keyed attribute still supersedes, and the multi-session diagnostic reports no supersession loss on an instance fixture; the coding prompt keeps its repository rules. Compat baseline regenerated (prompt literals only).
- **The consolidation extractor gets a real output budget, a reasoning level, truncation repair and counted failures** (plan 188.10 Task 5). `FabricConsolidationReport` now carries `failures` (a tally by the closed reason set) and `outputLimited`, and `readPromoteState` keeps both, so `memory:status` reports why a pass failed instead of dropping it; `consolidate()` returns `{ ran: false, reason: "failed", reasonCode }` with that report rather than throwing a pass away. Two host knobs decide the single extractor call. `promote.maxOutputTokens` (integer 256–32,768) re-resolves the client with `parameters.maxTokens = min(maxOutputTokens, model.limits.maxOutputTokens)` — the rule compaction already uses — so the write side can ask for room 48 notes need without changing the solver's 4,096-token answer budget; `promote.thinkingLevel` (`none`…`max`) goes to the worker loop. A call that stops on `max_output_tokens` without a valid `record_notes` answer is the new reason `output_truncated`, repaired once with an instruction for fewer, shorter notes over the same evidence, and a second truncation fails the pass (the stop reason is captured by wrapping the client's `generate`, because the worker loop drops `done` events). Both knobs validate through the config schema, and both are recorded in the benchmark's manifest `writeGeneration` with the write-side key hashing it, so two extractor budgets never share a snapshot while answer and judge generation stay at 4,096. The write side reports `consolidationFailuresByReason`, `extractorCallsAtMaxOutput` and `extractorInputPerSession`. Measured offline on 5 development multi-session haystacks: the extractor prompt is ~44 kB with an empty store and ~58 kB worst case, of which the harness (system 2,845 B, schema 1,621–2,156 B, up to 13,383 B of existing notes, segments) is a bounded minority and the session's own candidates are ~89% — so no trim was made, and `extractor_prompt_size_breakdown_matches_fixture` pins those sizes. Additive exports (`EXTRACTION_REASONS`, `memoryFabricPromoteSchema`, `readPromoteState`, the two budget bounds, `consolidationReport`); the compat baseline is regenerated.

- **Passive injection now discloses a partial selection, and the benchmark has the product's passive-plus-tools arms** (plan 188.10 Task 4). When fewer notes are shown than were eligible after the similarity floor, the injected block ends with `Showing N of M matching notes. To list, count or compare across past conversations, call memory.recall with several phrasings (queries) and memory.read the ids you need.` The line names both read tools unconditionally, because per-run tool visibility is not visible to the package; it carries counts only, is absent when everything eligible was shown or when `inject` is off, and counts inside `budgetTokens` (about 43 tokens by the block's own estimate) by taking the place of the last delivered note instead of overspending. `fitInjection` takes the eligible count as an optional fifth argument, `context.ts` passes the count it already computed (no second recall), and the delivery diagnostic mirror passes the same count so product and diagnostic stay byte-equal. Two benchmark arms measure that shape: `fabric-x-tools` and `fabric-xc-tools` keep `memory.*` visible next to passive injection over the matching store (their write sides are the existing `fabric-x`/`fabric-xc` snapshots) and report tool calls, recalls, reads, writes and the run limit like agentic arms; `fabric-xc-agentic` and the solver protocol are unchanged. Additive API change: `fitInjection`'s fifth parameter is optional, so existing callers are unaffected; the compat baseline is regenerated. The offline wiring check on the synthetic fixture made 28 tool calls and 14 recalls with no answer-phase write and no run limit.

- **A multi-session diagnostic says which lever a recall failure needs** (plan 188.10 Task 3). `retrieval-diagnostics.mjs --multi-session --store-from <snapshot>` reads the product store per multi-session id and reports, per needed fine round, its rank in the uncapped question-only lexical ranking and whether the round shares a stemmed content term with the question, plus all-needed at the top 10, 20 and uncapped, shipped passive coverage, and stage counts including `partial_evidence`. When the store holds facts it also reports the needed rounds covered by an active fact linked with relation `evidence`, the rounds whose only linked fact is superseded or retired (supersession loss), and facts per needed round; it reads note metadata only, so no note text is touched. It refuses `heldout-v3`, requires `--store-from` and fails closed on a missing snapshot, and the evidence holds ids, ranks, counts and booleans only. Measured on the free local `fabric-x` write side of the 60-id `screen-ms-v1` draw: 30 questions, 77 needed rounds across 73 sessions, 74/77 reachable, all-needed in the top 10 for 14 and uncapped for 19, shipped passive coverage 8/30, stages `partial_evidence` 16 / `answer_gap` 8 / `selection_gap` 5 / `candidate_gap` 1; a repeated diagnostic is byte-identical and the whole local run takes 28 s. Harness only; no product, protocol or default changed and nothing paid ran.

- **The memory benchmark has a fresh held-out draw and a multi-session development screen** (plan 188.10 Task 2). `heldout-v3.json` freezes 100 unused LongMemEval-S ids — 40 multi-session, 20 knowledge-update, 20 temporal-reasoning, 14 single-session-user, 6 single-session-assistant — by SHA-256(`prism-188.10-memory-bench-v3:` + id), excluding base, targeted, confirmatory and held-out v2, with the dataset SHA-256, ids hash, file hash and residual pool counts committed beside them; `drawHeldoutV3` reads only `id` and `ability` and fails closed on a short or duplicated stratum. `screen-ms-v1.json` is the development gate: all 20 held-out v2 multi-session ids, 10 drawn from the 30-id development multi-session pool and screen-v1's 30 non-multi-session ids, 60 ids in total, marked development-only; the screen profile now refuses `heldout-v3` as it already refused `heldout-v2`. `--subset heldout-v3`/`screen-ms-v1` select the frozen draws, `--ceiling` keeps its development-only refusal and the offline diagnostics refuse `heldout-v3` outright, because a diagnostic reads gold evidence. Harness-only: no product, protocol, default or export changed and nothing paid ran.

- **Held-out acceptance for 188.9, and the exchange arm wins on improvement but not substitution** (plan 188.9 Task 11). New selector `--acceptance-188.9` runs the 188.8-pinned baseline, the screened-in `fabric-x` arm and its local write side on held-out v2 (100 ids) under the unchanged protocol (OpenCode Go `mimo-v2.5`, temperature 0, max output 4,096, protocol v2), with `fabric-xc` out of scope by owner decision and the full-context reference reused from 188.8 after a semantic reuse check. Result: `fabric-x` 76/100 versus the pinned baseline's 51/100 (+0.25 overall, 95% CI 0.16..0.34; knowledge-update +0.5, Holm p 0.0078), which makes it an opt-in improvement over full context's 79/100 rather than a substitute (-0.03, 95% CI -0.12..0.06 against the 3-point margin), so F13 stays `unresolved` and defaults stay provisional. The report also keeps the product cost apart from the benchmark cost (write side per stored session, read side per question) and joins a product-store delivery diagnostic for the stage table and conditional accuracy. Harness-only: `scripts/memory-bench/{acceptance,acceptance-report,campaign}.mjs`, their tests, the README, and `docs/_evidence/memory-bench/188.9/`.
- **A paid development screen gates the held-out spend** (plan 188.9 Task 10). New campaign selectors `--acceptance-188.9-probe` and `--acceptance-188.9-screen`. The probe runs the passive baseline on 8 frozen `screen-v1` ids at question concurrency 4, 8, 16 and 24, one step at a time, records per-question p50/p95 and every provider overload (`calls.overloads`, which now counts 429/503/529 even when the gate cannot halve again), and `chooseConcurrency` picks the highest step with zero overloads and a p95 under 1.5x the step-4 value, bounded by the reservation the approved ceiling can hold. The screen then runs `fabric`, `fabric-x`, `fabric-xc` and `fabric-xc-agentic` on the 35-id development draw with injection labels off, ordered behind two write-side producers (`fabric-x`, local and free; `fabric-xc`, the one paid write path) whose snapshots the read arms reuse; a read arm refuses to run without one. The campaign writes `verdict.json` itself: a 3-question gain with no ability loss over 1 for the passive arms, a run-limit rate at most 5% for the agentic arm; arms it cannot afford are declared `excluded` with the measurement that says why, and the verdict marks them `missing` rather than failed. On the 188.9 screen the 188.8-pinned baseline (variant `fabric.delivery=188.8`: whole notes, 600 tokens, top 3, similarity 0.35) scored 17/35 and `fabric-x` 25/35, so `fabric-x` gains 8 questions with a worst ability loss of 1 and proceeds to held-out acceptance; the shipped 4,000-token delivery on the legacy store scored 26/35 as a descriptive arm, and `fabric-xc` stays unmeasured, with a 1.26-calls-per-session write side measured at concurrency 1. Write-side cache keys now include the config, because `fabric-x` and `fabric-xc` store different notes for the same ids. Harness-only: `scripts/memory-bench/{acceptance,campaign,run}.mjs`, their tests, the README, and `docs/_evidence/memory-bench/188.9/screen/`. The screen is development data: it can drop an arm, never tune a parameter.
- **The memory benchmark can measure the product write path, and the product can consolidate with scopes disabled** (plan 188.9 Task 9). New harness arms `fabric-x` (exchange archive only, LLM-free write side), `fabric-xc` (archive plus consolidation, facts linked to their exchanges) and `fabric-xc-agentic` (the `xc` store with tools and passive injection off). Instead of the 188.8 direct upsert — still used by the `fabric` baseline and labelled `legacy-direct-ingest` — each causal haystack session becomes its own product session: messages are appended with their timestamps, the shipped `memory:archive` command back-fills the session, `memory:consolidate` runs for `xc`, the session closes, and the answer session starts fresh. Reports record `ingestion: "product-per-session" | "legacy-direct-ingest" | "none"`; a parity test pins the archived exchange text and source ids to `sessionRounds`. New `--phase write` builds only the store and answers nothing (a local, extractor-free write side needs no provider or paid-attempt reservation), the `xc` write side is a `writeSide` job whose snapshot every read arm reuses and refuses to run without, snapshots carry a note→round map so evidence stages stay round-grained, and `retrieval-diagnostics.mjs --store-from <snapshot>` diagnoses a product-injected store offline. The extractor is now reachable during the ingest phase (`record_notes` requests reach the provider and are counted as `calls.consolidate`), which is what makes the `xc` write side possible. Product fix, found by the new arms: consolidation used to fail with `scope user is not active` before any model call when a scope was switched off — existing-note gathering now returns no notes for an inactive scope, and an extracted note for a disabled scope is written to the active project scope instead of aborting the pass. Harness-only otherwise: `scripts/memory-bench/{replay,ablations,run,retrieval-diagnostics,campaign}.mjs`, their tests, the README, and `docs/_evidence/memory-bench/188.9/delivery-development-fabric-x.json` (140 development questions, product store, 48 s, 0 tokens).
- **The consolidation extractor is domain-neutral and turns archived exchanges into dated, keyed facts** (plan 188.9 Task 8). `CONSOLIDATION_EXTRACT_SYSTEM` no longer says "coding agent": the repository and derivability rules moved to a second prompt, `CONSOLIDATION_EXTRACT_SYSTEM_PROJECT`, which the pass uses only when a project scope with a declared root is active. Both prompts add the general durability rule — a specific fact the user stated (a possession, event, date, count, name, plan or changed value) is a note — plus `tRef` (the instant the evidence names, validated as ISO 8601 and stored on the note) and `key` (a changeable attribute, so a later value supersedes the earlier one through `supersedes` instead of piling up); a keyed note whose `tRef` is later than the shown same-kind/same-scope/same-key note supersedes it. When `exchanges` is enabled, `consolidate()` also collects archived exchanges no pass has read yet (metadata `consolidatedAt`), scope-tagged and bounded by the same `maxCandidates`/`maxTextChars` budget, as a new `exchange` candidate source whose evidence ids are the exchange note ids and whose `tRef` is the exchange's; the pass marks them read whether or not it wrote anything. An accepted note that cites an exchange carries `links: [{ id, relation: "evidence" }]` — the fact is the index, the exchange is the verbatim detail — and only an exchange its own scope can read (same scope, `user`, or path→project) is linked; exchange ids are never written as transcript `sourceEntryIds`. An exchange candidate also supports the `explicit` signal, since an exchange is verbatim user speech. No extra model call: exchanges join the existing one-call-per-pass, with input bounded by the same candidate ceiling. Passive delivery now renders facts first and then exchanges, each group in time order (selection still by rank), and `DEFAULT_RECALL_KINDS` includes `exchange`, so an opted-in archive can be injected passively without naming kinds. Package-internal apart from the second prompt export, the `tRef`/`consolidatedAt` note fields and the new `ConsolidationSource` member; the compat baseline diff is the two prompt literals.
- **The exchange archive** stores conversation, not only conclusions (plan 188.9 Task 7). A new `exchange` kind holds one completed user turn plus the assistant reply that followed it, verbatim (`user: …` then `assistant: …`), dated with the user entry's timestamp and backed by the two **session entry** ids. `exchanges: {}` (off by default) archives each turn from the `message.completed` hook — the one that fires after the reply is appended — in `session.background`, so no model call, embedding call or extra store query is added to the request path; an assistant message carrying a tool call is never an exchange, and tool arguments/results are never stored. The scope is settled once at setup: `project` only when the project scope is active *and* a root was declared (`scopes.project.root` or `tools.root`), else `user`, else `agent`, never `path`, so a conversational host does not file history under its cwd. A turn longer than the configured `limits.maxEntryTextChars` splits on message, paragraph, line, then character boundaries into linked parts (`relation: "part"`, shared `tRef` and source ids, `context: "part i of n"`) instead of failing the write limit. Exchanges decay under their own `lifecycle.decay.exchange` (0.5) and follow capacity/archive, are never promoted, pinned to core, team-shared, or tool-written. `memory:archive` back-fills a session idempotently (by entry ids and a per-session cursor) and reports `archived`, `skipped`, `parts`. Security: the kernel persists entries after redaction, so a secret in a turn is already `[REDACTED]` by the time the archive reads it; the archive additionally refuses (and counts) a turn the trust scan flags or a supplied redactor would rewrite, and reports it through `prism.status.note`. `exchange` joins `MEMORY_NOTE_KINDS` and the `memory.recall` explicit kind enum (the tool default already covered it), the inject `kinds` enum and the `lifecycle.decay` schema; `DEFAULT_RECALL_KINDS` is unchanged, so history is injected only when asked for. Store layout gains an `exchanges/` directory (no migration); the compat baseline is unchanged.

- `memory.read` reads notes by id and `memory.recall` converges in one round (plan 188.9 Task 6). `memory.read` accepts exactly one of `path`, `id` or `ids` (1–8) and returns each note whole with its header — id, scope, kind, date, path — through the same scope, consent, invalidation and redaction filters as recall, so an unknown, foreign-scope or revoked id is `not_found` for that id alone. `memory.recall` counts its default 1,200-token budget on the **rendered** lines it prints, shows each hit as the densest 600-character window of matched query terms, names every hit's relative path, and with no `kinds` searches every stored kind except `working`, so an episode or procedure from stored history answers without a second call. Passive recall and `DEFAULT_RECALL_KINDS` are unchanged. The 188.8 kind-discovery follow-up and its `lexicalRecallContext` flag are deleted (with the now-dead embedding-suppression check), and the old ±200-character `renderSnippet` is replaced by the shared `queryExcerpt`; a new `readNotes` tool dependency serves cross-scope reads without widening the `MemoryFabric` interface, and the tool parameter schemas are runtime objects, so no export or compat baseline changed. Harness: the mock agentic provider now converges recall → `memory.read` → answer in three turns under the unchanged eight-turn cap.

- Opted-in passive injection now delivers evidence-sized whole notes: ranked hits render whole while they fit the budget (defaults `budgetTokens: 4000`, `topK: 12`, `minSimilarity: 0.2`, about 4,000 tokens per user turn), a later hit that does not fit is skipped instead of excerpted, only an over-budget best hit is cut down to the budget (query-centred, naming `memory.read <id>`), and shown hits are delivered in time order (`tRef` else `ingestedAt`, ties by rank) so a knowledge update is read after the value it replaces. Selection stays by rank, the untrusted envelope and eligibility filters are unchanged, `inject` remains `false` by default, and no model call, store query or write is added (plan 188.9 Task 5). The defaults are the committed pre-registered choice from `docs/_evidence/memory-bench/188.9/ceiling-development.json`: the pre-registered literal rule (`S >= S* - 2` plus abstention at or under the median) selects no cell, so the recorded fallback anchors the cost at the acceptance criterion's ~4,000 tokens and takes the best evidence cell whose per-ability answer visibility does not fall below the 600/3/0.35 control.

- Memory-benchmark campaigns can now run arms in parallel, reuse a completed arm, screen on a frozen development draw and pay for a fabric write side once (plan 188.9 Task 4). `--parallel-arms 1-8` (default 1) keeps the existing per-arm journal reservation, so the ceiling holds when every running arm hits its cap; a failed arm stops new launches but never cancels a sibling's reconciliation. A job may declare `reuse: { from }`, and the runner compares a `runPathFingerprint` over the id set, input cap, generation, provider, model, judge, judge template hash, dataset, seed, ablations, memory config, protocol and content hashes of the run-path files (instead of the whole-repo `sourceRevision`, which differs legitimately between plans); a match copies the verdict rows marked `reused: true` and spends nothing, a mismatch throws `MemoryBenchReuseMismatch` or runs on `fallback: "run"`. `--screen` applies the frozen 35-id development draw (`screen-v1.json`, 5 per LongMemEval ability) with `--phase all`, labels off and concurrency 8, and is refused for held-out ids, non-LongMemEval suites and acceptance manifests. A read-side arm may name a `writeSide` job: the producer snapshots each question's fabric store under `.cache/memory-bench/write/<key>/` (key over dataset, product write-path hash, extractor model and generation, prompt template hash and harness ingest hash) and readers seed from it instead of re-ingesting, failing closed with `MemoryBenchWriteSideMissing` when it is absent. Harness only; no product, protocol or default changed.

- Memory-benchmark runs can be screened in two phases with bounded concurrency and cached verdicts (plan 188.9 Task 3). `--phase answer` replays and writes a verdict-free checkpoint plus a private answer cache, `--phase judge` scores that checkpoint at its own concurrency against the same journal reservation, and `--phase all` stays the default. `--concurrency 1-32` (default 4) bounds the question pool, and a provider `429`/`503`/`529` halves the arm's active ceiling without dropping in-flight permits, recorded in `timing.concurrency` and `calls.concurrencyHalvings`. Judge verdicts are cached under `.cache/` keyed by prompt-template hash, judge model, generation, suite, ability, question id and answer text, so an identical verdict costs nothing (`calls.judgeCacheHits`) while a template, model, generation or answer change misses; injection labels run in parallel per question and `--label-injections off` records `injectionPrecision: null` with reason `not-labelled` (refused in an acceptance manifest). Every report gains a `timing` block: arm wall time, per-question p50/p95, and per-role call latency with time to first byte. Measured on the delayed mock, a 20-question arm at concurrency 8 with split phases takes 16% of the sequential time (656 ms vs 4112 ms). Harness only; no product, protocol or default changed.

- Memory-benchmark delivery stages no longer blame synthesis for partial evidence (plan 188.9 Task 2). `retrievalStage` now returns `partial_evidence` when some but not all labelled message rounds are visible and `answer_gap` only when every one is; the stage is computed by `stageFromCounts`, so a committed diagnostic can be re-bucketed without re-running recall. `retrieval-diagnostics.mjs --restage` does that from stored ids and counts alone and `acceptance-report.mjs --plan 188.8` re-derives stages itself and adds a conditional-accuracy table (accuracy when all needed evidence is visible vs when it is not). `--ceiling` runs the frozen 180-cell delivery grid (budget 600-6000, topK 3-16, floor 0-0.35, renderer excerpt/whole) on the development partition only, refuses any other subset before reading the dataset, ingests once per question and recalls once per floor; it also carries explicit `evidenceBudget` and `candidateCoverage` reproduction blocks. Corrected 188.8 attribution, from the committed diagnostics: post-fix fabric 17 partial_evidence + 15 fine_selection_gap + 9 answer_gap instead of 15 + 26, i.e. delivery (32 of 53 failures) rather than synthesis (9); when every needed round is visible post-fix fabric answers 36/45 (80%) and pre-fix 34/41 (83%), while full context scores 79/100 overall. Harness and evidence only; no product, default or protocol fingerprint changed, and no paid run was repeated.

- Memory-benchmark paid arms are measurable end to end (plan 188.8 Task 5). `campaign.mjs --acceptance-188.8` runs a frozen five-arm manifest on the 100-id held-out v2 draw under a 30M campaign ceiling: pre-fix `fabric` from an isolated worktree carrying only the post-fix harness, post-fix `fabric`, post-fix `fabric-agentic`, a `full` reference and a development non-regression arm. `prefixArmAudit` refuses a pre-fix worktree whose harness hash differs or whose product matches the treatment, the child's journal and output paths are absolute so a worktree child keeps one journal, and a child that writes no report stops the campaign instead of charging every remaining reservation. `acceptance-report.mjs --plan 188.8` reports the predeclared four-ability Holm family and the substitution question separately, gates quality verdicts on paired clusters and simulated power (`statisticallyPowered`) while `costClaim` follows complete usage accounting, and marks F13 from the substitution verdict alone. A per-question product error is scored wrong with `outcome: "error"`, `errorCode` and `errorRate` and the arm continues, guarded by `max(3, 5%)` and still failing on any provider failure carrying a cause; the delivery diagnostic gains a `--renderer whole` mirror of the pre-fix rule and a `store_error` stage so an unstoreable question is reported instead of aborting the run. Measured: post-fix fabric 47/100 versus pre-fix 46/100 on held-out v2 (overall +0.01, 95% CI [-0.05,0.07], Holm p 1.0), fabric-agentic 38/100 with an 18% run-limit rate, full context 79/100, so post-fix fabric stays -0.32 against full and F13 is unresolved; delivery moved selected evidence (fine_selection_gap 18 -> 15) without moving answers. Harness, evidence and docs only; no product default, export or config changed.

- `memory.recall` now hints at episode/procedure matches after an empty default-kind result, using one extra local lexical recall with no new embedding call or disclosed note content. Empty discovery returns `no match in any kind`. Memory benchmarks score run-limit answers wrong, retain bounded tool traces, continue the arm, and report `runLimitRate`; the eight-turn limit is unchanged (plan 188.8 Task 4).

- `memory-fabric` passive injection fits more of its top-k inside the snippet budget (plan 188.8 Task 3). The best eligible note is still shown whole, even past `budgetTokens`. Each later note now gets an equal share of the remaining budget, rendering whole when it fits and otherwise as an excerpt centred on the densest run of matched query terms, ending `(excerpt; memory.read <id> for the full note)`; before, a later note that did not fit whole was dropped. Selection, filters, the untrusted envelope and the `memory.recall` tool's snippets are unchanged (the tool now shares the same internal window helper), and no query, write or model call is added. Budget accounting now counts the newlines that join the block. Opt-in only (`inject: false` stays the default); no export, config or default changed. On the frozen 188.8 development partition, offline: notes injected per question go from 1.08 to 1.59, all needed evidence rounds become visible for more knowledge-update (0→4), multi-session (0→2), temporal-reasoning (2→4) and single-session-user (10→12) questions, and no ability loses a complete answer-bearing message. An equal-share excerpt of every hit, including the best, was measured and rejected: it dropped single-session-assistant answer visibility from 16 to 1.

- Memory-benchmark protocol v2 delivers the question date as message metadata and persisted answer time across all public-suite arms and retry branches (plan 188.8 Task 2). Solver date rendering runs after fabric query construction; passive queries contain only question text, and relative periods resolve against question time. Protocol fingerprints reject v1 checkpoints. Offline delivery diagnostics mirror v2 while the committed v1 baseline stays unchanged. Harness-only; no kernel or product behavior changed.

- Memory-benchmark delivery baseline and held-out split are frozen (plan 188.8 Task 1). The diagnosed 140 confirmatory ids become development data for this plan; `heldout-v2.json` freezes 100 disjoint ids using the prescribed SHA-256 draw, with preference and abstention development-only. The offline diagnostic now measures all selected questions, query contamination, fine evidence ranks, all-needed@k, actual budget visibility and full answer-message visibility without publishing text. Separate date-free ranking ablations identify the original investigation's omitted coverage floor. Harness/evidence only; no product behavior changed.

- Background waits are event-driven instead of polled (plan 189 follow-up). `ProcessSession` gains `onOutput(listener)` — published on every appended chunk and on the terminal transition, unsubscribed by the returned function — so `shell({ background: true })` returns as soon as the first output arrives rather than always paying the one-second first-output window, and `shell_output({ waitSeconds })` wakes on the publication instead of polling every 50 ms. One `shell_output` call also drains everything that accumulated since the previous call (whole chunks, bounded at twice the tail cap) instead of returning only the first 50 KB chunk. Caps, ownership, and the schema are unchanged.

- `worktree_changed` events come from the `workspace` service (plan 189 follow-up). `createWorkspaceService({ onEvent })` now emits the frozen coding lifecycle event `worktree_changed` — `{ action: "add", path }` once `worktree.create` succeeds and `{ action: "remove", path }` after the handle's `dispose()` removed one — through `createCodingLifecycleEmitter`, so the event keeps the emitter's path/byte caps. The kind is unchanged and needs no new export: worktree movement now has a producer at the layer that still creates worktrees, so the ACP lifecycle mapper's `worktree_changed` projection (`agent_message_chunk` keyed `prism:worktree:<path>`) is reachable again after plan 189 removed the `git_worktree` tool. Like every coding lifecycle event it is host-wired: the package registers no listener and the plugin passes no sink. `createCodingLifecycleEmitter` now swallows a listener that throws, so telemetry can never fail the operation that produced it (the worktree or file is already changed by then); the other listeners still receive the event.

- `memory-fabric` consolidation aligns the extractor schema with its parser and adds one bounded repair (plan 188.7 Task 5). With no shown task segment, `episode` is no longer offered by the `record_notes` kind enum; a path-scoped note is schema-constrained to require `applies_to`, while the runtime parser remains the final authority for path globs, shown segment ids and all other conditional rules. A shape-only rejection (invalid answer/note shape, kind, scope, path globs, key, evidence shape, segment, or a missing/duplicate tool call) may trigger exactly one extra extractor call carrying a safe `reasonCode` and the same shown ids; the repair prompt cannot loosen trust, provenance or scope. Invented evidence/contradicting ids, unknown segments, trust/secret rejections, unsafe scope escalation, aborts and worker limits never repair. A valid pass still costs one call, an empty/disabled pass costs zero, and the report now exposes actual `modelCalls` plus `correctionCalls` (optional, persisted state remains readable); failures carry `reasonCode`, `modelCalls` and `correctionCalls` without raw model text. Widening and write ordering are unchanged: nothing is retired or written until extraction succeeds. The lifecycle harness records safe extraction reasons and correction/model counts in `consolidation` and failure diagnostics.

- Memory-benchmark combined arms and retries are faithful and diagnosable (plan 188.7 Task 6). `om+fabric` now ingests the same pre-question corpus into both OM's dated session history and its disposable fabric store instead of leaving fabric empty, and every fabric-bearing arm gets an OS scratch root rather than a cwd/default-store fallback. Ingestion drops sessions dated after the question and never reads oracle fields. A truncated answer is retried at most twice from the pre-answer branch (or a fresh session when there is no history), never from the session that holds partial text; coding or mutating attempts are not replayed at all. Judge retries are one logical request with a fresh one-shot session per provider attempt, and only malformed/truncated output is retryable — auth, permanent, usage/budget and run-limit failures keep their original code. `calls.judge` counts logical requests and `calls.judgeAttempts` counts attempts; every retry re-checks campaign admission. Reports record the protocol (`maxTurns`, retry limits), bounded tool-name traces, visible channels, retry counts and proposed write scope/kind/resource-id codes, never note text. The shared eight-turn agentic cap is retained and now explicit rather than silently raised; a changed limit is a new arm. Harness-only; no SDK surface changed.

- `memory-fabric` widening is value-exact and fail-closed (plan 188.7 Task 4). A shared `key` no longer implies agreement: every readable note for one `kind|key` subject must carry the same normalized content (numbers and negation preserved), so one differing value blocks the subject and a third agreeing project cannot bridge it. Widening requires readable evidence in each project and distinct witness origins (copies citing the same `sourceEntryIds` count once), and text naming a participating resource stays project-bound. Keyless matching uses exact normalized text instead of Dice; `duplicateDice` remains accepted for source compatibility and is ignored. Path widening needs one identical value and one single-subtree glob per note, so a note listing two globs is one witness rather than two regions. The wider note carries the survivor's temporal fields, tags and combined evidence lineage, and project/path copies are archived only after the wider note is persisted at the wider level with equal content; a failed write leaves every local note active and a retry reconciles once. Bucket construction is linear (10k same-key notes grouped in ~70ms, down from ~62s). Separately, `memory.insert` from a project or user scope can no longer write `scope: "agent"`; it fails with `agent_scope_requires_widening` instead of bypassing replication into the shared agent store. Trusted host writes and the normal widening path are unchanged.

- `memory-fabric` lifecycle capacity eviction is deterministic: equal-strength notes tie-break on text and only then on id, and merge/episode member sorts use the same comparator, so identical state always evicts the same notes. A benchmark replay of the notes:2 capacity arm now makes zero extractor calls on the warm pass and reproduces its metrics (regression test in `scripts/memory-bench/lifecycle/lifecycle.test.mjs`).

- Language diagnostics through an opt-in `diagnostics` tool, and the dead patch-review module removed (plan 189 Task 10). A new `language.servers` config field (server name → `{ command, args?, languages, env? }`, where `languages` are LSP language ids) registers `diagnostics({ path })`, which refreshes the document and returns the server's diagnostics as `path:line:col severity message` (1-based, capped at 500 per file and 4 KiB per message); without `language.servers` no tool is registered and no server starts, and a configured server starts only on the first call that needs its language. A server that cannot start is a tool error naming it plus a one-time `status.note("warn", …)`, never a failed run, and servers stop when the last open session closes (so `agent.dispose()` leaves none running). The diagnostics path goes through `PATH_ARG_KEYS`, so hard-deny containment covers it exactly like `read`. Removed: the unregistered patch-review module (`createCodingPatchReviewManifest`, `assertCodingPatchAccepted`, `CodingPatchReviewError`, `resolveCodingReviewLimits`, and their types) and the four limits constants only it used (`DEFAULT_/HARD_MAX_REVIEW_REVISIONS`, `DEFAULT_/HARD_MAX_REVIEW_MANIFEST_BYTES`); `lifecycle.ts` and `coding-checkpoint.ts` stay — shipped tools type-import the former and `goal-verify.ts` uses the latter. New exports: `createDiagnosticsTool`, `DiagnosticsToolHandle`, `DiagnosticsToolOptions`, `ToolsCodingLanguageConfig`, `ToolsCodingLanguageServerConfig`, and `languageIdForPath`.

- Background processes through `shell` (plan 189 Task 9). `shell` gains an optional `background: true`, which starts the command on the process-session library and returns at once with `Started process <id>` plus the first output that arrived within one second; the new `shell_output({ id, waitSeconds?, kill? })` tool reads the output produced since the previous call (tail-capped like `shell`), reports whether the process is still running, and returns its exit code once it ended. `waitSeconds` (default 0, max 60) waits for new output or exit and `kill: true` ends the process. Every process is owned by the calling session, so another session's id is an error, `shell.background.maxProcesses` (default 4, hard 16) caps live processes per session, and `session.closed` — also emitted for every open session by `agent.dispose()` — kills them; a capability the backend lacks (the native `unshare` sandbox has no `startProcess`) keeps `background` out of the schema and does not register `shell_output`. The hard-deny `commandRules` and built-in denies apply to a background command exactly as to a foreground one, the environment allow-list and `commandPrefix`/`spawnHook` are inherited, and nothing in the process library is constructed until the first background call. New exports: `createBackgroundProcesses`, `createShellOutputTool`, `BackgroundProcesses`/`BackgroundProcessOptions`/`BackgroundRead`/`BackgroundStarted`/`BackgroundStartInput`, `ShellOutputToolOptions`, and the `DEFAULT_MAX_BACKGROUND_PROCESSES`/`HARD_MAX_BACKGROUND_PROCESSES`/`BACKGROUND_FIRST_OUTPUT_MS`/`HARD_MAX_BACKGROUND_WAIT_SECONDS` constants. The default set is 13 tools.

- Fewer tools, one job each (plan 189 Task 8, breaking). `repo_list`, `git_branch`, `git_worktree`, and `git_apply` are removed with `createRepoListTool`/`ListToolOptions`, `createGitBranchTool`, `createGitWorktreeTool`, `createGitApplyTool`, and the `classifyGit{Branch,Worktree,Apply}Effect` helpers; `createGitTools` now returns `git_status`, `git_diff`, `git_commit`, `git_pr_handoff`. Directory listing is the `glob` walk plus the repository `list` operation, worktrees are the `workspace` service's job, and `git branch`/`git apply`/`git worktree` are `shell` calls. `delete` and `move` are registered only when `shell` is not (`shell.enabled: false`, or a sandbox backend that withheld it), so the default set is 12 tools instead of 18; `readOnly` stays `read`/`grep`/`glob`. New `ToolsOptions.shellAvailable` selects the shell-less set, `GitToolsOptions.onEvent` is gone (the `workspace` service is the `worktree_changed` producer), and `hard-deny`/`coding.targets` follow the smaller `PATH_ARG_KEYS` automatically. Default coding+git descriptions and schemas total 8,548 chars (2,137 estimated tokens) over 10 tools.

- Schemas describe only what the model controls (plan 189 Task 7). `write` and `edit` carry `force` in their schema — and any mention of the read-before-write guard in their description — only when the host created them with `requireReadBeforeWrite`. Without the guard the model cannot pass `force` (schema validation rejects it as an unknown key) and is not told about a guard it does not have. `ask_user_decision` options take `pros`/`cons` as optional 0–3 strings each (`minItems: 0`, `maxItems: 3`) instead of exactly three, `AskUserDecisionOption.pros`/`.cons` are `readonly string[]`, and the description no longer says "MUST include exactly 3"; a fourth bullet is still rejected, and hosts render whatever is given. The guard's behavior when on is unchanged.

- `edit` matches exactly and reports the changed lines (plan 189 Task 6). **Breaking.** Fuzzy matching is gone: `normalizeForFuzzyMatch`, `fuzzyFindText`, `applyReplacementsPreservingUnchangedLines`, `AppliedEditsResult.usedFuzzyMatch` and `EditToolDetails.fuzzy` are removed, and an `oldText` that differs from the file (trailing whitespace, smart quotes, en-dash, NBSP) now fails with the `Nearby` hint instead of editing a nearby region. Success no longer says `Successfully replaced N block(s) in …` but `Edited <path>: N block(s).` followed by each changed region in `read`'s `N\ttext` format with two lines of context, capped at 40 lines with a `(… N more changed lines)` note, so the model can check its own edit without a second `read`. CRLF/BOM handling is unchanged. New export `generateChangedLinePreview`; `applyEditsToNormalizedContent` returns `{ baseContent, newContent }`. Actions: regenerate the `@arnilo/prism-tools/coding` compat baseline.

- `glob` expands braces by default (plan 189 Task 5). `{ts,tsx}` works on the first call, so a model that writes `**/*.{ts,tsx}` no longer loses a turn; the bounds are unchanged (128 alternatives, 4096 expanded bytes, unbalanced/nested/empty braces still error). The `braceExpansion` argument is gone from the schema — a host that wants braces rejected sets `GlobToolOptions.braceExpansion: false`, which also says so in the error. The same expansion now applies wherever a glob filters paths: the `grep` `glob` argument, the sandbox backend's `glob`, and its `grep` filter, through the new `compileGlob(pattern, braceExpansion?)` export. Actions: regenerate the `@arnilo/prism-tools/coding` compat baseline.

- `repo_search` becomes `grep`: regex by default, in a terminate-able worker (plan 189 Task 4). **Breaking.** The tool is renamed, `query` becomes `pattern`, `caseSensitive` now defaults to `true`, `context` defaults to `0`, and content output is ripgrep-style — `path:line:text` for a match, `path-line-text` for context, `--` between non-adjacent groups, and each line printed once even when two context windows overlap. A pattern is a JavaScript regex unless `literal: true`; regex matching runs in a Worker that the main thread terminates when the search time limit elapses, returning the matches so far with `truncatedBy: "time"`, so a catastrophic pattern (`(a+)+$` over a 40 KB line) can no longer block the event loop — plan 001's literal-only refusal is retired. A new `glob` argument filters files by path (`"src/**/*.ts"`), `mode` appears in the schema only when the host enabled an indexed mode, and `createRepoSearchTool` becomes `createGrepTool` (`SearchToolOptions` → `GrepToolOptions`, `ToolsOptions.search` kept as a deprecated alias for `grep`). The matcher seam is new: `RepositorySearchRequest.matcher` (a `RepositoryFileMatcher`) plus `RepositorySearchRequest.glob`, and `matchLines`/`literalMatcher` are exported so the worker and literal mode share one implementation; the sandbox backend honors both, so every backend keeps working. `PATH_ARG_KEYS`/`CODING_TOOL_NAMES` carry `grep`, so approval and the hard-deny hook follow the rename with no rule change. Actions: regenerate the `@arnilo/prism-tools/coding` compat baseline and rebaseline the kernel budgets.

- `read` numbers its lines and `edit` refuses a numbered `oldText` (plan 189 Task 3): every text line `read` returns now starts with its 1-based number and a tab (`12\treturn 1;`), so a model can cite `file:line` and pick an offset; the prefix is not part of the file and the 2000-line / 50 KB page caps count the numbered text, so a page never exceeds them. The trailing newline of a file no longer prints a dangling empty numbered line, `findText` pages start at the real line number, image reads are unchanged, and both tool descriptions say the prefix is not file content. `edit` fails with `oldText includes read line-number prefixes ("12\t"); remove them` when every line of an unmatched `oldText` is numbered, and never strips prefixes silently, so a file line that really starts with digits and a tab still edits correctly. The line numbering is applied in `createReadTool.execute` after `ReadOperations.readText` returns, so the native, sandbox, and ACP backends all produce it.

- Tool results reach the model as text (plan 189 Task 2): `serializeToolResultJson` becomes `serializeToolResultText`, so a text result is sent byte-for-byte instead of JSON-escaped — the model no longer reads `"line \"one\"\n\tline two"` and can copy `edit.oldText` directly, on both the OpenAI Chat and Anthropic Messages codecs. A structured `ToolResult.value` stays JSON, an error is sent as its `message` (with `is_error: true` on Anthropic Messages), and `EMPTY_TOOL_RESULT_TEXT` is unchanged. Images in a tool result now survive: Anthropic Messages carries them as `image` blocks inside `tool_result.content`, OpenAI Chat Completions gets them in one follow-up `user` message after the round's contiguous tool replies (`Images returned by <tool> (<toolCallId>):`), and a model without image input receives `[image omitted: the model does not accept images]` as text on both routes. `assertMessagesSupportModelCapabilities` stops failing the request for an image inside a tool message (other messages still fail closed). New export `OMITTED_TOOL_RESULT_IMAGE_NOTE`; `serializeToolResultJson` is retired. Actions: regenerate the provider-kit compat baseline and rebaseline the kernel budgets.

- Memory-benchmark metrics are versioned and comparisons match their design (plan 188.7 Task 3). `scripts/memory-bench/metrics.mjs` defines metric version 2 with a definition, unit and direction per metric; `compare` now uses each row's raw `value` (the historical `correct` flag is inverted for risk metrics), reports both raw means plus a direction-adjusted `improvementDelta`, labels the cluster unit/count, and refuses reports with a different `metricVersion`, `protocolHash` or dataset. A group is `confirmatory` only for two completed usage-complete arms with at least 8 independent clusters, so historical or partial reports stay descriptive. Power is simulated with the same paired cluster sign-flip test the comparison uses; the independent-arm approximation remains as a historical function. Lifecycle clusters are personas, not persona×project (the three project stores share one user/agent store), leakage is output use with `foreignContextExposure` reported separately, and poison splits into stored/visible/reused channels. Public retrieval evidence separates coarse `answerSessionHitAtK` from round coverage, all-required coverage and fine-gold recall/precision; fine gold is null when unavailable, never inferred. Attribution stores only tool/channel/scope/kind and key/text/target hashes. LongMemEval questions cluster by shared haystack session set, LoCoMo by conversation and MemoryAgentBench-CR by source context. Injection calibration keeps its descriptive floor but adds `minBinN`, `eligibleFloor` and `defaultEligible`, so sparse or unaudited bins cannot become defaults. Replay evidence records `exercised`/`insensitive`/`unexercised`/`policy` per arm. Harness-only; no SDK surface changed.

- Retrieval losses are staged and passive injection keeps its best hit (plan 188.7 Task 7). `memory-fabric` passive injection now always shows the best matching note even when that line alone exceeds `inject.budgetTokens`; later notes must still fit, matching recall's existing scope-reserve contract, so an oversized top note can no longer hide the only useful memory. The benchmark gains `scripts/memory-bench/retrieval-diagnostics.mjs`, which replays the lexical fabric through the real package and writes ids/ranks/counts with a stage per failed question (`candidate_gap`, `selection_gap`, `fine_selection_gap`, `answer_gap`) and never source or answer text; the frozen development report attributes 21 answer-gap, 13 fine-selection-gap and 13 selection-gap failures. `extractEvidence` keeps message-level gold inside an answer session instead of losing it to the coarse session label. Lifecycle reports now expose `cost` with extractor input separated from solver/worker input plus extractor, correction, worker, embedding and retry counts, so an unchanged tail is not guessed from totals. Harness/package only; no retrieval default, model or embedding backend changed.

- Memory-benchmark spend is journaled per paid attempt (plan 188.7 Task 2). `scripts/memory-bench/journal.mjs` makes one hash-checked journal the source of truth: every attempt id persists its full allocation (cap plus one-question overshoot) before the child starts, so a renamed report, a crash before the first checkpoint, a failed setup or a killed child keeps its reservation instead of vanishing from a filename glob; only a matched report reconciles it, once, at confirmed wire usage. Missing usage keeps the reservation and flags `usageComplete: false`. A single lock file (pid-checked with `EPERM`/`ESRCH`, never `pgrep`) refuses a second writer, SIGINT/SIGTERM stops owned children, and a live `run.mjs` or lifecycle suite refuses to start without `PRISM_MEMORY_BENCH_JOURNAL` + `PRISM_MEMORY_BENCH_ATTEMPT` matching `--output`; every provider request (retries and in-flight calls included) passes a 250k admission check. `--resume` reuses scored question ids only when `protocolHash` (suite/subset/ability/config/provider/model/judge/dataset revision/config/ablations/source revision) matches, and lifecycle arms deliberately start a new charged attempt instead of resuming an unvalidated store. `campaign.mjs --manifest` is dry-run only and never touches the journal; the existing evidence directory imports once for historical accounting, so the current 90M ceiling fails closed until Task 8 records a new cap. Harness-only; no SDK surface changed.

- Memory benchmarks: licence-gated smokes, sandboxed Vibe grading, ablations, per-hit coverage and provisional calibration (188.6 Tasks 5–6). DreamBench needs hidden oracles.

- Added consented, redacted memory ledger export, seeded labelled synthetic sessions, and an offline lifecycle replay runner with cached extraction and component metrics (`scripts/memory-bench/lifecycle`; plan 188.6 Task 2).

- `@arnilo/prism-memory-observational` ledger fold no longer throws on a sourceless entry: the reopen-recovery fold maps a branch's entries through `recordData`, which yields a bare `undefined` for an entry with no `data` (any `message` entry), and `isSessionEntry` then read `.kind` off it — so a session reopened onto a compaction entry crashed `context.build` instead of rebuilding the ledger. It now skips anything that is not an object, as its contract already stated. Found by repairing `examples/autonomous-coding-loop.ts`, which also needed `dropper.targetTokens >= reflector.observationTokens` in its package config.

- Memory delivery tiers, documented and proven (plan 188.5 Task 4): `examples/memory-lifecycle.ts` now drives all three tiers through a real agent — the pinned core in the prompt prefix, one path cue in the tail after a `read` under a path note's glob, and a `memory:share` that writes a team note after a scripted `host.ui` confirm — and prints one more tally field (`delivery: { prefixes: 10, cues: 1, shares: 1 }`). A new `packages/memory/fabric/src/__tests__/prefix-stability.test.ts` asserts the plan 184 cache-parity claim for the fabric's own blocks: ten turns without a memory write send byte-identical prefix blocks, with the directory before the core and no tail block. The fabric README's Delivery tiers section states the per-turn cost of each tier and names the test. No source change, no new export.

- Memory delivery tier 3 — the team store (plan 188.5 Task 3): `team: { dir: ".prism/memory" }` turns the checked-in tree at `<project root>/<team.dir>` into a markdown memory store under the fixed tenant/resource `team` / `project`, read as scope `team`. It joins union recall and injection with prior 0.075 (between `project` 0.10 and `agent` 0.05), and key shadowing is now `path` > `project` > `team` > `agent` > `user`, so a person's private exception still wins. Its derived index lives at `<tools.root>/.team-index/<project id8>.sqlite`, so the repository holds notes only, and the file-mirror reconcile never indexes it. The directory is untrusted input: on load every note is trust scanned, and a flagged note is rewritten as a `candidate` with its `flags`, never injected, reported by `memory:status` and warned about once at setup. Tools never write it — `memory.insert` refuses `scope: "team"` — and the new `memory:share { id }` moves one verified project note (`fact`, `preference`, `procedure`) into it after showing the note and the target path through `host.ui`, writing `generated.by: human`, `sources` naming the private note, and a `shared-to-team` log line. A candidate, an archived or flagged note, `write: "off"`, a declined prompt, or a host with no UI refuses with a typed reason and writes nothing; the commit stays the person's. New config `team: false | { dir, write }` (off by default) and `scopes.priors.team`; new exports `createFabricTeam`, `resolveFabricTeamOptions`, `MemoryFabricTeamConfig`, `ResolvedFabricTeamOptions`, `FabricTeam`, `FabricTeamCensus`, `FabricTeamShareResult`, `TEAM_TENANT`, `TEAM_RESOURCE`, plus `CreateMemoryFabricOptions.readOnlyScopes`. Actions: regenerate the memory-core and memory-fabric compat baselines.

- Memory delivery tier 2 — path cues (plan 188.5 Task 2): when a file tool reads or writes a file, the project's path-scoped notes (`applies_to` globs) that match it are shown once in the session, in the tail after the topic-injected block, one `- [id] path <kind> (for <glob>) <text>` line each. Matching is a glob check over an index bucketed by each glob's first literal segment, built from one `scanMetadata` pass cached on the directory's write counter, so a turn with no new path does no work; there is no model call. A verified note wins, and a candidate shows only where no verified note covers the path, marked `(candidate)`. At most `cues.maxPerTurn` (2) notes per turn within `cues.budgetTokens` (300, hard 1000), ordered by storage strength then retrieval strength. Cross-source dedup drops a note already in the core, already injected by topic, already cued, or whose text is verbatim in a visible file read; injected cues join the citation tracker, so citing one counts as a use, and the injected set lives in session state, so a restore never shows a cue again. New config `cues: false | { budgetTokens, maxPerTurn }` (default on); `cues: false` makes no index query and adds no lines. Actions: regenerate the memory-fabric compat baseline.

- Memory delivery tier 1 — the pinned core (plan 188.5 Task 1): the fabric now renders a small `Core memory` prefix block (`metadata.source: "fabric-core"`) after the working-memory and directory blocks. It holds verified `preference`, `fact`, and `procedure` notes ordered by storage strength S with pinned notes first, one `[id] text` line each, user-scope notes first. Selection is deterministic (ties by id), the `core.maxTokens` budget (default 600, hard 1000) splits between user and project with unused budget flowing to the other scope, and the block is cached per memory instance on the directory's write counter — so a turn with no relevant write sends byte-identical bytes and the provider cache survives. Only verified, unflagged, unarchived, still-readable notes enter; the text passes the redactor and the block stays in the untrusted envelope. No model call: one `scanMetadata` page per scope plus one bounded read for the text that fits. With the markdown store each scope's slice is also written to `<scope>/core.md` as a derived file, and a manual edit is overwritten with a `log.md` line pointing at `pinned: true`. New config `core: false | { maxTokens, scopes }` (default on) and exports `createFabricCore`, `resolveFabricCoreOptions`, `HARD_CORE_TOKENS`, `DEFAULT_CORE_OPTIONS`, and the core types. Actions: regenerate the memory-fabric compat baseline.

- Recall kinds accept `["all"]` as shorthand for every note kind (`working`, `episode`, `fact`, `preference`, `procedure`, `file`), so recalling an archived procedure or episode is one call — `{ kinds: ["all"], includeArchived: true }` — instead of a hand-written kind list plus the archive flag. The same parser serves `fabric.recall` and the `memory.recall` tool.

- `memory:lifecycle`: a per-note lifecycle inspection command (plan 188.4 Further Action 4). One line each for S, R, the use-log size, and the archive/widening gate that refused the note (`archive=no(retrieval,grace)`, `widen=no(harmful)`), filtered by `id`, `key`, `includeArchived`, and `limit`. It reuses `shouldArchive`, `storageStrength`, and `retrievalStrength`, so the printed verdict cannot disagree with `memory:maintain`. New exports `inspectNote`, `describeNote`, and the `LifecycleNoteView`/`LifecycleDecisionView`/`InspectNoteInput` types.

- `memory-fabric` maintenance can now reach projects the host does not bind (plan 188.4 Further Action 2): `createMemoryFabric` accepts `openScope: ({ level, tenantId, resourceId }) => Memory | undefined`, which the widening pass calls for each project it discovers through `listResources` but has no `Memory` for. A returned memory gives that project readable text (so its note can be the wider note) and a lifecycle (so its copy archives with `widenedTo`); `undefined` keeps it metadata-only, and a resolver that throws or answers for a different resource is treated as "not opened". Archiving and capacity still run only for bound scopes. New export `MemoryFabricScopeOpenInput`. Actions: none — with no resolver, maintenance behaves as before.

- Headless `command` scheduler targets: `createScheduler` now accepts `{ kind: "command", agent, command, input }`, which runs one registered package command on the schedule session without a model turn — so `memory:maintain` (plan 188.4) can be scheduled nightly instead of needing a host loop, and the fabric README's recipe is now a real scheduler job. The firing status is taken from the command result (`status`, else `ok: false` ⇒ `failed`, else `completed`), a throw records `failed` without persisting the error or the result, and an unregistered command fails closed with the new `unknown_command` code. Actions: none — existing targets are unchanged.

- Memory lifecycle step 7 — the end-to-end example (plan 188.4 Task 7): `examples/memory-lifecycle.ts` runs one note's whole life with no model and no network — a user preference written in project A and cited in B, a procedure verified in both projects that `maintain()` widens to an `agent` candidate, a project exception that shadows the preference inside A, an unused episode archived by the two strengths and revived by citing it after an archive search, and a capacity budget that folds duplicate notes then evicts the weakest survivor. It asserts its own expectations and prints a single JSON tally. Added to the example gate and the e2e surface manifest. Actions: none.

- Memory lifecycle step 6 — scope widening and narrowing, and `maintain()` (plan 188.4 Task 6): in a full maintenance pass, a `fact` or `procedure` that is **verified** in at least `lifecycle.widen.minProjects` (2, never fewer) distinct project resources now widens to an `agent`-scope **candidate** with the project notes as evidence, matching on an equal `key` or term-set Dice at or above `merge.duplicateDice`; the project copies archive with `widenedTo` and the wider note has to earn verification through use in the agent scope, so one project can never install agent-wide knowledge by itself. Path notes with an equal `key` covering `widen.minSiblings` (2) sibling subtrees widen to their project. A `user` or `agent` note that `widen.contradictionsToSupersede` (2) projects contradict is superseded with `validTo`, status `retired`, and the audit verb `superseded-by-contradiction`; the project exceptions stay valid. `fabric.maintain({ scope?, signal })` and the new `memory:maintain` command run the whole pass in order — archive, capacity, widening, narrowing — and report `archived`, `merged`, `episodesMerged`, `evicted`, `widened`, `superseded`, and `overBudget`; `fabric.consolidate()` ends every pass with the scoped (archive + capacity only) form for the session's project scope. Everything is metadata-only: no model call unless `merge.episodes` is on. New exports: `lifecycle/widen.ts` (`crossProjectGroups`, `siblingGroups`, `narrowingCandidates`, `mergedKeywords`, `DEFAULT_WIDEN_CONFIG`, the `MIN`/`MAX_WIDEN_*` bounds), `ResolvedLifecycleConfig.widen`, `MemoryNoteMetadata.widenedTo`, and `FabricLifecycle.supersede`. Actions: none required — `lifecycle.widen` is optional and defaults to the values above.

- Memory lifecycle step 5 — capacity budgets and merging (plan 188.4 Task 5): `fabric.maintain()` now measures each active scope against `lifecycle.capacity` (notes and estimated tokens; defaults 200/20k for `user` and `agent`, 500/60k for `project`, and a `path` note spends the project budget) and acts in order, stopping as soon as the scope is inside it. First **duplicate merge** (no model): notes of one kind in one scope with an equal `key`, or term-set Dice at or above `lifecycle.merge.duplicateDice` (0.8) over the same globs, fold into the note with the higher `S`, which keeps its id, text, and status and absorbs keywords, tags, links, evidence, the promotion stamp, and the lifecycle counters; the others archive with `mergedInto` and the audit verb `merged`. Episodes are never duplicate-merged. Second **episode merge**, only when `lifecycle.merge.episodes` is set (off until plan 188.6): `minCluster` (5) or more active episodes of one project sharing a `key` or tag become one `fact`/`procedure` candidate with `generated.by: promotion` and the episodes as evidence, one model call per cluster with the consolidator's fail-closed rules, and the episodes archive with `mergedInto`. Third **eviction**: the lowest `S·exp(R)` notes archive with the verb `evicted`, never a pinned note and never one at or above `archive.storageBelow`. A scope whose protected notes alone exceed the budget archives nothing more: it is reported in `maintain()`'s new `overBudget` list and raises one status warning with the scope and counts. `MemoryFabricMaintenanceReport` gained `episodesMerged` and `overBudget`; note metadata gained `mergedInto`; `createMemoryFabric` accepts `notify` for maintenance warnings. Merging never crosses a scope. Actions: none required — `lifecycle.capacity` and `lifecycle.merge` are optional, and episode merging is off by default.

- Memory lifecycle step 4 — archive, revival, and archive search (plan 188.4 Task 4): a note now leaves the active index only when both strengths are low (`R < lifecycle.archive.retrievalBelow`, `S < lifecycle.archive.storageBelow`) and it is past `lifecycle.archive.graceSessions` scope sessions since it was written, unless it is pinned or under legal hold. `shouldArchive` is the one predicate (`fabric/src/lifecycle/archive.ts`) and `fabric.maintain()` is the only pass that applies it, one `scanMetadata` pass per scope. Archiving never sets `validTo` — supersession and expiry still own that field — and it remembers the status to restore. `memory.recall { includeArchived: true }` searches active and archived notes; without the flag archived notes are searched only when the best active hit is weak, and at most three are appended after the active hits marked `[archived]`. Passive injection never searches or shows archives. A citation of an archived note revives it: the previous status returns, the use resets `R`, `use_sessions` raises `S`, and the audit line reads `revived`. Notes can be pinned (`pinned: true`, `fabric.pin(id)`, `memory.replace { id, pin }`), which exempts them from archive and eviction. **Retired:** calendar-day archiving — `fabric.archiveStale`, `isStaleCandidate`, `promote.archiveAfterDays` and the scoped `policy.decay.candidateArchiveDays` knob; the scoped `gcPass()` now proposes what the lifecycle rule selects and still only proposes. Actions: regenerate the memory-fabric compat baseline; a config that still sets `promote.archiveAfterDays` or `policy.decay` fails validation.

- Memory lifecycle step 3 — two strengths and the ranking prior (plan 188.4 Task 3): every note now has a storage strength S and a retrieval strength R. `S = a·explicit + b·log(1+useSessions) + c·helpful − d·harmful + e·outcome` accumulates evidence of value and only harmful evidence lowers it; `R = ln Σ (clock − s + 1)^(−d)` is ACT-R base-level activation over the use log, where the clock is the scope-session ordinal, so a project left alone does not decay while other projects run (D7). Per-kind decay (episode 0.8, fact/procedure 0.4, preference 0.2) and the storage weights are config; uses older than the 32-event log are approximated with Petrov (2006) from `useSessions`. `memory.recall` adds the two bounded priors `w_R·σ(R) + w_S·σ(S)` (0.05 each) after fusion and the scope prior, so a used note outranks an equally relevant neglected one while relevance keeps the final word. The fibre's notes now carry `importance` derived from S (`noteImportance`, `[0,1)`) instead of falling back to core's neutral 0.5 (D14). **Retired:** `scoreScopedHit` and the exponential calendar-time decay multiplied into relevance (D8), together with `scoped.policy.decay.tauDays`; the scoped read policy keeps the floor, topK, and abstention and no longer rescales scores. Actions: regenerate the memory-fabric compat baseline.

- Memory lifecycle step 2 — the scope clock and the lifecycle scan (plan 188.4 Task 2): three optional `VectorStore` members now let the fabric's maintenance pass read a project without reading its text. `scanMetadata({ scope, limit, cursor? })` pages `{ id, metadata, createdAt, lastAccessedAt }` over every thread of one resource, ordered by `(threadId, id)` and filtered like an export (visible consent, no invalidation or legal hold); `listResources({ tenantId, prefix? })` lists a tenant's resource ids for scope widening; and `scopeCounter.get/increment({ tenantId, resourceId, name: "sessions" })` is one atomic counter per resource. The fabric ticks each active scope's clock once per session on the first `context.build`, storing the counted resources in session state so a restore never double counts; decay and archival can then measure sessions worked instead of calendar days (D7). `MemoryFabric.maintain({ signal })` lands with the capability gate: a store without `scanMetadata` fails closed with `MemoryValidationError("maintenance requires scanMetadata")` while recall and writes keep working. SQLite moves to schema v3 — the index mirrors `status`, `storage` (`importance`) and `last_use_ordinal` as columns, a v2 index migrates in place with a JSON backfill, an unknown version still refuses, and the counter lives in `memory_scope_counter`; the markdown store keeps the counter in `<resource>/.state.json` (an index cannot rebuild it); Postgres adds `<table>_scope_counter` with an idempotent `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`. The shared conformance suite checks all four stores for each member it declares. Actions: regenerate the memory-core and memory-fabric compat baselines.

- One note lifecycle (plan 188.4 Task 1): status, use events, evidence, and reconsolidation now live in `packages/memory/fabric/src/lifecycle/` and the scoped ledger's second lifecycle is gone. A use is a citation of an id the session was shown, recorded once per session per note with its scope-session ordinal; `retrievals` counts returns for diagnostics only and never moves status. Outcome-tagged turns record `helpful`, and a user correction citing a note records `harmful`, sets `reconsolidate: true`, and returns a verified note to `candidate`. A candidate verifies after `lifecycle.verifyAfterSessions` (default 2) distinct sessions with no harmful evidence, or by an explicit confirmation. `promote.verifyAfterUses`, `lastUseKey`, the scoped ledger's `uses`/`status`/`lastUsedAt`/`promotedAt` rows, `touchScopedLedger`, `runScopedPromotionPass`, and `policy.promotionPass()` are retired; the ledger keeps only pending approvals and write statistics, and an older ledger file is read with its lifecycle rows ignored and reported once. The scoped reader no longer records a use on retrieval, and `scopedMemoryHealth(memory, ledgerFile)` reads the unified lifecycle. `MemoryNote.uses` (a count) becomes `useSessions`, and `uses` is the use log; `MemoryFabric.recordUse` becomes `recordUses(sessionKey, ids, { clock, verifyAfterSessions })`. Actions: regenerate the memory-fabric compat baseline; grep the retired names (clean).

- Memory session consolidation, step 5 — triggers, command and status (plan 188.3 Task 5): the pass runs from `turn.completed` (after `promote.minTurnsBetween` turns, only when observational memory recorded a new reflection, supersession or task-state decision after the cursor), from the new `memory:consolidate` command, and once from `session.opened` with `restored: true`; no timers. One pass per session runs at a time (a concurrent trigger is dropped), and the cursor advances only after every write succeeds, so a failed pass keeps the material for the next trigger and fails only its background task. `fabric.consolidate(session, { signal, cursor })` is the host API for a pass before `session.close()`, `memory:status` reports the last pass's counts and rejections by reason (`score`, `ephemeral`, `derivable`, `trust`, `secret`), and `promote` is reshaped to `false | { model, minScore, weights, maxCandidates, minTurnsBetween, derivableFrom, verifyAfterUses, archiveAfterDays }` — off by default, so no model call. Superseded reflections are handled by the pass, and a `reconsolidate` note is re-extracted in place or retired.

- Memory session consolidation, step 4 — the write path (plan 188.3 Task 4): accepted notes are written through the same `remember` path as agent writes with `status: candidate`, promotion provenance (`generated.by: "promotion"`), `sourceEntryIds`, and a `promotion: { score, signals, pass }` stamp. A wider-scope contradiction becomes a narrower-scope exception with the same `key` and `overrides`, which makes plan 188.2's key shadowing hide the wider note in that project only, while the wider note records the project id in `contradictedIn`; same-scope contradictions stay linked `contradicts` and route to the decider's `SUPERSEDE` when enabled. Reconsolidating notes are rewritten in place with the same id and correction lineage or retired with `validTo`, a verified note is never demoted, and flagged or archived notes are never rewritten. Per-reflection promotion is retired: `runFabricPromotion`, `promote.observational`, and the per-turn reflection trigger are removed, and `promote` now only configures the candidate lifecycle.

- Memory session consolidation, step 3 — scoring and the promotion gate (plan 188.3 Task 3): `scorePromotion(note, context)` returns `{ score, signals, accepted, reason }` from a transparent linear score (`explicit + correction + outcome·0.5 + recurrence·0.5 + generality·0.5 − ephemerality − derivability − duplicate·0.5`), with weights as exported data and a provisional `minScore` of 0.5 until plan 188.6 calibrates them. Explicit and correction flags are cross-checked against the evidence's candidate sources, recurrence is `log2(1 + distinct sessions)` normalised to one at four sessions, ephemerality rises to one for line references, temp paths, commit hashes, branch names and outlived dates, derivability covers the extractor flag and ≥ 0.9 lexical coverage by a `derivableFrom` file, and duplicate is the highest Dice with a same-kind note in the target scope. Each rejection carries one reason (`ephemeral`, `derivable`, `score`), and an explicit user statement is never rejected for score. `loadDerivableSources` reads those files once per pass through the fabric file jail and reports escapes as skipped notes. No model call.

- Memory session consolidation, step 2 — the extractor (plan 188.3 Task 2): `extractConsolidationNotes({ client, candidates, segments, recallExisting, limits, redactor, secrets })` makes exactly one model call per pass through the shared worker loop with the single schema-declared tool `record_notes`, whose schema also drives parsing so an answer can only cite shown candidate ids (`c1`, `c2`, ...) and shown existing-note ids. It returns atomic typed notes (`fact`, `procedure`, `preference`, `episode`) with a scope, keys and aliases, evidence ids, generality and ephemerality, and contradictions; `agent` scope is downgraded to `project` and an episode is one per task segment at 280 characters or fewer. It fails closed on a malformed answer, an unknown kind or scope, an unshown evidence or `contradicts` id, too many notes, or an oversize item; an aborted call aborts the pass. The trust scan and secret redactor drop individual notes and count them. Input is bounded by `workerLimits` before it is sent, and the default package load resolves no model client.

- Memory session consolidation, step 1 — candidate gathering (plan 188.3 Task 1): `gatherConsolidationCandidates(entries, { cursor, maxCandidates, maxTextChars, patterns, redact })` is a pure, bounded function over `session.entries()` with no model or store call. It gathers un-superseded reflections, `om.task_state.recorded` decisions, `high`/`critical` observations, explicit user statements (matched sentence only), and corrections (carrying the corrected assistant entry and the note ids cited in that turn), and tags candidates whose evidence shares a run with a successful test/commit tool result or an approval grant. Order is priority (explicit, correction, decision, observation, reflection), bounded by `maxCandidates` (default 48) and `maxTextChars`, idempotent for the same cursor. `CONSOLIDATION_PATTERNS` exports the default English pattern sets; assistant text is never an explicit candidate and tool output is never candidate text.

- Memory scope hierarchy (plan 188.2 Task 5): isolation proof and examples. One parameterised suite (`__tests__/scope-isolation.ts`) runs over the in-memory, SQLite, and markdown stores and inside the Postgres integration suite: each fabric binds one project resource plus the shared `user`/`agent` resources, two projects hold the same `key` without crossing, the preference and agent note come back in both, a `path` note stays inside its project, and forgetting one project's fact leaves the other's. `examples/scoped-memory.ts` now shows the same over shared stores, and `examples/long-term-memory.ts` adds a third agent rooted in another project at the same markdown store (shared preference, no project fact). The memory bench resolves and passes one project scope per question with `user`/`agent` off (`scripts/memory-bench/bench-scope.mjs`), keeping LongMemEval and LoCoMo comparisons paired with the 188.1 configurations; a regression test asserts the resolved scopes.
- Memory scope hierarchy (plan 188.2 Task 4): recall now reads the union of the active scopes. Each scope is queried through its own `Memory` (consent, invalidation, and share grants stay per scope), the per-scope rankings are fused with RRF, and one additive prior is added (`scopes.priors`, defaults path 0.15, project 0.10, agent 0.05, user 0; each in `[0,0.5]`). A `path` note earns the path prior only when an `applies_to` glob matches a workspace-relative file the session touched through `read`/`edit`/`write`; the tracker keeps at most 256 paths newest-first in session state and writes them once per turn only when the set changes. A shared `key` keeps only the narrower scope's hit and records `shadowedBy` in `explain`. Recall and the `memory.recall` tool reserve one slot per scope with a hit before filling the token budget; injected and recalled lines name the scope; the memory directory renders per-scope counts and topics. `fuseRankings` is now used for cross-scope fusion, and the scoped read policy's floor reads the pre-prior similarity.
- Memory scope hierarchy (plan 188.2 Task 3): a new `preference` note kind (`preferences/` under the markdown layout) and per-note scope metadata. `MemoryFabric.remember` and `memory.insert` accept `scope` (`user | agent | project | path`), `appliesTo`, and `key`; the default scope comes from one table (`preference` → `user`, everything else → `project`) and `path` notes are stored in the project resource with 1–8 validated project-root-relative globs (no absolute path, `..`, or NUL). `key` (`[a-z0-9.-]{1,64}`) is the stable subject id for shadowing and widening. OKF files carry `prism.scope.level`, `applies_to:`, and `prism.key`. A write to a disabled scope fails with `scope <x> is not active`; consolidation only folds notes in the same scope; `memory.replace`, `memory.forget`, `fabric.verify`, and `fabric.forget` resolve an id across the active scopes (narrowest first) and cross-scope supersession is refused with `MemoryScopeError`. `memory.insert`'s description states the precision-first scope rule, and default recall now includes `preference`.
- Memory scope hierarchy (plan 188.2 Task 2): the fabric config's `scope: { tenantId, resourceId, threadId }` is replaced by `scopes: { tenantId, user, agent, project }` with no alias; the old key fails config validation and `DEFAULT_FABRIC_SCOPE`/`MemoryFabricScopeConfig` are removed. The project scope resolves a stable identity from the git remote (`origin` preferred, scheme/credentials/`.git` stripped, host lowercased), else the real path of the git top level, else the real root — file reads only, no `git` subprocess — and maps to `projects/<slug>--<id8>`.
